````rustix/AGENTS.md
     1	# Rustix agent instructions
     2	
     3	These instructions apply to every coding agent working in this repository. Read this file before changing code. For the architecture and detailed subsystem contracts, use the linked documents under `docs/`; this file summarizes the rules agents must preserve.
     4	
     5	## Project mission
     6	
     7	Rustix is an experimental 64-bit, Unix-like operating system with its own Rust kernel. Limine is the external boot protocol/loader only. Do not describe Limine, a host shell, or a userspace project as the Rustix kernel.
     8	
     9	The kernel is `no_std` and targets `x86_64-unknown-none`. Rust is the implementation language for kernel and OS components. The repository currently uses a Python standard-library script only to assemble and smoke-test the boot image; it does not replace kernel or OS functionality.
    10	
    11	Unix-like behavior is a long-term design goal, not a claim of POSIX compatibility. Make unsupported behavior explicit rather than approximating it silently.
    12	
    13	## Current project status — read before planning
    14	
    15	The source contains:
    16	
    17	- A Limine-based x86_64 kernel entry, linker layout, serial diagnostics, and boot-memory reporting.
    18	- A separate allocation-free `no_std` `crabshell` parser crate and a kernel-mode COM1 shell frontend.
    19	- A volatile, fixed-capacity RAM filesystem used by the shell.
    20	- A validated, fixed-capacity boot memory-map snapshot, CPUID/address-width reporting, and gated read-only EFER.NXE/IA32_PAT reporting.
    21	- A fixed-capacity physical frame allocator retained in `BootInfo`; Crabshell's `meminfo` command reports counts. No kernel service currently allocates frames for mappings or other services.
    22	- Pure 4 KiB mapping-range validation, leaf-PTE bit encoding, and PAT layout parsing in `src/paging.rs`; these helpers check alignment, address widths, canonical virtual ranges, W^X, NXE input, and PAT indices. Boot separately captures EFER.NXE and `IA32_PAT` read-only when supported. No code establishes frame ownership, mapping coverage, or effective MTRR cache semantics, or modifies page tables.
    23	- Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT setup and fatal exception handlers for vectors 0–31. Double faults use an IST stack and page faults report CR2. Opt-in QEMU tests inject a page fault and invalid opcode and verify diagnostics plus fatal halt; maskable IRQs remain disabled and exceptions are not recoverable.
    24	
    25	Important incomplete work:
    26	
    27	- Milestone 0's local exit criteria were met on 2026-10-16: `just verify` passed from both the working tree and a clean detached worktree in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04. The detached worktree had an empty `target` directory, so the run exercised fresh Limine download/host-tool compilation, formatting, 19 host tests, target check, optimized build, image creation, and the 20-second QEMU prompt smoke test. Hosted CI has not been observed.
    28	- A separate interactive `just run` session exercised directory navigation and create/write/append/read/copy/move/list/remove operations; `cp` replaced an existing destination, `rmdir` rejected a nonempty directory and succeeded after its child was removed, and unsupported `memtest` was rejected as expected. Boot snapshot tests and QEMU boot-metadata reporting were exercised. Structural checks still cannot prove that a Limine-provided non-null pointer is mapped or readable; that remains a loader trust boundary.
    29	- Milestone 0 is closed for local acceptance; hosted CI remains pending. Milestone 1's validation criteria are met for the current Limine path, subject to the documented pointer-readability trust boundary. Milestone 2 is **in progress**, not complete. The frame allocator specifies token ownership and preserves the token on failed deallocation; unique allocator-instance use is still caller-enforced. CPU address widths, physical map bounds, protocol-scoped HHDM range arithmetic, Limine executable image bases, page-aligned linker-defined kernel segment bounds, and gated read-only EFER.NXE/IA32_PAT reporting are checked; the segment bounds were reported in QEMU but do not prove inherited page-table coverage or permissions. The transition-critical mapping inventory is documented. `just fault-smoke` has validated controlled page-fault/CR2 and invalid-opcode diagnostics plus fatal halting in QEMU. Rustix still inherits Limine's page tables; their root and complete live mapping extents are not inspected, MTRR interactions are not validated, and there is no kernel-owned page-table manager, heap, or live frame-allocation caller.
    30	- There is no scheduler, userspace, syscall ABI, persistent filesystem, user/kernel isolation, or general IRQ subsystem.
    31	
    32	Never advance status or check off a roadmap item merely because source code or an interface exists. Update `docs/roadmap/README.md` only to reflect implementation and validation actually achieved.
    33	
    34	## Source-of-truth documents
    35	
    36	Consult the relevant documentation before modifying a subsystem:
    37	
    38	- [`docs/index.md`](docs/index.md) — documentation entry point and current status.
    39	- [`docs/roadmap/README.md`](docs/roadmap/README.md) — milestone sequencing and exit criteria.
    40	- [`docs/architecture/overview.md`](docs/architecture/overview.md) — current architecture and intended layers.
    41	- [`docs/architecture/boot.md`](docs/architecture/boot.md) — Limine requests, boot snapshot, and trust boundary.
    42	- [`docs/architecture/memory.md`](docs/architecture/memory.md) — memory ownership, frame allocator, and paging direction.
    43	- [`docs/architecture/crabshell.md`](docs/architecture/crabshell.md) — parser/kernel boundary and shell/VFS contract.
    44	- [`docs/architecture/drivers.md`](docs/architecture/drivers.md) — current driver scope and limitations.
    45	- [`docs/security.md`](docs/security.md) — unsafe boundaries and security policy.
    46	- [`docs/development/building.md`](docs/development/building.md) and [`docs/development/testing.md`](docs/development/testing.md) — supported commands and validation expectations.
    47	
    48	When code and documentation disagree, inspect the implementation, resolve the discrepancy deliberately, and update the relevant documentation in the same change.
    49	
    50	## Repository map
    51	
    52	- `src/main.rs` — freestanding entry, boot snapshot acquisition, panic path, and shell handoff.
    53	- `src/boot.rs` — mutable Limine requests, structural response/range validation, owned `BootInfo`, and boot diagnostics.
    54	- `src/memory.rs` — allocation-free memory-map, address-width, canonical-address, Limine HHDM-range, and linker-defined kernel segment validation.
    55	- `src/frame.rs` — bounded physical-frame allocator core; it only uses validated `Usable` ranges, reserves the first MiB, and has fixed range metadata.
    56	- `src/paging.rs` — pure checked range and 4 KiB leaf-PTE bit construction for future mappings; no table access or physical-memory dereference.
    57	- `src/shell.rs` — serial line editor and kernel-side Crabshell executor.
    58	- `src/vfs.rs` — fixed-capacity volatile RAM filesystem and host-side tests.
    59	- `crates/crabshell/` — separate `no_std` parser crate. It has no terminal, filesystem, or kernel dependencies.
    60	- `src/arch/x86_64/serial.rs` — polling COM1 driver for shell input/output.
    61	- `src/arch/x86_64/interrupts.rs` — bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT, assembly stubs, and fatal exception diagnostics.
    62	- `src/arch/x86_64/cpu.rs` — CPUID identification and selected feature reporting.
    63	- `src/arch/x86_64/keyboard.rs` — raw, non-blocking PS/2-compatible scancode polling; not an input subsystem.
    64	- `src/arch/x86_64/io.rs` — narrow port-I/O wrappers.
    65	- `linker.ld`, `.cargo/config.toml`, `rust-toolchain.toml` — target, linker, and toolchain configuration.
    66	- `limine.conf`, `scripts/boot.py` — boot configuration and pinned image/emulator workflow.
    67	- `justfile` — supported development commands.
    68	- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — CI validation workflow.
    69	
    70	## Engineering rules
    71	
    72	### Keep changes incremental and coherent
    73	
    74	1. Inspect the relevant source, tests, docs, and call sites before editing.
    75	2. State assumptions, invariants, and failure behavior for new kernel interfaces.
    76	3. Prefer small vertical slices that compile and can be tested over broad scaffolding or placeholder APIs.
    77	4. Update tests and documentation alongside behavior changes.
    78	5. Do not change unrelated code, rename files gratuitously, add speculative features, or rewrite project history.
    79	6. Do not commit or create branches unless the user explicitly asks.
    80	
    81	### Rust and dependency policy
    82	
    83	- Preserve the kernel's `no_std`/freestanding constraints. Do not introduce `std` into kernel code.
    84	- Prefer `core` and existing in-tree abstractions. Do not add third-party Rust dependencies without a concrete need and compatibility/security review.
    85	- Keep the Crabshell parser independent of kernel I/O and VFS implementation. Kernel execution belongs in `src/shell.rs` or an appropriate kernel service.
    86	- Do not add allocation-dependent APIs before a real heap exists. Fixed-capacity structures must document their limits and exhaustion behavior.
    87	- Respect the workspace structure and the host-testable `rustix-kernel` library versus freestanding `rustix` binary boundary.
    88	
    89	### Unsafe and hardware code
    90	
    91	Unsafe code is expected only at explicit hardware, boot-protocol, assembly, or raw-memory boundaries. Keep each unsafe block narrow and document the invariant that makes it sound. Do not add broad `unsafe impl`, mutable global references, unchecked pointer arithmetic, or unreviewed inline/global assembly.
    92	
    93	Before changing boot or memory code, account for these facts:
    94	
    95	- Limine fills response pointers before `_start`; the request records must remain writable in the ELF image.
    96	- Rustix validates nullness, alignment, counts, arithmetic, and physical ranges, but cannot prove that a non-null bootloader pointer is readable.
    97	- Physical ranges are half-open `[start, end)` and must use checked arithmetic.
    98	- Only regions classified `Usable` may enter the frame allocator. ACPI, bootloader-reclaimable, framebuffer, reserved, unknown, and other types are not free frames.
    99	- A `Frame` is a physical address, not a dereferenceable pointer. Do not access it through the HHDM or change CR3 until an explicit, reviewed mapping policy and ownership proof exist.
   100	- The kernel currently inherits Limine's page tables. Do not casually replace or mutate them: preserve the executing kernel, boot stack, `BootInfo`, request/response data, exception stacks, and mappings needed to continue execution.
   101	- The exception setup is fatal-only. It does not enable maskable interrupts, configure a PIC/APIC, or provide recovery. Do not call `sti` or claim interrupt support without an IRQ design and tests.
   102	- The shell and VFS run in ring 0 and are not isolated or secure.
   103	
   104	For hardware changes, document assumptions about CPU mode, selectors, stacks, page permissions, interrupt state, and failure handling. Avoid new architecture mechanisms that cannot be statically reviewed or validated.
   105	
   106	### Shell and filesystem contract
   107	
   108	- Crabshell is presently a kernel-mode serial shell, not a userspace program or a POSIX shell.
   109	- Its parser is a separate `no_std`, allocation-free crate; retain that boundary.
   110	- The RAM filesystem is volatile and bounded. Preserve explicit errors and test capacity, path, and mutation edge cases.
   111	- Do not imply persistence, permissions, links, process launching, or shell features that do not exist.
   112	- Update the parser tests and `docs/architecture/crabshell.md` when adding or changing commands.
   113	
   114	## Validation workflow
   115	
   116	Run the narrowest relevant checks first, then broaden them. From the repository root, the supported commands are:
   117	
   118	```sh
   119	just fmt
   120	just test
   121	just check
   122	just build
   123	just image
   124	just smoke
   125	just verify
   126	```
   127	
   128	`just test` runs host-side library tests (memory-map validation, frame allocator, VFS, and Crabshell parser). `just check` checks `x86_64-unknown-none`; `just build` links the optimized ELF. `just verify` includes formatting, host tests, target check, pinned Limine image creation, and QEMU prompt smoke test. Image/boot checks need Python, `xorriso`, QEMU, the Rust target, and network access for the first pinned Limine download; Linux/WSL also needs `cc` to build Limine's host utility.
   129	
   130	For changes to `src/main.rs`, `src/boot.rs`, linker/request configuration, `src/arch/x86_64/`, `src/frame.rs`, or paging/heap code, the intended full validation is `just verify`. If the environment does not permit a check, report the exact blocker and leave the corresponding roadmap criterion pending.
   131	
   132	Do not claim a check passed unless it was run and its successful result was observed. Editor diagnostics are useful but are not a substitute for `cargo test`, a target build, or QEMU. A target check is not a final link; a linked ELF is not proof of boot; a prompt smoke test is not proof of interactive shell behavior or physical-hardware correctness.
   133	
   134	## Completion criteria for agent changes
   135	
   136	Before reporting work complete:
   137	
   138	- Ensure source, tests, docs, and call sites agree.
   139	- Review unsafe and hardware assumptions, including error paths and capacity limits.
   140	- Run appropriate formatting/tests/build/boot checks, or clearly state why they could not run.
   141	- Report files changed, behavior delivered, validation actually observed, and remaining risks.
   142	- Keep milestone status accurate. A subsystem is not complete just because it has code.

  thinking [L4003-4004]
# Nuke built-in rules. [L4012-4015]
# Delete the target of a failed recipe. [L4015-4018]
# Target architecture to build for. Default to x86_64. [L4018-4021]
# Default user QEMU flags. These are appended to the QEMU command calls. [L4021-4024]
# Internal architecture specific variables that should not be changed by the [L4024-4025]
# user. x86_64 images get an MBR, the most portable form for BIOS and UEFI. [L4025-4061]
# User controllable size of the HDD image, in MiB. [L4061-4064]
# Internal HDD geometry that should not be changed by the user. Older mtools [L4064-4065]
# require one; 64 heads of 32 sectors make a cylinder exactly 1 MiB in size. [L4065-4070]
# Internal HDD partition layout that should not be changed by the user. sgdisk [L4070-4071]
# lays the partition out as GPT, even where it then converts the table to MBR, [L4071-4072]
# so the first and last cylinders are left to the GPT structures. [L4072-4078]
# Toolchain for building the 'limine' executable for the host. [L4078-4136]
# Date the source by the tarball, not the release inside it, so that what [L4136-4137]
# is made from it is remade whenever it is extracted. Not by the clock, as [L4137-4138]
# some touch use a finer one than file writes, dating it after what follows. [L4138-4144]
# The utility depends on records of the variables it is built with, named [L4144-4145]
# after them, which are compared here and remade when they differ. They are [L4145-4146]
# made after extracting, which removes them. [L4146-4155]
# Remove the utility first, as its makefile does not track the toolchain. [L4155-4228]

The build uses no third-party Rust crates; the boot protocol is represented by small in-tree Rust structures and constants.
 78633	+`.cargo/config.toml` applies the Rustix linker script and x86_64 kernel constraints only to `x86_64-unknown-none`. `linker.ld` emits an ELF64 x86_64 image at the higher-half base, retains Limine request sections in a writable load segment for bootloader response pointers, defines `_start` as an assembly entry shim, and exports the load-image boundary symbols consumed by `BootInfo`. The shim captures the initial stack pointer before entering Rust. The build uses no third-party Rust crates; the boot protocol is represented by small in-tree Rust structures and constants.

 78640	
 78641	+## Debugging the kernel from Zed
 78642	+
 78643	+On the Windows Zed workspace with the repository built through WSL2, run the `Rustix: QEMU Debug Server` task from the task picker. It builds the release ELF with full DWARF debug information, creates the ISO, and starts QEMU paused with its GDB stub bound only to `127.0.0.1:1234`. Then open Zed's debugger (`F4`) and start `Rustix: QEMU (CodeLLDB)`. The configuration loads the ELF from the Windows worktree, maps WSL compiler paths back to the workspace, and sets a breakpoint at `rustix_start`; continue once from the initial reset stop to reach the Rust entry function. Stop QEMU with `Ctrl+C` in its task terminal.
 78644	+
 78645	+The debug build uses a per-command Cargo profile override and does not change the normal release profile. It writes to the usual release ELF and ISO paths, so a later `just build`, `just image`, or `just run` rebuilds the normal image. CodeLLDB remote debugging must be available in Zed. The GDB stub listens on loopback only; do not change this to a public interface.
 78646	+
 78647	 `just verify` includes the emulator smoke test and therefore needs Python 3, xorriso, QEMU, network access for the first Limine download, and the Rust target. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` installs the system tools on Ubuntu 24.04 and runs the same command. This verifies the boot path in CI; it is still not a substitute for testing physical hardware.

 38080	     1	# Rustix roadmap
 38081	     2	
 38082	     3	This roadmap describes an incremental path from the current bare-metal kernel to a useful Unix-like operating system. It is a dependency plan, not a schedule or a claim of compatibility. Features become current only after the implementation, tests, and exit criteria below agree.
 38083	     4	
 38084	     5	## How to read status
 38085	     6	
 38086	     7	- A checked box means the source/design work exists; it does **not** imply the code compiled, linked, booted, or passed an integration test.
 38087	     8	- A milestone is not complete until its stated exit criteria are met and the evidence is recorded.
 38088	     9	- Hardware work needs target build and emulator evidence. Host tests cannot validate privileged instructions, bootloader pointers, page tables, device I/O, or exception-entry assembly.
 38089	    10	- Future work is intentionally not represented by placeholder APIs or dummy implementations.
 38090	    11	
 38091	    12	## Current status
 38092	    13	
 38093	    14	- **Milestone 0: implementation present; end-to-end ISO build and QEMU boot validation pending.** The latest source must be built and booted before this milestone exits.
 38094	    15	- **Milestone 1: source implementation underway, not build-/boot-verified.** The kernel has a bounded boot snapshot, memory-range classification, and CPUID reporting. The loader readability trust boundary remains.
 38095	    16	- **Milestone 2: in progress, not complete.** A bounded frame allocator is retained in `BootInfo`, and bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT fatal exception handlers exist. The kernel still inherits Limine's page tables; there is no owned page-table manager, global heap, live frame-allocation caller, or fault-injection/emulator validation.
 38096	    17	- **Milestones 3 and later: planned.** There is no general IRQ system, scheduler, userspace, syscall ABI, persistent filesystem, GUI, or networking stack.
 38097	    18	
 38098	    19	## Dependency path
 38099	    20	
 38100	    21	```text
 38101	    22	Reliable boot
 38102	    23	    -> validated boot state
 38103	    24	    -> owned mappings + frame allocation + heap + fault diagnostics
 38104	    25	    -> timer/IRQ model + scheduler
 38105	    26	    -> isolated processes + syscall ABI
 38106	    27	    -> VFS/storage + userland utilities
 38107	    28	    -> graphical terminal and other optional services
 38108	    29	```
 38109	    30	
 38110	    31	Some device-driver work can proceed in parallel after the core ownership, locking, and testing rules exist. It must not bypass them with direct, unreviewed hardware access.

 38204	   114	
 38205	   115	Do not enable interrupts as a side effect of installing exception gates. IRQ delivery needs its own controller, acknowledgement, masking, ownership, and synchronization design.
 38206	   116	
 38207	   117	### Planned work
 38208	   118	
 38209	   119	- [ ] Select and document the initial interrupt controller policy for QEMU and target hardware (for example, legacy PIC compatibility versus local I/O APIC; do not enable both accidentally).
 38210	   120	- [ ] Add IRQ vector allocation, controller masking/unmasking, end-of-interrupt acknowledgement, spurious-interrupt handling, and a policy for unexpected IRQs.
 38211	   121	- [ ] Add a calibrated/verified timer source and monotonic timekeeping; test interval arithmetic and drift assumptions.
 38212	   122	- [ ] Establish interrupt-safe lock primitives and rules for code callable from normal, exception, and IRQ contexts. No allocator or blocking console operation may run in an unsafe interrupt context.
 38213	   123	- [ ] Add a minimal kernel-thread context and lifecycle model, stack ownership/guard policy, context-switch tests, and emulator scheduling tests.
 38214	   124	- [ ] Add preemption only after lock, stack, timer, and interrupt-return invariants have deterministic tests.
 38215	   125	- [ ] Keep application-processor startup/SMP as a separate sub-milestone after per-CPU state, TLB invalidation, and lock semantics exist.
 38216	   126

 38221	   131	## Milestone 4 — isolated processes and syscall ABI
 38222	   132	
 38223	   133	Userspace begins only after page tables, exception handling, scheduling, and controlled memory allocation are trustworthy. The syscall ABI is a product interface and must not emerge accidentally from internal functions.
 38224	   134	
 38225	   135	### Planned work
 38226	   136	
 38227	   137	- [ ] Decide and document the x86_64 user-entry/syscall mechanism, register convention, selector/MSR setup, per-CPU state requirements, and safe return path.
 38228	   138	- [ ] Add user/kernel page permissions and process-owned address spaces, including teardown and TLB invalidation rules.
 38229	   139	- [ ] Implement copy-in/copy-out helpers that validate lengths, arithmetic, canonical ranges, permissions, and faults; never form a kernel reference directly from a user pointer.
 38230	   140	- [ ] Specify version discovery, stable syscall numbers, argument layouts, error encoding, partial-I/O behavior, interruption/restart, and compatibility policy before exposing calls.
 38231	   141	- [ ] Add initial process lifecycle and descriptor-table semantics: create/load, exit, wait, close, duplication/inheritance, and process cleanup.
 38232	   142	- [ ] Load a deliberately small documented executable format with bounds/segment/permission checks; reject malformed binaries safely.
 38233	   143	- [ ] Build adversarial tests for bad pointers, overflow, invalid mappings, process teardown, descriptor misuse, and failed exec.
 38234	   144	
 38235	   145	### First user-visible target
 38236	   146	
 38237	   147	A small controlled `init` process should start from a reproducible image, write through a descriptor, exit with a status, and be waited on by a kernel test harness. Do not claim userspace or process isolation from ring-0 tasks that merely use process-shaped structs.
 38238	   148	
 38239	   149	**Exit criteria:** an isolated init process runs in user mode and uses a versioned, documented syscall subset; invalid user pointers and process teardown are tested without corrupting the kernel.

 38310	   220	
 38311	   221	## Cross-cutting acceptance gates
 38312	   222	
 38313	   223	Every milestone should include the relevant subset of these gates:
 38314	   224	
 38315	   225	1. **Design:** documented ownership, invariants, resource limits, error semantics, and unsupported cases.
 38316	   226	2. **Tests:** host tests for pure policy and malformed/boundary input; integration tests for hardware-facing behavior.
 38317	   227	3. **Safety:** review unsafe blocks, pointer provenance/access, integer overflow, privilege boundaries, concurrency, and cleanup/rollback paths.
 38318	   228	4. **Build:** format, host tests, target check, and final ELF link as appropriate.
 38319	   229	5. **Boot/integration:** QEMU tests for changes to boot, paging, descriptors, interrupts, drivers, processes, or persistence.
 38320	   230	6. **Documentation/status:** update architecture and roadmap docs; never check off a criterion without evidence.
 38321	   231	7. **Compatibility:** claim Unix/POSIX behavior only for the exact documented subset with conformance-style tests.
 38322	   232	
 38323	   233	## Working rule
 38324	   234	
 38325	   235	Prefer a small end-to-end vertical slice over broad, unbootable scaffolding. A source file, interface, or architecture document is not proof that the corresponding subsystem is operational. When a check cannot run, record the exact environment blocker and leave that acceptance gate pending.

 38333	````rustix/docs/architecture/overview.md
 38334	     1	# Architecture overview
 38335	     2	
 38336	     3	## Goals
 38337	     4	
 38338	     5	Rustix is a from-scratch operating system project with its own kernel, written in Rust. The intended initial platform is 64-bit x86 (`x86_64`), with a Unix-like process and file-descriptor model. The kernel should eventually own machine initialization, physical and virtual memory management, interrupt handling, scheduling, system calls, and device policy. A bootloader is a transport into the kernel, not a replacement for those facilities.
 38339	     6	
 38340	     7	Unix-like is a compatibility direction. It does **not** mean the current kernel is POSIX-compliant, nor does it commit the project to a particular existing kernel's internals. Compatibility profiles and a userland should be defined only after the underlying process, descriptor, and syscall semantics are implemented and tested.
 38341	     8	
 38342	     9	## Current implementation
 38343	    10	
 38344	    11	1. Cargo builds a `no_std`, `no_main` ELF for `x86_64-unknown-none` and uses Limine for the boot handoff.
 38345	    12	2. `_start` initializes COM1 and exception diagnostics, captures a validated boot snapshot and frame-allocator state, reports memory/CPU information, initializes a fixed-capacity RAM filesystem, and enters Crabshell.
 38346	    13	3. Crabshell reads an ASCII command line from the serial UART and supports basic navigation, directory creation/listing, and text-file operations.
 38347	    14	4. The separately packaged `crabshell` `no_std` crate parses commands without allocation or dependence on kernel I/O, making that layer reusable by a future graphical frontend.
 38348	    15	5. The kernel library contains checked memory accounting and the bounded VFS implementation, both covered by host-side unit tests.
 38349	    16	
 38350	    17	The current shell and filesystem run in kernel mode. Files exist only in RAM and disappear at reboot. There is no heap, Rustix-owned page-table manager, interrupt descriptor table, scheduler, user/kernel isolation, syscall ABI, on-disk filesystem, network stack, or userspace process.
 38351	    18	
 38352	    19	## Intended layers (planned)
 38353	    20	
 38354	    21	```text
 38355	    22	User programs and system utilities
 38356	    23	        | versioned syscall ABI
 38357	    24	Kernel services: processes, descriptors, VFS, IPC, signals
 38358	    25	        | explicit ownership and authorization checks
 38359	    26	Core kernel: scheduler, virtual memory, physical frames, interrupts
 38360	    27	        | narrow architecture abstractions
 38361	    28	x86_64 platform: paging, exceptions, timers, AP startup, drivers
 38362	    29	        | Limine boot handoff only
 38363	    30	Firmware and hardware
 38364	    31	```
 38365	    32	
 38366	    33	The diagram is a target decomposition, not a claim that those layers are implemented. Architecture-independent policy should live in safe Rust where possible; privileged instructions and raw device access should stay in small, reviewed architecture modules.
 38367	    34	
 38368	    35	## Design constraints
 38369	    36	
 38370	    37	- **Freestanding:** kernel code does not link `std`; allocation must be introduced deliberately after a physical-frame and heap strategy exists.
 38371	    38	- **Explicit unsafe boundaries:** raw pointers from boot protocol responses and x86 port I/O are unsafe. Each use must document its preconditions and remain short-lived.
 38372	    39	- **Fail visibly:** early boot failures should produce serial diagnostics where possible and then halt, rather than continue with invalid machine state.
 38373	    40	- **Checked arithmetic:** physical ranges, sizes, and address translations must reject overflow.
 38374	    41	- **No premature ABI:** userspace interfaces stay unstable until process lifetime, error conventions, copy-in/copy-out, and versioning are designed together.
 38375	    42	- **Hardware-independent tests:** parsing, range arithmetic, and policy should be testable as ordinary host-side Rust wherever possible.
 38376	    43	
 38377	    44	## Boot and CPU assumptions
 38378	    45	
 38379	    46	The current target is x86_64, with a 4 KiB page-oriented linker layout and no red zone. Limine performs the initial platform setup required by its protocol. Rustix must not assume that receiving a memory-map response means it owns the loader's page tables or can overwrite reserved regions. The current bootstrap does not enable interrupts or start application processors.
 38380	    47	
 38381	    48	See [Boot protocol](boot.md), [Memory management](memory.md), [x86_64 drivers](drivers.md), [Crabshell](crabshell.md), and [the roadmap](../roadmap/README.md) for subsystem boundaries and planned acceptance criteria.
 38382	
 38383	````
 38384

 38389	```rustix/AGENTS.md
 38390	     1	# Rustix agent instructions
 38391	     2	
 38392	     3	These instructions apply to every coding agent working in this repository. Read this file before changing code. For the architecture and detailed subsystem contracts, use the linked documents under `docs/`; this file summarizes the rules agents must preserve.
 38393	     4	
 38394	     5	## Project mission
 38395	     6	
 38396	     7	Rustix is an experimental 64-bit, Unix-like operating system with its own Rust kernel. Limine is the external boot protocol/loader only. Do not describe Limine, a host shell, or a userspace project as the Rustix kernel.
 38397	     8	
 38398	     9	The kernel is `no_std` and targets `x86_64-unknown-none`. Rust is the implementation language for kernel and OS components. The repository currently uses a Python standard-library script only to assemble and smoke-test the boot image; it does not replace kernel or OS functionality.
 38399	    10	
 38400	    11	Unix-like behavior is a long-term design goal, not a claim of POSIX compatibility. Make unsupported behavior explicit rather than approximating it silently.
 38401	    12	
 38402	    13	## Current project status — read before planning
 38403	    14	
 38404	    15	The source contains:
 38405	    16	
 38406	    17	- A Limine-based x86_64 kernel entry, linker layout, serial diagnostics, and boot-memory reporting.
 38407	    18	- A separate allocation-free `no_std` `crabshell` parser crate and a kernel-mode COM1 shell frontend.
 38408	    19	- A volatile, fixed-capacity RAM filesystem used by the shell.
 38409	    20	- A validated, fixed-capacity boot memory-map snapshot and baseline CPUID reporting.
 38410	    21	- A fixed-capacity physical frame allocator retained in `BootInfo`; Crabshell's `meminfo` command reports counts. No kernel service currently allocates frames for mappings or other services.
 38411	    22	- Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT setup and fatal exception handlers for vectors 0–31. Double faults use an IST stack and page faults report CR2. Maskable IRQs remain disabled; exception recovery and fault-injection tests are not implemented.
 38412	    23	
 38413	    24	Important incomplete work:
 38414	    25	
 38415	    26	- The roadmap still has Milestone 0's end-to-end image build and QEMU boot validation pending. The latest source has not been established as successfully built or booted.
 38416	    27	- Milestone 1 source exists but is not build-/boot-verified. Structural pointer checks do not prove that a Limine-provided non-null pointer is mapped or readable; that remains a loader trust boundary.
 38417	    28	- Milestone 2 is **in progress**, not complete. Rustix still inherits Limine's page tables; it has no kernel-owned page-table manager, no heap, and no live frame allocation callers. GDT/TSS/IDT assembly and exception paths have not had fault-injection or emulator validation.
 38418	    29	- There is no scheduler, userspace, syscall ABI, persistent filesystem, user/kernel isolation, or general IRQ subsystem.
 38419	    30	
 38420	    31	Never advance status or check off a roadmap item merely because source code or an interface exists. Update `docs/roadmap/README.md` only to reflect implementation and validation actually achieved.

 38458	`src/frame.rs` provides a fixed-capacity allocator core with host-side tests. It only manages complete 4 KiB frames inside validated `Usable` regions, reserves the first MiB, and rejects invalid/double frees while merging adjacent ranges. Its bounded range metadata can report fragmentation exhaustion. The kernel initializes it from the validated map, retains it in `BootInfo`, and exposes counts through Crabshell's `meminfo` command. It does not yet allocate frames for kernel services or map/dereference any allocated frame. Rustix does not reclaim bootloader memory, own or replace the inherited Limine page tables, or provide a heap. The HHDM offset is checked and logged, not used for memory access. Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT setup and fatal exception diagnostics live in `src/arch/x86_64/interrupts.rs`; they do not enable maskable IRQs.

 38482	The repository contains a bare-metal `no_std` Rust kernel for `x86_64-unknown-none`. After Limine handoff it validates and copies memory-map metadata into an owned snapshot, reports baseline CPUID features, seeds a volatile RAM filesystem, and runs Crabshell over the COM1 serial console. Crabshell's allocation-free parser is a separate crate; the kernel handles command execution. The image builder and bounded QEMU prompt smoke test are wired into CI, but the latest source changes have not yet been built or boot-tested and Milestone 0's end-to-end run remains pending. This is a kernel-mode shell, not a user process. A bounded physical-frame allocator core is initialized from the validated map and reported by `meminfo`, but no kernel service allocates or maps frames yet; there is not yet page-table management, a heap, a general IRQ subsystem, a scheduler, process isolation, a persistent filesystem, or a Unix compatibility layer. Fatal CPU exceptions have basic GDT/TSS/IDT diagnostics but no recovery or fault-injection tests yet.

 38541	# Architecture overview
 38542	
 38543	## Goals
 38544	
 38545	Rustix is a from-scratch operating system project with its own kernel, written in Rust. The intended initial platform is 64-bit x86 (`x86_64`), with a Unix-like process and file-descriptor model. The kernel should eventually own machine initialization, physical and virtual memory management, interrupt handling, scheduling, system calls, and device policy. A bootloader is a transport into the kernel, not a replacement for those facilities.
 38546	
 38547	Unix-like is a compatibility direction. It does **not** mean the current kernel is POSIX-compliant, nor does it commit the project to a particular existing kernel's internals. Compatibility profiles and a userland should be defined only after the underlying process, descriptor, and syscall semantics are implemented and tested.
 38548	
 38549	## Current implementation
 38550	
 38551	1. Cargo builds a `no_std`, `no_main` ELF for `x86_64-unknown-none` and uses Limine for the boot handoff.
 38552	2. `_start` initializes COM1 and exception diagnostics, captures a validated boot snapshot and frame-allocator state, reports memory/CPU information, initializes a fixed-capacity RAM filesystem, and enters Crabshell.
 38553	3. Crabshell reads an ASCII command line from the serial UART and supports basic navigation, directory creation/listing, and text-file operations.
 38554	4. The separately packaged `crabshell` `no_std` crate parses commands without allocation or dependence on kernel I/O, making that layer reusable by a future graphical frontend.
 38555	5. The kernel library contains checked memory accounting and the bounded VFS implementation, both covered by host-side unit tests.
 38556	
 38557	The current shell and filesystem run in kernel mode. Files exist only in RAM and disappear at reboot. Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT setup and fatal exception handlers for vectors 0–31 exist, but maskable IRQs remain disabled and exception recovery is not implemented. There is no heap, Rustix-owned page-table manager, general IRQ subsystem, scheduler, user/kernel isolation, syscall ABI, on-disk filesystem, network stack, or userspace process.
 38558	
 38559	## Intended layers (planned)
 38560	
 38561	```text
 38562	User programs and system utilities
 38563	        | versioned syscall ABI
 38564	Kernel services: processes, descriptors, VFS, IPC, signals
 38565	        | explicit ownership and authorization checks
 38566	Core kernel: scheduler, virtual memory, physical frames, interrupts
 38567	        | narrow architecture abstractions
 38568	x86_64 platform: paging, exceptions, timers, AP startup, drivers
 38569	        | Limine boot handoff only
 38570	Firmware and hardware
 38571	```
 38572	
 38573	The diagram is a target decomposition, not a claim that those layers are implemented. Architecture-independent policy should live in safe Rust where possible; privileged instructions and raw device access should stay in small, reviewed architecture modules.
 38574	
 38575	## Design constraints
 38576	
 38577	- **Freestanding:** kernel code does not link `std`; allocation must be introduced deliberately after a physical-frame and heap strategy exists.
 38578	- **Explicit unsafe boundaries:** raw pointers from boot protocol responses and x86 port I/O are unsafe. Each use must document its preconditions and remain short-lived.
 38579	- **Fail visibly:** early boot failures should produce serial diagnostics where possible and then halt, rather than continue with invalid machine state.
 38580	- **Checked arithmetic:** physical ranges, sizes, and address translations must reject overflow.
 38581	- **No premature ABI:** userspace interfaces stay unstable until process lifetime, error conventions, copy-in/copy-out, and versioning are designed together.
 38582	- **Hardware-independent tests:** parsing, range arithmetic, and policy should be testable as ordinary host-side Rust wherever possible.
 38583	
 38584	## Boot and CPU assumptions
 38585	
 38586	The current target is x86_64, with a 4 KiB page-oriented linker layout and no red zone. Limine performs the initial platform setup required by its protocol. Rustix must not assume that receiving a memory-map response means it owns the loader's page tables or can overwrite reserved regions. The current bootstrap does not enable interrupts or start application processors.
 38587	
 38588	See [Boot protocol](boot.md), [Memory management](memory.md), [x86_64 drivers](drivers.md), [Crabshell](crabshell.md), and [the roadmap](../roadmap/README.md) for subsystem boundaries and planned acceptance criteria.
 38589	
 38590	```

   681	     4	
   682	     5	- Rust stable with `rustfmt`; the repository pins these in `rust-toolchain.toml`.
   683	     6	- The `x86_64-unknown-none` target. The toolchain file requests it automatically; if needed, install it with `rustup target add x86_64-unknown-none`.
   684	     7	- [`just`](https://just.systems/) to run the repository's named tasks.
   685	     8	- Python 3.8 or newer (standard library only), with `python` on `PATH`; `xorriso`; and QEMU with `qemu-system-x86_64` for image creation and boot smoke tests.
   686	     9	
   687	    10	An ELF inspection tool such as `llvm-readelf` or `readelf` is useful when diagnosing linker output. The first image build downloads the pinned Limine v12.9.3 binary archive into `target/tools/` and verifies its published SHA-256 before extraction.
   688	    11	
   689	    12	## Common commands
   690	    13

   694	    17	just help

```rustix/docs/architecture/memory.md
     1	# Memory-management direction
     2	
     3	## What exists now
     4	
     5	`src/memory.rs` contains an allocation-free `MemoryMapBuilder` that copies Limine ranges into validated half-open physical regions. It rejects empty ranges, `base + length` overflow, overlapping ranges, empty maps, and maps exceeding the fixed 256-region capacity. Known Limine types remain distinct, and unknown type values are preserved. Host-side tests cover range validation/classification, canonical-address boundaries, physical-width bounds, and HHDM range arithmetic. `src/boot.rs` stores the resulting map in an owned boot snapshot and reports usable bytes.
     6	
     7	`src/frame.rs` provides a fixed-capacity allocator core with host-side tests. It only manages complete 4 KiB frames inside validated `Usable` regions, reserves the first MiB, and coalesces adjacent free ranges. The ownership contract assumes one allocator instance per physical map: within that instance, each eligible frame is owned either by its free-range table or by a non-copyable `Frame` token while allocated. The public constructor does not enforce unique instances, so callers must not initialize multiple allocators for the same physical map; the current boot path retains one allocator in `BootInfo`. `allocate` returns `None` when no free frame remains. `deallocate` consumes the token only on success; on any error, including free-range-table exhaustion, it returns a `DeallocationError` containing the original token and leaves allocator state unchanged. The range tables each have a fixed `MAX_FRAME_RANGES` capacity, so fragmentation can prevent a frame from being returned until nearby ranges coalesce. No kernel service currently allocates frames for mappings or other work.
     8	
     9	`Frame::address()` is a physical address value, not a dereferenceable pointer and not a proof that the frame is mapped. CPU diagnostics now read `CR4.LA57` to report the active linear-address width (48 or 57 bits) and query `CPUID.80000008H` for physical and maximum linear-address widths when available. Boot-protocol pointers are checked against the active width, with host tests for 48- and 57-bit canonical boundaries. These facts do not prove that any physical address is mapped.
    10	
    11	`src/paging.rs` adds pure, host-tested 4 KiB mapping groundwork. Its range validator requires a known physical-address width, checked aligned bounds, canonical virtual endpoints in one canonical half, and rejects writable-executable permissions. A validated range can encode the raw bits of one 4 KiB leaf PTE, including supervisor/user and write permissions, NX when the caller confirms EFER.NXE, and a bounded three-bit PAT index. `PatLayout` parses a supplied `IA32_PAT` value and selects WB, UC, or WC indices. `src/arch/x86_64/cpu.rs` now captures EFER.NXE only when NX is supported and reads `IA32_PAT` only when PAT is supported; these reads are diagnostic and do not modify CPU state. The kernel does not yet verify MTRR interactions or use the resulting cache indices in mappings. None of these helpers proves frame ownership or HHDM coverage, restricts memory types, or touches hardware page tables. No page mapping operation or CR3 transition is implemented.
    12	
    13	The requested Limine base revision is 3. Its protocol maps only `Usable`, `BootloaderReclaimable`, `ExecutableAndModules`, and `Framebuffer` memory-map regions into the HHDM; reserved, ACPI, bad, and unknown regions are not in that mapping set. HHDM mappings use at least 4 KiB granularity and may cover page-aligned bytes beyond unaligned region boundaries. On x86_64, inherited HHDM mappings are supervisor RWX, normally write-back cached, with framebuffer mappings write-combining when PAT is available. Therefore an HHDM offset is not a blanket physical-memory access grant, and inherited HHDM permissions are not Rustix's future W^X policy. See the [Limine base revision 3 protocol](https://github.com/limine-bootloader/limine-protocol/blob/trunk/PROTOCOL.md#base-revision-3) and its memory-layout/caching sections.
    14	
    15	The boot snapshot now rejects physical map ranges beyond the reported CPUID physical-address width when that CPUID leaf is available. For base revision 3 HHDM-mapped types, it also validates the page-rounded physical span, checked `offset + address` arithmetic, and canonical endpoints in the active linear-address width. If the CPU does not report a physical width, the physical-bound check is skipped and no mapping caller may treat the bound as known. These checks are derived from metadata and Limine's protocol contract; they do not inspect page tables or prove that a non-null loader pointer is readable. Rustix still does not dereference physical memory through the HHDM. Rustix-owned mappings should be non-executable by default; executable kernel text must be explicit. MMIO requires an explicit device range and cache policy, and guard regions must remain unmapped. A page-table transition must preserve the kernel image, active stacks, boot data, descriptor tables, and any loader mappings still needed. Rustix does not reclaim bootloader memory, own or replace the inherited Limine page tables, or provide a heap. Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT setup and fatal exception diagnostics live in `src/arch/x86_64/interrupts.rs`; they do not enable maskable IRQs.
    16	
    17	## Mapping inventory before a CR3 transition
    18	
    19	The current runtime has several mappings that a future page-table transition must preserve. The address sources and remaining gaps are:
    20	
    21	- **Kernel ELF image:** `linker.ld` places `.text`, `.rodata`, `.limine_requests`, `.data`, `.bss`, and `.got` in three `PT_LOAD` segments: RX text, read-only rodata, and RW data/BSS. It exports `__kernel_start` and `__kernel_end` around the complete loadable image, plus page-aligned bounds for each segment. `BootInfo` checks that Limine's reported virtual base matches the linker start, validates ordered contiguous segment bounds covering the rounded image, derives corresponding half-open physical ranges from the executable physical base with checked arithmetic, and validates canonical virtual and reported physical address widths. The release ELF and QEMU runtime report both showed the three aligned ranges on 2026-10-21. The RX, R/NX, and RW/NX labels describe the linker segment intent; these are linker/boot metadata, not proof that the inherited page tables map every page or enforce those permissions, nor proof that loader pointers are readable. The memory-map `ExecutableAndModules` entries remain illustrative, not an authoritative kernel-image extent.
    22	- **Active stack and `BootInfo`:** The assembly `_start` shim captures Limine's entry `RSP` before any Rust prologue. Under the SysV entry convention the synthetic return slot occupies 8 bytes, so `RSP + 8` is the exclusive stack top. The protocol guarantees at least 64 KiB (without a Stack Size request); `BootInfo` records that minimum virtual span, translates it through the HHDM, and requires it to fit within a single `BootloaderReclaimable` physical region. The full loader allocation may be larger, and neither its exact bounds nor a guard page are known. Preserve the inherited mapping; do not infer a guard page.
    23	- **Kernel static data:** `RAM_FILESYSTEM`, GDT, IDT, TSS, the ring-0 stack, and double-fault IST stack are static BSS/data in the kernel image. Exception entry also requires the handler code and the TSS/IDT references to remain mapped. Preserve the complete relevant data/BSS pages and stack pages.
    24	- **Limine request data:** request records are in the kernel's writable `.limine_requests` segment. Response objects and the memory-map pointer array are bootloader-reclaimable; `capture_boot_information` copies the values used later, and the shell does not retain those pointers. Keep them mapped until the copy and validation finish; any later reclaim must be explicit rather than reclaiming the whole bootloader-reclaimable class.
    25	- **Inherited page tables:** Limine places its page tables in bootloader-reclaimable memory, but their layout is unspecified. `BootInfo` captures the raw CR3 register value for diagnostics; Rustix does not decode it into a dereferenceable pointer, inspect the root entries, or inventory the live mappings. Keep the old root and its backing pages intact while inspecting/building a replacement; do not reclaim them until a verified switch no longer depends on them.
    26	- **Devices:** COM1 uses x86 port I/O and needs no MMIO virtual mapping. Rustix does not currently request or use a framebuffer. Any future framebuffer or device-register mapping needs an explicit physical range and cache policy.
    27	
    28	## Initial kernel-owned mapping policy (design only)
    29	
    30	A first Rustix-owned x86_64 root should use 4 KiB pages only and require NX support; if NX is unavailable, abort before switching. Preserve ELF permissions exactly: text RX, rodata R/NX, and `.limine_requests`/data/BSS/GOT RW/NX. Reject W+X mappings. Exclude the complete physical kernel-image span from the generic HHDM in the first implementation so no writable direct-map alias can undermine text or rodata permissions. Ordinary RAM HHDM mappings should be RW/NX and WB.
    31	
    32	Preserve the inherited PAT/MTRR configuration during the first transition. The current boot report reads the active `IA32_PAT` value when PAT is supported and parses WB/UC/WC entries, but does not yet inspect MTRRs or establish effective cache types for physical ranges. Under Limine base revision 3 on x86_64, use PAT index 0 for WB, index 3 for UC, and index 5 for WC only when the active configuration is verified. Map MMIO only from explicit device ranges and use UC by default; do not infer MMIO from generic reserved memory-map entries. A framebuffer may use WC only after its response and cache policy are explicitly validated. COM1 uses port I/O and requires no MMIO mapping.
    33	
    34	Do not use huge pages in the first root; add them only when physical coverage, permissions, and cache attributes are uniform across the entire huge-page extent. Future Rustix-owned downward-growing stacks should have at least one 4 KiB unmapped guard page below them. The Limine bootstrap stack has no known guard, so none may be assumed there.
    35	
    36	Never modify Limine's active page tables in place. Build a separate candidate root from owned frames, leave the inherited root and its backing memory intact, and do not switch CR3 until every transition-critical mapping has been preserved and checked. Any pre-switch failure must discard the candidate root and return its frames without changing the active root. This policy is not yet implemented; Rustix does not inspect the inherited root or replace CR3.
    37	
    38	## Planned memory layers
    39	
    40	1. **Validated physical regions:** implemented as a fixed-capacity owned snapshot; allocator ownership and reclaim policy remain future work.
    41	2. **Frame allocator integration:** the bounded core is initialized from `BootInfo`; add real allocation callers only after the page-mapping policy and ownership rules are implemented.
    42	3. **Kernel virtual memory:** Rustix still runs on inherited Limine page tables. Before replacing them, preserve the executing kernel, bootstrap/Ring-0 stacks, request/response data, and exception stacks; define the HHDM extent and explicit mapping permissions/cache attributes. Keep writable-executable mappings disallowed by default and reserve MMIO and guard regions.
    43	4. **Heap:** add a global allocator only after frame allocation and page mapping are initialized. Define allocation failure behavior for kernel and interrupt contexts.
    44	5. **Process address spaces:** isolate userspace; define copy-in/copy-out helpers and never treat user pointers as kernel references.
    45	
    46	## Invariants to preserve
    47	
    48	- Physical ranges use checked arithmetic and the half-open convention `[start, end)`.
    49	- Only complete frames in validated `Usable` regions enter the allocator; the first MiB remains reserved. Firmware and loader-reclaimable regions are not silently promoted to free memory.
    50	- Under the single-allocator-per-physical-map contract, a frame is represented by one allocator-free range or one caller-owned, non-copyable `Frame` token. Only a successful `deallocate` transfers the token back to the allocator; errors return the token and leave allocator ranges and accounting unchanged. The current public constructor relies on callers to enforce unique allocator instances.
    51	- `allocate` returns `None` on frame exhaustion. Metadata-capacity exhaustion is an explicit error, never silent loss of a frame.
    52	- A `Frame` address is physical, not directly dereferenceable. No HHDM translation, page-table mutation, or CR3 switch is permitted until address widths, mapping coverage, permissions/cache attributes, and transition-preservation requirements are established.
    53	- Mapping changes validate alignment, canonical addresses, ownership, and permissions before touching tables.
    54	- User-controlled addresses are validated on every syscall boundary; prior validation is not a substitute for access checks during later copies.
    55	- Tests cover unaligned edges, exact-boundary allocations, arithmetic overflow, overlaps, exhaustion, and unchanged state/ownership on failure.
    56	
    57	These are design requirements for future milestones, not functionality delivered by the current boot summary.

```

     1	//! Fixed-capacity physical frame allocator built from validated memory regions.
     2	//!
     3	//! This module assigns physical frame addresses only. It does not map or access
     4	//! them, and it never promotes loader- or firmware-reclaimable memory to free RAM.
     5	
     6	use crate::memory::{MemoryMap, RegionKind, MAX_MEMORY_REGIONS};
     7	
     8	/// The current x86_64 base page size used by Rustix.
     9	pub const FRAME_SIZE: u64 = 4096;
    10	/// Keep the first MiB unavailable even if a loader labels it usable.
    11	pub const LOW_MEMORY_RESERVED_END: u64 = 0x10_0000;
    12	/// Maximum number of disjoint ranges kept in each fixed range table.
    13	pub const MAX_FRAME_RANGES: usize = MAX_MEMORY_REGIONS;
    14	
    15	#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
    16	pub struct Frame(u64);
    17

    19	    /// Physical address of the beginning of this frame.

    22	    }

    26	        Self(address)

    31	pub enum FrameError {
    32	    RangeTableFull,
    33	    RangeOverlap,
    34	    UnalignedFrame,
    35	    NotManaged,
    36	    AlreadyFree,
    37	    AccountingOverflow,
    38	}

    48	struct FrameRange {

    51	}

    53	impl FrameRange {

    56	
    57	/// A bounded allocator over only validated `Usable` memory-map ranges.
    58	///
    59	/// For a given physical map, callers must use only one allocator instance as
    60	/// the allocation authority; the type does not enforce this uniqueness. Within
    61	/// that instance, each eligible frame is owned by the allocator while free, or
    62	/// by the non-copyable `Frame` token returned by `allocate` while allocated. A
    63	/// frame address is an identifier only; this allocator does not map or access it.
    64	/// Both range tables are sorted, disjoint, page-aligned half-open ranges. The
    65	/// allocator returns `None` on frame exhaustion and reports metadata exhaustion
    66	/// explicitly without changing state or consuming the caller's frame token.
    67	pub struct FrameAllocator {
    68	    managed: [FrameRange; MAX_FRAME_RANGES],
    69	    managed_count: usize,
    70	    free: [FrameRange; MAX_FRAME_RANGES],
    71	    free_count: usize,
    72	    managed_frames: u64,
    73	    free_frames: u64,
    74	}
    75	
    76	impl FrameAllocator {
    77	    const EMPTY: Self = Self {
    78	        managed: [FrameRange::EMPTY; MAX_FRAME_RANGES],
    79	        managed_count: 0,
    80	        free: [FrameRange::EMPTY; MAX_FRAME_RANGES],
    81	        free_count: 0,
    82	        managed_frames: 0,
    83	        free_frames: 0,
    84	    };
    85	
    86	    /// Build allocator metadata without allocating or touching physical memory.
    87	    ///
    88	    /// The caller must ensure there is only one allocator instance per physical
    89	    /// map; constructing multiple instances would duplicate allocation authority.
    90	    pub fn from_memory_map(map: &MemoryMap) -> Result<Self, FrameError> {
    91	        let mut allocator = Self::EMPTY;
    92	        let page_mask = !(FRAME_SIZE - 1);
    93	
    94	        for region in map.regions() {
    95	            if region.kind() != RegionKind::Usable {
    96	                continue;
    97	            }
    98	
    99	            let start = region.start().max(LOW_MEMORY_RESERVED_END);
   100	            let Some(start) = start
   101	                .checked_add(FRAME_SIZE - 1)
   102	                .map(|address| address & page_mask)
   103	            else {
   104	                continue;
   105	            };
   106	            let end = region.end() & page_mask;
   107	            if start >= end {
   108	                continue;
   109	            }
   110	
   111	            let range = FrameRange { start, end };
   112	            Self::insert_range(&mut allocator.managed, &mut allocator.managed_count, range)?;
   113	            Self::insert_range(&mut allocator.free, &mut allocator.free_count, range)?;
   114	
   115	            let frames = (end - start) / FRAME_SIZE;
   116	            allocator.managed_frames = allocator
   117	                .managed_frames
   118	                .checked_add(frames)
   119	                .ok_or(FrameError::AccountingOverflow)?;
   120	            allocator.free_frames = allocator
   121	                .free_frames
   122	                .checked_add(frames)
   123	                .ok_or(FrameError::AccountingOverflow)?;
   124	        }
   125	
   126	        Ok(allocator)
   127	    }
   128	
   129	    /// Number of complete frames inside eligible usable regions.
   130	    pub const fn managed_frames(&self) -> u64 {
   131	        self.managed_frames
   132	    }
   133	
   134	    /// Number of currently unallocated frames.
   135	    pub const fn free_frames(&self) -> u64 {
   136	        self.free_frames
   137	    }
   138	
   139	    /// Allocate the lowest-address available frame, or return `None` on exhaustion.
   140	    pub fn allocate(&mut self) -> Option<Frame> {
   141	        if self.free_count == 0 {
   142	            return None;
   143	        }
   144	
   145	        let address = self.free[0].start;
   146	        self.free[0].start += FRAME_SIZE;
   147	        self.free_frames -= 1;
   148	        if self.free[0].start == self.free[0].end {
   149	            Self::remove_range(&mut self.free, &mut self.free_count, 0);
   150	        }
   151	        Some(Frame(address))
   152	    }
   153	
   154	    /// Return a frame to the allocator, merging adjacent free ranges.
   155	    ///
   156	    /// On error, the returned `DeallocationError` retains the frame token so the
   157	    /// caller can retry or otherwise preserve ownership. Failed returns do not
   158	    /// change allocator state.
   159	    pub fn deallocate(&mut self, frame: Frame) -> Result<(), DeallocationError> {
   160	        let address = frame.address();
   161	        if address & (FRAME_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
   162	            return Err(DeallocationError {
   163	                error: FrameError::UnalignedFrame,
   164	                frame,
   165	            });
   166	        }
   167	        let Some(end) = address.checked_add(FRAME_SIZE) else {
   168	            return Err(DeallocationError {
   169	                error: FrameError::NotManaged,
   170	                frame,
   171	            });
   172	        };
   173	        if !self
   174	            .managed
   175	            .iter()
   176	            .take(self.managed_count)
   177	            .any(|range| range.start <= address && end <= range.end)
   178	        {
   179	            return Err(DeallocationError {
   180	                error: FrameError::NotManaged,
   181	                frame,
   182	            });
   183	        }
   184	        if self
   185	            .free
   186	            .iter()
   187	            .take(self.free_count)
   188	            .any(|range| address < range.end && range.start < end)
   189	        {
   190	            return Err(DeallocationError {
   191	                error: FrameError::AlreadyFree,
   192	                frame,
   193	            });
   194	        }
   195	
   196	        let Some(new_free_count) = self.free_frames.checked_add(1) else {
   197	            return Err(DeallocationError {
   198	                error: FrameError::AccountingOverflow,
   199	                frame,
   200	            });
   201	        };
   202	        if let Err(error) = Self::insert_range(
   203	            &mut self.free,
   204	            &mut self.free_count,
   205	            FrameRange {
   206	                start: address,
   207	                end,
   208	            },
   209	        ) {
   210	            return Err(DeallocationError { error, frame });
   211	        }
   212	        self.free_frames = new_free_count;
   213	        Ok(())
   214	    }
   215	
   216	    fn insert_range(
   217	        ranges: &mut [FrameRange; MAX_FRAME_RANGES],
   218	        count: &mut usize,
   219	        incoming: FrameRange,
   220	    ) -> Result<(), FrameError> {
   221	        let mut index = 0;
   222	        while index < *count && ranges[index].start < incoming.start {
   223	            index += 1;
   224	        }
   225	
   226	        if (index > 0 && ranges[index - 1].end > incoming.start)
   227	            || (index < *count && incoming.end > ranges[index].start)
   228	        {
   229	            return Err(FrameError::RangeOverlap);
   230	        }
   231	
   232	        let joins_previous = index > 0 && ranges[index - 1].end == incoming.start;
   233	        let joins_next = index < *count && incoming.end == ranges[index].start;
   234	        match (joins_previous, joins_next) {
   235	            (true, true) => {
   236	                ranges[index - 1].end = ranges[index].end;
   237	                Self::remove_range(ranges, count, index);
   238	            }
   239	            (true, false) => ranges[index - 1].end = incoming.end,
   240	            (false, true) => ranges[index].start = incoming.start,
   241	            (false, false) => {
   242	                if *count == MAX_FRAME_RANGES {
   243	                    return Err(FrameError::RangeTableFull);
   244	                }
   245	                for move_index in (index..*count).rev() {
   246	                    ranges[move_index + 1] = ranges[move_index];
   247	                }
   248	                ranges[index] = incoming;
   249	                *count += 1;
   250	            }
   251	        }
   252	        Ok(())
   253	    }
   254	
   255	    fn remove_range(ranges: &mut [FrameRange; MAX_FRAME_RANGES], count: &mut usize, index: usize) {
   256	        for move_index in index..(*count - 1) {
   257	            ranges[move_index] = ranges[move_index + 1];
   258	        }
   259	        *count -= 1;
   260	        ranges[*count] = FrameRange::EMPTY;
   261	    }
   262	}

   429	    fn fragmentation_is_bounded_by_the_fixed_range_table() {
   430	        let frame_count = MAX_FRAME_RANGES * 2 + 1;
   431	        let regions = [RawMemoryRegion {
   432	            base: 0x10_0000,
   433	            length: FRAME_SIZE * frame_count as u64,
   434	            kind: 0,
   435	        }];
   436	        let mut allocator = allocator_for(&regions);
   437	        let mut allocated: [Frame; MAX_FRAME_RANGES * 2 + 1] =
   438	            core::array::from_fn(|_| Frame::from_address(0));
   439	
   440	        for frame in &mut allocated {
   441	            *frame = allocator.allocate().unwrap();
   442	        }
   443	        let mut rejected = None;
   444	        for index in (0..allocated.len()).step_by(2) {
   445	            let frame = core::mem::replace(&mut allocated[index], Frame::from_address(0));
   446	            let result = allocator.deallocate(frame);
   447	            if index == MAX_FRAME_RANGES * 2 {
   448	                let error = result.unwrap_err();
   449	                assert_eq!(error.error, FrameError::RangeTableFull);
   450	                assert_eq!(error.frame.address(), 0x10_0000 + index as u64 * FRAME_SIZE);
   451	                assert_eq!(allocator.free_frames(), MAX_FRAME_RANGES as u64);
   452	                rejected = Some(error.frame);
   453	            } else {
   454	                assert_eq!(result, Ok(()));
   455	            }
   456	        }
   457	
   458	        let neighbor = core::mem::replace(
   459	            &mut allocated[MAX_FRAME_RANGES * 2 - 1],
   460	            Frame::from_address(0),
   461	        );
   462	        allocator.deallocate(neighbor).unwrap();
   463	        allocator.deallocate(rejected.unwrap()).unwrap();
   464	        assert_eq!(allocator.free_frames(), MAX_FRAME_RANGES as u64 + 2);
   465	    }
   466	}

    84	## Milestone 2 — memory ownership, paging, heap, and exceptions (in progress)
    85	
    86	This milestone must establish a safe basis for future allocation and kernel services. Do not allocate a frame for a mapping or dereference it through the HHDM until the mapping/ownership proof is explicit.
    87	
    88	### Source work present
    89	
    90	- [x] Fixed-capacity frame allocator over validated `Usable` ranges, with page alignment, first-MiB reservation, deallocation, coalescing, bounded metadata, and host tests (`src/frame.rs`).
    91	- [x] Initialize allocator metadata from the boot snapshot and expose counts through Crabshell `meminfo`.
    92	- [x] Specify frame-token ownership and failure semantics: `Frame` is non-copyable, failed deallocation returns the token without changing allocator state, and the current API documents (but does not enforce) one allocator instance per physical map.
    93	- [x] Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT and fatal handlers for exception vectors 0–31.
    94	- [x] Give double faults a dedicated IST stack; report saved register/error context and CR2/error bits for page faults.
    95	- [x] Keep maskable IRQ delivery disabled; current exception handling is fatal-only.
    96	
    97	### Required memory-management work
    98	
    99	- [x] Read `CR4.LA57` and `CPUID.80000008H` when available to report active/max address widths; validate Limine pointers against active canonical width and host-test 48-/57-bit boundaries.
   100	- [x] Document base revision 3 HHDM region coverage, page-boundary behavior, x86 permissions/cache attributes, and the rule that Rustix does not yet use the HHDM for physical access.
   101	- [x] Reject physical map ranges outside the reported CPUID width when available; validate page-rounded HHDM address arithmetic/canonicality for base revision 3 mapped types. This is protocol-derived validation, not a page-table walk or proof of pointer readability.
   102	- [x] Record current transition-critical objects, their mapping sources/permissions, and unresolved bounds in `docs/architecture/memory.md`.
   103	- [x] Capture Limine's executable physical/virtual bases and resolve the complete linker-defined half-open kernel-image span in `BootInfo`; validate the linker/loader base agreement, canonical virtual span, arithmetic, and reported physical width.
   104	- [x] Capture and validate the linker-defined page-aligned text, rodata, and data segment bounds in `BootInfo`; host-test their ordering, contiguity, rounded image coverage, and derived physical ranges. `just verify` reported all three in QEMU on 2026-10-21. This metadata does not prove inherited page-table coverage or live permissions.
   105	- [x] Capture and validate the protocol-guaranteed minimum 64 KiB active boot-stack span from entry `RSP`; validate its HHDM translation and `BootloaderReclaimable` backing. The actual loader allocation may be larger.
   106	- [ ] Resolve the exact loader stack allocation bounds and guard status.

/// Read the current raw CR3 value without changing page-table state.
pub fn read_cr3_raw() -> u64 {
    let cr3: u64;
    // SAFETY: Rustix runs at CPL 0; reading CR3 is privileged but non-mutating.
    unsafe { asm!("mov {}, cr3", out(reg) cr3, options(nomem, nostack, preserves_flags)) };
    cr3
}
```

    let ring0_stack = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(RING0_STACK).cast::<u8>();
    let double_fault_stack = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(DOUBLE_FAULT_STACK).cast::<u8>();

    let ring0_top = unsafe { ring0_stack.add(STACK_SIZE) } as u64;

    let double_fault_top = unsafe { double_fault_stack.add(STACK_SIZE) } as u64;

## Matches in rustix\docs\architecture\memory.md

### # Memory-management direction › ## Mapping inventory before a CR3 transition › L22-23
```
- **Active stack and `BootInfo`:** The assembly `_start` shim captures Limine's entry `RSP` before any Rust prologue. Under the SysV entry convention the synthetic return slot occupies 8 bytes, so `RSP + 8` is the exclusive stack top. The protocol guarantees at least 64 KiB (without a Stack Size request); `BootInfo` records that minimum virtual span, translates it through the HHDM, and requires it to fit within a single `BootloaderReclaimable` physical region. The full loader allocation may be larger, and neither its exact bounds nor a guard page are known. Preserve the inherited mapping; do not infer a guard page.
- **Kernel static data:** `RAM_FILESYSTEM`, GDT, IDT, TSS, the ring-0 stack, and double-fault IST stack are static BSS/data in the kernel image. Exception entry also requires the handler code and the TSS/IDT references to remain mapped. Preserve the complete relevant data/BSS pages and stack pages.
```

   152	impl PatLayout {
   153	    pub fn from_msr_value(value: u64) -> Result<Self, PatLayoutError> {
   154	        let mut memory_types = [0; 8];
   155	        for (index, memory_type) in memory_types.iter_mut().enumerate() {
   156	            let entry = ((value >> (index * 8)) & 0xff) as u8;
   157	            if entry & !0x07 != 0 {
   158	                return Err(PatLayoutError::ReservedBits { index: index as u8 });
   159	            }
   160	            if entry == 0b010 || entry == 0b011 {
   161	                return Err(PatLayoutError::ReservedMemoryType {
   162	                    index: index as u8,
   163	                    value: entry,
   164	                });
   165	            }
   166	            *memory_type = entry;
   167	        }
   168	        Ok(Self { memory_types })
   169	    }

   175	            CachePolicy::WriteCombining => 0b001,
   176	            CachePolicy::WriteBack => 0b110,

   203	/// Validate one contiguous range for a future 4 KiB page-mapping operation.
   204	///
   205	/// The physical-address width must be known; passing `None` is rejected rather
   206	/// than treating an unknown bound as unbounded. Successful validation does not
   207	/// establish that physical frames are owned by a caller or mapped in the HHDM.
   208	pub fn validate_mapping_range(
   209	    virtual_start: u64,
   210	    physical_start: u64,
   211	    length: u64,
   212	    permissions: PagePermissions,
   213	    physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
   214	    virtual_address_bits: u8,
   215	) -> Result<ValidatedMappingRange, MappingError> {
   216	    if virtual_address_bits == 0 || virtual_address_bits > 64 {
   217	        return Err(MappingError::InvalidVirtualAddressWidth);
   218	    }
   219	    let physical_address_bits =
   220	        physical_address_bits.ok_or(MappingError::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)?;
   221	    if physical_address_bits == 0 || physical_address_bits > 64 {
   222	        return Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
   223	    }
   224	    if length == 0 {
   225	        return Err(MappingError::EmptyRange);
   226	    }
   227	    if (virtual_start | physical_start | length) & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
   228	        return Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange);
   229	    }
   230	    if permissions.writable && permissions.executable {
   231	        return Err(MappingError::WritableExecutable);
   232	    }
   233	
   234	    let virtual_end = virtual_start
   235	        .checked_add(length)
   236	        .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
   237	    let physical_end = physical_start
   238	        .checked_add(length)
   239	        .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
   240	    if !physical_range_fits_address_width(physical_start, physical_end, physical_address_bits) {
   241	        return Err(MappingError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds);
   242	    }

   244	    let last_virtual_address = virtual_end - 1;
   245	    if !is_canonical_virtual_address(virtual_start, virtual_address_bits)
   246	        || !is_canonical_virtual_address(last_virtual_address, virtual_address_bits)
   247	    {
   248	        return Err(MappingError::NonCanonicalVirtualRange);
   249	    }
   250	    if virtual_address_bits < 64
   251	        && ((virtual_start ^ last_virtual_address) & (1u64 << (virtual_address_bits - 1))) != 0
   252	    {
   253	        return Err(MappingError::CrossesCanonicalHole);
   254	    }
   255	
   256	    Ok(ValidatedMappingRange {
   257	        virtual_start,
   258	        virtual_end,
   259	        physical_start,
   260	        physical_end,
   261	        physical_address_bits,
   262	        permissions,
   263	    })
   264	}

   270	    };

   105	/// Validate page-aligned PT_LOAD mapping spans and translate them to physical ranges.
   106	///
   107	/// The three spans must be contiguous, ordered text/rodata/data ranges that cover
   108	/// the linker-defined image after rounding its final byte to a 4 KiB boundary.
   109	/// This validates linker metadata; it does not prove that inherited page tables
   110	/// map these ranges or that their backing frames are owned by Rustix.
   111	pub const fn kernel_image_segments(
   112	    physical_base: u64,
   113	    reported_virtual_base: u64,
   114	    image_virtual_start: u64,
   115	    image_virtual_end: u64,
   116	    virtual_segments: KernelImageVirtualSegments,
   117	    physical_bits: Option<u8>,
   118	    virtual_bits: u8,
   119	) -> Option<KernelImageSegments> {
   120	    let Some(_) = kernel_image_physical_end(
   121	        physical_base,
   122	        reported_virtual_base,
   123	        image_virtual_start,
   124	        image_virtual_end,
   125	        physical_bits,
   126	        virtual_bits,
   127	    ) else {
   128	        return None;
   129	    };
   130	
   131	    let (text_start, text_end) = virtual_segments.text;
   132	    let (rodata_start, rodata_end) = virtual_segments.rodata;
   133	    let (data_start, data_end) = virtual_segments.data;
   134	    if text_start != image_virtual_start
   135	        || text_start >= text_end
   136	        || text_end != rodata_start
   137	        || rodata_start >= rodata_end
   138	        || rodata_end != data_start
   139	        || data_start >= image_virtual_end
   140	        || image_virtual_end > data_end
   141	    {
   142	        return None;
   143	    }
   144	
   145	    let Some(rounded_image_end) = image_virtual_end.checked_add(0xfff) else {
   146	        return None;
   147	    };
   148	    let rounded_image_end = rounded_image_end & !0xfff;
   149	    if data_end != rounded_image_end
   150	        || text_start & 0xfff != 0
   151	        || text_end & 0xfff != 0
   152	        || rodata_start & 0xfff != 0
   153	        || rodata_end & 0xfff != 0
   154	        || data_start & 0xfff != 0
   155	        || data_end & 0xfff != 0

   158	    }
