```rustix/docs/architecture/boot.md
     1	# Boot protocol and kernel entry
     2	
     3	## Current boot contract
     4	
     5	Rustix currently expects a Limine-compatible x86_64 handoff. Limine is an external loader; it does not implement Rustix's kernel services. The kernel requests protocol base revision 3, the higher-half direct-map (HHDM) offset, the executable physical/virtual base addresses, and the memory map.
     6	
     7	Requests are kept in `.limine_requests_start`, `.limine_requests`, and `.limine_requests_end` by `linker.ld`. The response-bearing request records are mutable and the output `.limine_requests` section is placed in a writable load segment because Limine fills their response pointers before entering Rustix. The ELF entry symbol is `_start`.
     8	
     9	### Entry sequence
    10	
    11	1. Limine loads the ELF and transfers control to the assembly `_start` shim under the x86_64 Limine protocol. The shim captures the entry `RSP`, aligns the stack for a SysV call, and invokes `rustix_start`.
    12	2. `rustix_start` configures the legacy COM1 UART for polling output and input.
    13	3. The kernel disables maskable interrupts and installs its bootstrap GDT, TSS, and fatal-exception IDT. This does not initialize IRQ delivery.
    14	4. The kernel reads CPU address widths and the raw inherited CR3 value. It reads EFER.NXE only when CPUID reports NX, and reads `IA32_PAT` only when CPUID reports PAT; these MSR reads do not change CPU state. It copies the HHDM, executable-address, and memory-map responses into an owned `BootInfo` snapshot, validates pointers against the active linear width, derives and validates the linker-defined kernel image span and its page-aligned text/rodata/data segment spans from Limine's executable bases, and derives the protocol-guaranteed minimum initial-stack span from the captured entry `RSP`. It validates physical bounds, stack HHDM arithmetic/coverage, and page-rounded HHDM arithmetic for protocol-mapped memory types. It then builds a frame allocator only from validated usable ranges and rejects malformed metadata before starting the shell.
    15	5. The kernel reports the raw inherited CR3 value, physical and virtual kernel-image spans, linker-defined text RX, rodata R/NX, and data RW/NX segment spans, minimum protocol-guaranteed stack span, validated memory ranges, frame counts, CPU features/address widths, NXE state, and the active PAT value plus recognized WB/UC/WC indices, then seeds a volatile RAM filesystem and enters Crabshell on the serial console.
    16	6. The shell busy-polls COM1 because no scheduler or UART interrupt handler exists yet. The kernel does not return to the loader.
    17	
    18	The linker exports page-aligned virtual bounds for each of the kernel's three load segments. Boot validates their order, contiguity, coverage of the rounded image span, and derived physical ranges, then reports them with the permissions intended by `linker.ld`. This is a validated linker/boot inventory only: Rustix does not inspect the inherited page tables, so it has not established that the segments are mapped or that their live permissions match the ELF flags.
    19	
    20	The memory-map snapshot stores physical regions as checked half-open ranges `[start, end)` and retains Limine's known classifications plus unknown type values. It does not authorize Rustix to access every region, reclaim loader memory, or mutate the current page tables. Rustix checks physical bounds and calculates canonical HHDM ranges for memory types covered by the requested base revision, but does not dereference physical memory through the HHDM or verify the inherited page tables. These metadata checks cannot prove that a Limine-provided non-null pointer is readable; that remains an explicit handoff trust boundary.
    21	
    22	## Link and target configuration
    23	
    24	- Rust target: `x86_64-unknown-none`
    25	- Linker script: `linker.ld`
    26	- Kernel virtual base: `0xffffffff80000000`
    27	- Required code-generation constraints: kernel code model, static relocation, and disabled red zone
    28	- Rust panic strategy: abort; the panic handler logs and halts
    29	
    30	See [`../development/building.md`](../development/building.md) for commands and toolchain setup.
    31	
    32	## Failure behavior and limitations
    33	
    34	Missing responses, bad pointers/alignment, invalid HHDM values, invalid image or segment bounds, invalid initial-stack spans, excessive counts, null entries, address arithmetic overflow, and invalid or overlapping physical ranges reject the complete boot snapshot. `_start` halts before starting Crabshell rather than continuing with partial memory metadata. Structural checks cannot establish that a non-null loader pointer is mapped and readable; that remains an explicit trust assumption of the Limine handoff. The installed exception handlers print fatal context and halt; no fault recovery or maskable IRQ delivery is provided. COM1 writes use a bounded polling loop so a non-responsive UART does not block forever.
    35	
    36	The repository includes a Limine v12.9.3 binary archive pin with SHA-256 verification, a hybrid ISO recipe, and a bounded QEMU serial smoke test that checks Crabshell's prompt. On 2026-10-16, `just verify` passed in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 on both the working tree and a clean detached worktree with an empty `target` directory; the clean run fetched and built the pinned Limine host utility, created the image, and QEMU reported boot metadata before reaching the prompt. Milestone 0's local exit criteria are met. Hosted CI has not been observed. There is still no framebuffer console, reboot/shutdown interface, or broader boot-protocol compatibility test. The available serial and keyboard drivers do not imply a general device model.
    37	
    38	## Future boot work
    39	
    40	The kernel now snapshots and validates the Limine map and preserves firmware and loader-reclaimable classifications without reclaiming them. No region is considered Rustix-owned or free for allocation yet. Loader-reclaimable memory must remain unavailable until Rustix has copied any needed boot data and explicitly reaches a documented reclaim point; ACPI reclaimable memory needs the ACPI tables to be copied or parsed first. Frame allocation, page-table replacement, and ownership transfer remain future work.

```

    28	/// Read CPUID identity, selected feature bits, and supported read-only MSR state.
    29	pub fn identify() -> CpuInfo {
    30	    // CPUID is part of the x86_64 architectural baseline.
    31	    let basic = __cpuid(0);
    32	    let mut vendor = [0; 12];
    33	    vendor[0..4].copy_from_slice(&basic.ebx.to_le_bytes());
    34	    vendor[4..8].copy_from_slice(&basic.edx.to_le_bytes());
    35	    vendor[8..12].copy_from_slice(&basic.ecx.to_le_bytes());
    36	
    37	    let basic_features = if basic.eax >= 1 {
    38	        Some(__cpuid(1))
    39	    } else {
    40	        None
    41	    };
    42	    let extended = __cpuid(0x8000_0000);
    43	    let extended_features = if extended.eax >= 0x8000_0001 {
    44	        Some(__cpuid(0x8000_0001))
    45	    } else {
    46	        None
    47	    };
    48	    let structured_features = if basic.eax >= 7 {
    49	        Some(__cpuid_count(7, 0))
    50	    } else {
    51	        None
    52	    };

    93	pub fn read_cr3_raw() -> u64 {

   100	/// Read the active linear-address width from CR4.LA57 without changing paging.
   101	/// Read a model-specific register at CPL 0.
   102	///
   103	/// # Safety
   104	/// The caller must ensure the MSR exists on this CPU and that the current
   105	/// privilege level permits the read.
   106	unsafe fn read_msr(index: u32) -> u64 {
   107	    let low: u32;
   108	    let high: u32;
   109	    // SAFETY: The caller guarantees that this MSR is supported and accessible.
   110	    unsafe {
   111	        asm!(
   112	            "rdmsr",
   113	            in("ecx") index,
   114	            out("eax") low,
   115	            out("edx") high,
   116	            options(nomem, nostack)
   117	        );
   118	    }
   119	    (u64::from(high) << 32) | u64::from(low)
   120	}
   121	
   122	pub fn active_linear_address_bits() -> u8 {
   123	    let cr4: u64;
   124	    // SAFETY: Rustix runs at CPL 0; reading CR4 is a privileged, non-mutating operation.
   125	    unsafe { asm!("mov {}, cr4", out(reg) cr4, options(nomem, nostack, preserves_flags)) };
   126	    if cr4 & (1 << 12) != 0 {
   127	        57
   128	    } else {
   129	        48
   130	    }
   131	}

pub extern "C" fn rustix_exception_dispatch(context: *const ExceptionContext) -> ! {
    // SAFETY: The common assembly stub passes a pointer to its normalized stack
    // frame, which remains live for the duration of this non-returning handler.
    let context = unsafe { &*context };
    crate::kprintln!(
        "KERNEL EXCEPTION: vector {} ({}) error={:#018x} RIP={:#018x}",
        context.vector,
        exception_name(context.vector),
        context.error_code,
        context.rip
    );

### impl ValidatedMappingRange › pub fn leaf_4k_entry › L60-77
```
    pub fn leaf_4k_entry(
        self,
        page_offset: u64,
        pat_index: u8,
        nx_enabled: bool,
    ) -> Result<PageTableEntry, MappingError> {
        if page_offset & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
            return Err(MappingError::UnalignedPageOffset);
        }
        if page_offset >= self.length() {
            return Err(MappingError::PageOffsetOutOfRange);
        }
        if self.physical_address_bits > MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS {
            return Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
        }
        if pat_index > 7 {
            return Err(MappingError::InvalidPatIndex);
        }

**Considering paging module implementation**

I’m thinking about potentially implementing MTRR inside the paging module, but I'm not sure if we have enough time for that. A simpler approach might be necessary, like clarifying the exact stack issue in the documentation. I realize the user might expect coding, so let's focus on crafting a roadmap for the page-table. Implementing precise CR3 parsing seems like a safer first step, so I’m considering how to design `Cr3Value` accurately.

**Evaluating PCID and parsing logic**

I’m considering the implications of using a 12-bit PCID. If the PCID is enabled, flags should address low12, without PWT/PCD flags. For scenarios where it’s not enabled, low bits 3 and 4 can be used, while 0-2 should remain reserved. I should also clarify that the root address shouldn't be zero, though it might theoretically be zero. Importantly, for bits set to 52, allowed address bits span 12 to 51, with others reserved. I need to check the current CPU's physical address bits and PCID status for accurate parsing.

**Assessing CR4 and PCID availability**

I’m looking at how CR4 is read twice without any issues, but I need to consider the availability of the CR4.PCIDE. The PCID feature is supported if the CPUID.1 ECX's bit 17 is set, but it also needs to be enabled. I think I need to add a `has_pcid` check within the CPUID and ensure that it's active with both the `has_pcid` and CR4 bit 17. This should apply to all x64 CPUID.1 availability checks.
</thinking>

   489	fn is_valid_pointer<T>(pointer: *const T, virtual_address_bits: u8) -> bool {
   490	    let start = pointer as usize;
   491	    let Some(end) = start.checked_add(size_of::<T>()) else {
   492	        return false;
   493	    };
   494	    !pointer.is_null()
   495	        && start & (align_of::<T>() - 1) == 0
   496	        && is_canonical_virtual_address(start as u64, virtual_address_bits)
   497	        && is_canonical_virtual_address((end - 1) as u64, virtual_address_bits)
   498	}

pub fn active_linear_address_bits() -> u8 {
    let cr4: u64;
    // SAFETY: Rustix runs at CPL 0; reading CR4 is a privileged, non-mutating operation.
    unsafe { asm!("mov {}, cr4", out(reg) cr4, options(nomem, nostack, preserves_flags)) };
    if cr4 & (1 << 12) != 0 {
        57
    } else {
        48
    }
}
```

//! Pure validation for future x86_64 4 KiB mappings.
//!
//! This module validates address shape and permissions only. It does not prove
//! frame ownership or mapping coverage, access physical memory, or modify tables.

use crate::memory::{is_canonical_virtual_address, physical_range_fits_address_width};

/// Base page size supported by the initial Rustix mapping policy.
pub const PAGE_SIZE: u64 = 4096;

/// The physical root address and interpretation of CR3's low bits.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Cr3Root {
    physical_address: u64,
    low_bits: Cr3LowBits,
}

impl Cr3Root {
    pub const fn physical_address(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_address
    }

    pub const fn low_bits(self) -> Cr3LowBits {
        self.low_bits
    }
}

/// CR3's low bits are either a PCID or the paging-structure cache controls.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Cr3LowBits {
    Pcid(u16),
    CacheControls {
        page_level_write_through: bool,
        page_level_cache_disable: bool,
    },
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Cr3Error {
    UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth,
    InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth,
    ReservedBits,
}

/// Decode the currently active CR3 value without dereferencing its root table.
///
/// The caller supplies the reported physical-address width and whether CR4.PCIDE
/// is active, since those determine the valid address and low-bit encodings.
pub fn parse_cr3_root(
    raw: u64,
    physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    pcid_enabled: bool,
) -> Result<Cr3Root, Cr3Error> {
    let physical_address_bits =
        physical_address_bits.ok_or(Cr3Error::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)?;
    if !(12..=MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS).contains(&physical_address_bits) {
        return Err(Cr3Error::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
    }

    let physical_mask = ((1u64 << physical_address_bits) - 1) & !(PAGE_SIZE - 1);
    let low_mask = if pcid_enabled {
        PAGE_SIZE - 1
    } else {
        (1 << 3) | (1 << 4)
    };
    if raw & !(physical_mask | low_mask) != 0 {
        return Err(Cr3Error::ReservedBits);
    }

    let low_bits = if pcid_enabled {
        Cr3LowBits::Pcid((raw & (PAGE_SIZE - 1)) as u16)
    } else {
        Cr3LowBits::CacheControls {
            page_level_write_through: raw & (1 << 3) != 0,
            page_level_cache_disable: raw & (1 << 4) != 0,
        }
    };

    Ok(Cr3Root {
        physical_address: raw & physical_mask,
        low_bits,
    })
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PagePermissions {
    pub writable: bool,
    pub executable: bool,
    pub user_accessible: bool,
}

/// A mapping range whose arithmetic, address widths, alignment, and W^X policy passed validation.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ValidatedMappingRange {
    virtual_start: u64,
    virtual_end: u64,
    physical_start: u64,
    physical_end: u64,
    physical_address_bits: u8,
    permissions: PagePermissions,
}

impl ValidatedMappingRange {
    pub const fn virtual_start(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_start
    }

    pub const fn virtual_end(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_end
    }

    pub const fn physical_start(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_start
    }

    pub const fn physical_end(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_end
    }

    pub const fn permissions(self) -> PagePermissions {
        self.permissions
    }

    pub const fn length(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_end - self.virtual_start
    }

    /// Encode a single 4 KiB leaf PTE within this validated range.
    ///
    /// `pat_index` is the raw three-bit PAT index; the caller must have verified
    /// that the active PAT MSR gives it the intended cache semantics. `nx_enabled`
    /// must reflect EFER.NXE, not merely CPUID support. This only creates entry
    /// bits—it neither owns the frame nor writes a page table.
    pub fn leaf_4k_entry(
        self,
        page_offset: u64,
        pat_index: u8,
        nx_enabled: bool,
    ) -> Result<PageTableEntry, MappingError> {
        if page_offset & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
            return Err(MappingError::UnalignedPageOffset);
        }
        if page_offset >= self.length() {
            return Err(MappingError::PageOffsetOutOfRange);
        }
        if self.physical_address_bits > MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS {
            return Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
        }
        if pat_index > 7 {
            return Err(MappingError::InvalidPatIndex);
        }
        if !self.permissions.executable && !nx_enabled {
            return Err(MappingError::NxNotEnabled);
        }

        let physical_address = self
            .physical_start
            .checked_add(page_offset)
            .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
        let mut bits = physical_address | PTE_PRESENT;
        if self.permissions.writable {
            bits |= PTE_WRITABLE;
        }
        if self.permissions.user_accessible {
            bits |= PTE_USER;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b001 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_WRITE_THROUGH;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b010 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_CACHE_DISABLE;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b100 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_PAT_4K;
        }
        if !self.permissions.executable {
            bits |= PTE_NO_EXECUTE;
        }

        Ok(PageTableEntry(bits))
    }
}

/// Maximum x86_64 physical-address width representable by a 4 KiB PTE.
pub const MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS: u8 = 52;
const PTE_PRESENT: u64 = 1 << 0;
const PTE_WRITABLE: u64 = 1 << 1;
const PTE_USER: u64 = 1 << 2;
const PTE_WRITE_THROUGH: u64 = 1 << 3;
const PTE_CACHE_DISABLE: u64 = 1 << 4;
const PTE_PAT_4K: u64 = 1 << 7;
const PTE_NO_EXECUTE: u64 = 1 << 63;

/// Raw bits for one x86_64 4 KiB leaf page-table entry.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PageTableEntry(u64);

impl PageTableEntry {
    pub const fn bits(self) -> u64 {
        self.0
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum CachePolicy {
    WriteBack,
    Uncacheable,
    WriteCombining,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum PatLayoutError {
    ReservedBits { index: u8 },
    ReservedMemoryType { index: u8, value: u8 },
}

/// Parsed PAT memory types from an `IA32_PAT` MSR value supplied by the caller.
///
/// Parsing does not read the MSR or establish that this is the active value. The
/// MTRR configuration may also affect the effective cache type for an address.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PatLayout {
    memory_types: [u8; 8],
}

impl PatLayout {
    pub fn from_msr_value(value: u64) -> Result<Self, PatLayoutError> {
        let mut memory_types = [0; 8];
        for (index, memory_type) in memory_types.iter_mut().enumerate() {
            let entry = ((value >> (index * 8)) & 0xff) as u8;
            if entry & !0x07 != 0 {
                return Err(PatLayoutError::ReservedBits { index: index as u8 });
            }
            if entry == 0b010 || entry == 0b011 {
                return Err(PatLayoutError::ReservedMemoryType {
                    index: index as u8,
                    value: entry,
                });
            }
            *memory_type = entry;
        }
        Ok(Self { memory_types })
    }

    /// Return the first PAT index with the exact requested memory type.
    pub fn index_for(self, policy: CachePolicy) -> Option<u8> {
        let requested_type = match policy {
            CachePolicy::Uncacheable => 0b000,
            CachePolicy::WriteCombining => 0b001,
            CachePolicy::WriteBack => 0b110,
        };
        self.memory_types
            .iter()
            .position(|memory_type| *memory_type == requested_type)
            .map(|index| index as u8)
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum MappingError {
    EmptyRange,
    UnalignedRange,
    AddressOverflow,
    InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth,
    UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth,
    InvalidVirtualAddressWidth,
    PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds,
    NonCanonicalVirtualRange,
    CrossesCanonicalHole,
    WritableExecutable,
    UnalignedPageOffset,
    PageOffsetOutOfRange,
    InvalidPatIndex,
    NxNotEnabled,
}

/// Validate one contiguous range for a future 4 KiB page-mapping operation.
///
/// The physical-address width must be known; passing `None` is rejected rather
/// than treating an unknown bound as unbounded. Successful validation does not
/// establish that physical frames are owned by a caller or mapped in the HHDM.
pub fn validate_mapping_range(
    virtual_start: u64,
    physical_start: u64,
    length: u64,
    permissions: PagePermissions,
    physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    virtual_address_bits: u8,
) -> Result<ValidatedMappingRange, MappingError> {
    if virtual_address_bits == 0 || virtual_address_bits > 64 {
        return Err(MappingError::InvalidVirtualAddressWidth);
    }
    let physical_address_bits =
        physical_address_bits.ok_or(MappingError::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)?;
    if physical_address_bits == 0 || physical_address_bits > 64 {
        return Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
    }
    if length == 0 {
        return Err(MappingError::EmptyRange);
    }
    if (virtual_start | physical_start | length) & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
        return Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange);
    }
    if permissions.writable && permissions.executable {
        return Err(MappingError::WritableExecutable);
    }

    let virtual_end = virtual_start
        .checked_add(length)
        .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
    let physical_end = physical_start
        .checked_add(length)
        .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
    if !physical_range_fits_address_width(physical_start, physical_end, physical_address_bits) {
        return Err(MappingError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds);
    }

    let last_virtual_address = virtual_end - 1;
    if !is_canonical_virtual_address(virtual_start, virtual_address_bits)
        || !is_canonical_virtual_address(last_virtual_address, virtual_address_bits)
    {
        return Err(MappingError::NonCanonicalVirtualRange);
    }
    if virtual_address_bits < 64
        && ((virtual_start ^ last_virtual_address) & (1u64 << (virtual_address_bits - 1))) != 0
    {
        return Err(MappingError::CrossesCanonicalHole);
    }

    Ok(ValidatedMappingRange {
        virtual_start,
        virtual_end,
        physical_start,
        physical_end,
        physical_address_bits,
        permissions,
    })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::{
        validate_mapping_range, MappingError, PagePermissions, ValidatedMappingRange, PAGE_SIZE,
    };

    const KERNEL_READ_ONLY: PagePermissions = PagePermissions {
        writable: false,
        executable: false,
        user_accessible: false,
    };

    #[test]
    fn accepts_aligned_ranges_at_canonical_boundaries() {
        let lower = validate_mapping_range(
            0x0000_7fff_ffff_e000,
            0x1000,
            0x2000,
            KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            Some(14),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            lower,
            ValidatedMappingRange {
                virtual_start: 0x0000_7fff_ffff_e000,
                virtual_end: 0x0000_8000_0000_0000,
                physical_start: 0x1000,
                physical_end: 0x3000,
                physical_address_bits: 14,
                permissions: KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            }
        );

        let upper = validate_mapping_range(
            0xffff_8000_0000_0000,
            0,
            PAGE_SIZE,
            KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            Some(12),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(upper.virtual_end(), 0xffff_8000_0000_1000);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_empty_and_unaligned_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x2000, 0, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(32), 48),
            Err(MappingError::EmptyRange)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1001, 0x2000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(32), 48),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                0x2000,
                PAGE_SIZE + 1,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(32),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn requires_known_valid_address_widths_and_bounded_physical_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, None, 48),
            Err(MappingError::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(0), 48),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(52), 0),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidVirtualAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                0x1000,
                PAGE_SIZE * 2,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(13),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_noncanonical_ranges_and_ranges_crossing_the_canonical_hole() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x0000_8000_0000_0000,
                0,
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(52),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::NonCanonicalVirtualRange)
        );

        let crosses_hole = (0xffff_8000_0000_1000u64) - 0x0000_7fff_ffff_f000;
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x0000_7fff_ffff_f000,
                0,
                crosses_hole,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                48,
            ),
            Err(MappingError::CrossesCanonicalHole)
        );
    }

    fn pat_value(entries: [u8; 8]) -> u64 {
        entries.iter().enumerate().fold(0, |value, (index, entry)| {
            value | ((*entry as u64) << (index * 8))
        })
    }

    #[test]
    fn resolves_cache_policies_from_the_supplied_pat_layout() {
        let pat = pat_value([6, 4, 7, 0, 6, 1, 7, 0]);
        let layout = super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(pat).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteBack), Some(0));
        assert_eq!(layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::Uncacheable), Some(3));
        assert_eq!(
            layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteCombining),
            Some(5)
        );

        let without_write_combining =
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(pat_value([6, 4, 7, 0, 6, 4, 7, 0])).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            without_write_combining.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteCombining),
            None
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_reserved_pat_entries() {
        assert_eq!(
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(0x08),
            Err(super::PatLayoutError::ReservedBits { index: 0 })
        );
        assert_eq!(
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(0b010 << 24),
            Err(super::PatLayoutError::ReservedMemoryType {
                index: 3,
                value: 0b010,
            })
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn encodes_4k_leaf_permissions_nx_and_pat_index_bits() {
        let writable_user_page = PagePermissions {
            writable: true,
            executable: false,
            user_accessible: true,
        };
        let mapping = validate_mapping_range(
            0x4000,
            0x20_0000,
            PAGE_SIZE * 2,
            writable_user_page,
            Some(52),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();

        let first = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0b101, true).unwrap().bits();
        assert_eq!(
            first,
            0x20_0000 | (1 << 0) | (1 << 1) | (1 << 2) | (1 << 3) | (1 << 7) | (1 << 63)
        );
        let second = mapping
            .leaf_4k_entry(PAGE_SIZE, 0b101, true)
            .unwrap()
            .bits();
        assert_eq!(second & 0x000f_ffff_ffff_f000, 0x20_1000);
        assert_eq!(second & (1 << 63), 1 << 63);

        let all_pat_bits = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0b111, true).unwrap().bits()
            & ((1 << 3) | (1 << 4) | (1 << 7));
        assert_eq!(all_pat_bits, (1 << 3) | (1 << 4) | (1 << 7));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_unencodable_leaf_attributes_and_page_offsets() {
        let mapping =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(52), 48)
                .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 8, true),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPatIndex)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, false),
            Err(MappingError::NxNotEnabled)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(1, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedPageOffset)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(PAGE_SIZE, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::PageOffsetOutOfRange)
        );

        let too_wide =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(53), 48)
                .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            too_wide.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn executable_leaf_does_not_set_nx() {
        let executable = PagePermissions {
            writable: false,
            executable: true,
            user_accessible: false,
        };
        let mapping =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, executable, Some(52), 48).unwrap();
        let entry = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, false).unwrap().bits();
        assert_eq!(entry, 0x20_0000 | 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_arithmetic_overflow_and_writable_executable_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                u64::MAX - (PAGE_SIZE - 1),
                0,
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                64
            ),
            Err(MappingError::AddressOverflow)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                u64::MAX - (PAGE_SIZE - 1),
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                64
            ),
            Err(MappingError::AddressOverflow)
        );

        let writable_executable = PagePermissions {
            writable: true,
            executable: true,
            user_accessible: false,
        };
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x2000, PAGE_SIZE, writable_executable, Some(52), 48),
            Err(MappingError::WritableExecutable)
        );
    }
}

//! CPU feature and gated, read-only MSR reporting for x86_64 boot diagnostics.

use core::arch::asm;
use core::arch::x86_64::{__cpuid, __cpuid_count};

const IA32_EFER: u32 = 0xc000_0080;
const IA32_PAT: u32 = 0x0000_0277;
const EFER_NXE: u64 = 1 << 11;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct CpuInfo {
    pub vendor: [u8; 12],
    pub max_basic_leaf: u32,
    pub max_extended_leaf: u32,
    pub has_sse2: bool,
    pub has_nx: bool,
    pub nx_enabled: bool,
    pub has_pat: bool,
    pub pat_msr_value: Option<u64>,
    pub has_1g_pages: bool,
    pub has_long_mode: bool,
    pub has_la57: bool,
    pub physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    pub max_linear_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    pub active_linear_address_bits: u8,
    pub pcid_enabled: bool,
}

/// Read CPUID identity, selected feature bits, and supported read-only MSR state.
pub fn identify() -> CpuInfo {
    // CPUID is part of the x86_64 architectural baseline.
    let basic = __cpuid(0);
    let mut vendor = [0; 12];
    vendor[0..4].copy_from_slice(&basic.ebx.to_le_bytes());
    vendor[4..8].copy_from_slice(&basic.edx.to_le_bytes());
    vendor[8..12].copy_from_slice(&basic.ecx.to_le_bytes());

    let basic_features = if basic.eax >= 1 {
        Some(__cpuid(1))
    } else {
        None
    };
    let extended = __cpuid(0x8000_0000);
    let extended_features = if extended.eax >= 0x8000_0001 {
        Some(__cpuid(0x8000_0001))
    } else {
        None
    };
    let structured_features = if basic.eax >= 7 {
        Some(__cpuid_count(7, 0))
    } else {
        None
    };
    let address_widths = if extended.eax >= 0x8000_0008 {
        Some(__cpuid(0x8000_0008).eax)
    } else {
        None
    };
    let cr4 = read_cr4_raw();
    let has_nx = extended_features.is_some_and(|features| features.edx & (1 << 20) != 0);
    let nx_enabled = if has_nx {
        // SAFETY: EFER is available in x86_64 long mode; CPUID confirms NX support,
        // and this boot-time code executes at CPL 0. The read does not change state.
        unsafe { read_msr(IA32_EFER) & EFER_NXE != 0 }
    } else {
        false
    };
    let has_pat = basic_features.is_some_and(|features| features.edx & (1 << 16) != 0);
    let pat_msr_value = if has_pat {
        // SAFETY: CPUID reports PAT support and boot-time kernel code runs at CPL 0.
        Some(unsafe { read_msr(IA32_PAT) })
    } else {
        None
    };

    CpuInfo {
        vendor,
        max_basic_leaf: basic.eax,
        max_extended_leaf: extended.eax,
        has_sse2: basic_features.is_some_and(|features| features.edx & (1 << 26) != 0),
        has_nx,
        nx_enabled,
        has_pat,
        pat_msr_value,
        has_1g_pages: extended_features.is_some_and(|features| features.edx & (1 << 26) != 0),
        has_long_mode: extended_features.is_some_and(|features| features.edx & (1 << 29) != 0),
        has_la57: structured_features.is_some_and(|features| features.ecx & (1 << 16) != 0),
        physical_address_bits: address_widths.map(|eax| (eax & 0xff) as u8),
        max_linear_address_bits: address_widths.map(|eax| ((eax >> 8) & 0xff) as u8),
        active_linear_address_bits: if cr4 & (1 << 12) != 0 { 57 } else { 48 },
        pcid_enabled: cr4 & (1 << 17) != 0,
    }
}

/// Read the current raw CR3 value without changing page-table state.
pub fn read_cr3_raw() -> u64 {
    let cr3: u64;
    // SAFETY: Rustix runs at CPL 0; reading CR3 is privileged but non-mutating.
    unsafe { asm!("mov {}, cr3", out(reg) cr3, options(nomem, nostack, preserves_flags)) };
    cr3
}

/// Read the active linear-address width from CR4.LA57 without changing paging.
/// Read a model-specific register at CPL 0.
///
/// # Safety
/// The caller must ensure the MSR exists on this CPU and that the current
/// privilege level permits the read.
unsafe fn read_msr(index: u32) -> u64 {
    let low: u32;
    let high: u32;
    // SAFETY: The caller guarantees that this MSR is supported and accessible.
    unsafe {
        asm!(
            "rdmsr",
            in("ecx") index,
            out("eax") low,
            out("edx") high,
            options(nomem, nostack)
        );
    }
    (u64::from(high) << 32) | u64::from(low)
}

pub fn active_linear_address_bits() -> u8 {
    if read_cr4_raw() & (1 << 12) != 0 {
        57
    } else {
        48
    }
}

fn read_cr4_raw() -> u64 {
    let cr4: u64;
    // SAFETY: Rustix runs at CPL 0; reading CR4 is privileged but non-mutating.
    unsafe { asm!("mov {}, cr4", out(reg) cr4, options(nomem, nostack, preserves_flags)) };
    cr4
}

/// Owned snapshot of bootloader data and CPU state consumed by the kernel.
pub struct BootInfo {
    pub hhdm_revision: u64,
    pub hhdm_offset: u64,
    pub inherited_cr3_raw: u64,
    pub inherited_cr3_root: Result<Cr3Root, Cr3Error>,
    pub executable_address_revision: u64,
    pub kernel_physical_base: u64,
    pub kernel_physical_end: u64,
    pub kernel_virtual_base: u64,
    pub kernel_virtual_end: u64,
    pub kernel_image_segments: KernelImageSegments,
    pub boot_stack_physical_start: u64,
    pub boot_stack_physical_end: u64,
    pub boot_stack_virtual_start: u64,
    pub boot_stack_virtual_end: u64,
    pub memory_map_revision: u64,
    pub memory_map: MemoryMap,
    pub frame_allocator: FrameAllocator,
    pub cpu_info: crate::arch::x86_64::cpu::CpuInfo,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum BootInfoError {
    MissingHhdmResponse,
    InvalidHhdmResponsePointer,
    InvalidHhdmOffset,
    MissingExecutableAddressResponse,
    InvalidExecutableAddressResponsePointer,
    InvalidKernelImageRange,
    InvalidKernelImageSegments,
    InvalidInitialStackBounds,
    InvalidInitialStackHhdmRange,
    InitialStackNotBootloaderReclaimable,
    MissingMemoryMapResponse,
    InvalidMemoryMapResponsePointer,
    MemoryMapCountDoesNotFit,
    TooManyMemoryRegions,
    EmptyMemoryMap,
    InvalidEntriesPointer,
    EntriesAddressOverflow,
    InvalidEntryPointer { index: usize },
    InvalidPhysicalRange { index: usize },
    InvalidHhdmMappingRange { index: usize },
    InvalidMemoryMap(MemoryMapError),
    FrameAllocator(FrameError),
}

/// Copy and validate the boot data needed by the current kernel.
///
/// Structural checks can reject malformed pointers and ranges, but cannot prove
/// that a loader-provided pointer is mapped and readable. That remains part of
/// the documented Limine handoff trust boundary until the kernel owns its maps.
pub fn capture_boot_information(entry_rsp: u64) -> Result<BootInfo, BootInfoError> {
    let cpu_info = crate::arch::x86_64::cpu::identify();
    let inherited_cr3_raw = crate::arch::x86_64::cpu::read_cr3_raw();
    let inherited_cr3_root = parse_cr3_root(
        inherited_cr3_raw,
        cpu_info.physical_address_bits,
        cpu_info.pcid_enabled,
    );
    let virtual_address_bits = cpu_info.active_linear_address_bits;
    let (boot_stack_virtual_start, boot_stack_virtual_end) =
        limine_x86_64_stack_bounds(entry_rsp, virtual_address_bits)
            .ok_or(BootInfoError::InvalidInitialStackBounds)?;
    let hhdm_request = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(HHDM_REQUEST);
    // SAFETY: Limine has completed writing the request response before `_start`.
    let hhdm_pointer =
        unsafe { core::ptr::read_volatile(core::ptr::addr_of!((*hhdm_request).response)) };
    if hhdm_pointer.is_null() {
        return Err(BootInfoError::MissingHhdmResponse);
    }
    if !is_valid_pointer(hhdm_pointer, virtual_address_bits) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidHhdmResponsePointer);
    }
    // SAFETY: The Limine response pointer is trusted to refer to a readable
    // response object; its nullness and alignment are checked above.
    let hhdm = unsafe { hhdm_pointer.read_volatile() };
    if hhdm.offset & 0xfff != 0 || !is_canonical_virtual_address(hhdm.offset, virtual_address_bits)
    {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidHhdmOffset);
    }

    let executable_address_request = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(EXECUTABLE_ADDRESS_REQUEST);
    // SAFETY: Limine has completed writing the request response before `_start`.
    let executable_address_pointer = unsafe {
        core::ptr::read_volatile(core::ptr::addr_of!((*executable_address_request).response))
    };
    if executable_address_pointer.is_null() {
        return Err(BootInfoError::MissingExecutableAddressResponse);
    }
    if !is_valid_pointer(executable_address_pointer, virtual_address_bits) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidExecutableAddressResponsePointer);
    }
    // SAFETY: Limine's response pointer is trusted to refer to a readable object;
    // nullness, alignment, and active-width canonicality were checked above.
    let executable_address = unsafe { executable_address_pointer.read_volatile() };
    // `linker.ld` defines these symbols as the boundaries of the loaded image.
    let (linked_virtual_start, linked_virtual_end) = (
        core::ptr::addr_of!(__kernel_start) as u64,
        core::ptr::addr_of!(__kernel_end) as u64,
    );
    let kernel_physical_end = kernel_image_physical_end(
        executable_address.physical_base,
        executable_address.virtual_base,
        linked_virtual_start,
        linked_virtual_end,
        cpu_info.physical_address_bits,
        virtual_address_bits,
    )
    .ok_or(BootInfoError::InvalidKernelImageRange)?;
    let linked_segments = KernelImageVirtualSegments {
        text: (
            core::ptr::addr_of!(__kernel_text_mapping_start) as u64,
            core::ptr::addr_of!(__kernel_text_mapping_end) as u64,
        ),
        rodata: (
            core::ptr::addr_of!(__kernel_rodata_mapping_start) as u64,
            core::ptr::addr_of!(__kernel_rodata_mapping_end) as u64,
        ),
        data: (
            core::ptr::addr_of!(__kernel_data_mapping_start) as u64,
            core::ptr::addr_of!(__kernel_data_mapping_end) as u64,
        ),
    };
    let kernel_image_segments = kernel_image_segments(
        executable_address.physical_base,
        executable_address.virtual_base,
        linked_virtual_start,
        linked_virtual_end,
        linked_segments,
        cpu_info.physical_address_bits,
        virtual_address_bits,
    )
    .ok_or(BootInfoError::InvalidKernelImageSegments)?;

    let memory_map_request = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(MEMORY_MAP_REQUEST);
    // SAFETY: Limine has completed writing the request response before `_start`.
    let memory_map_pointer =
        unsafe { core::ptr::read_volatile(core::ptr::addr_of!((*memory_map_request).response)) };
    if memory_map_pointer.is_null() {
        return Err(BootInfoError::MissingMemoryMapResponse);
    }
    if !is_valid_pointer(memory_map_pointer, virtual_address_bits) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidMemoryMapResponsePointer);
    }
    // SAFETY: The Limine response pointer is trusted to refer to a readable
    // response object; its nullness and alignment are checked above.
    let response = unsafe { memory_map_pointer.read_volatile() };
    let entry_count = usize::try_from(response.entry_count)
        .map_err(|_| BootInfoError::MemoryMapCountDoesNotFit)?;
    if entry_count > MAX_MEMORY_REGIONS {
        return Err(BootInfoError::TooManyMemoryRegions);
    }
    if entry_count == 0 {
        return Err(BootInfoError::EmptyMemoryMap);
    }
    if response.entries.is_null() {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidEntriesPointer);
    }
    if !is_valid_pointer(response.entries, virtual_address_bits) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidEntriesPointer);
    }
    let entry_bytes = entry_count
        .checked_mul(size_of::<*const MemoryMapEntry>())
        .ok_or(BootInfoError::EntriesAddressOverflow)?;
    let entries_start = response.entries as usize;
    let entries_end = entries_start
        .checked_add(entry_bytes)
        .ok_or(BootInfoError::EntriesAddressOverflow)?;
    if !is_canonical_virtual_address(entries_start as u64, virtual_address_bits)
        || !is_canonical_virtual_address((entries_end - 1) as u64, virtual_address_bits)
    {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidEntriesPointer);
    }

    let mut builder = MemoryMapBuilder::new();
    for index in 0..entry_count {
        // SAFETY: Limine promises an array of `entry_count` pointers. The count
        // is capped and pointer alignment/address arithmetic are checked above.
        let entry_pointer = unsafe { response.entries.add(index).read_volatile() };
        if !is_valid_pointer(entry_pointer, virtual_address_bits) {
            return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidEntryPointer { index });
        }
        // SAFETY: Limine promises each non-null entry pointer is readable.
        let entry = unsafe { entry_pointer.read_volatile() };
        builder
            .push(entry.base, entry.length, entry.kind)
            .map_err(BootInfoError::InvalidMemoryMap)?;
    }

    let memory_map = builder.finish().map_err(|error| match error {
        MemoryMapError::EmptyMap => BootInfoError::EmptyMemoryMap,
        other => BootInfoError::InvalidMemoryMap(other),
    })?;
    for (index, region) in memory_map.regions().iter().enumerate() {
        if let Some(physical_bits) = cpu_info.physical_address_bits {
            if !physical_range_fits_address_width(region.start(), region.end(), physical_bits) {
                return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidPhysicalRange { index });
            }
        }
        if region.kind().is_mapped_by_limine_v3_hhdm()
            && !limine_v3_hhdm_range_is_valid(
                hhdm.offset,
                region.start(),
                region.end(),
                cpu_info.physical_address_bits,
                virtual_address_bits,
            )
        {
            return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidHhdmMappingRange { index });
        }
    }
    let boot_stack_physical_start = boot_stack_virtual_start
        .checked_sub(hhdm.offset)
        .ok_or(BootInfoError::InvalidInitialStackHhdmRange)?;
    let boot_stack_physical_end = boot_stack_virtual_end
        .checked_sub(hhdm.offset)
        .ok_or(BootInfoError::InvalidInitialStackHhdmRange)?;
    if !limine_v3_hhdm_range_is_valid(
        hhdm.offset,
        boot_stack_physical_start,
        boot_stack_physical_end,
        cpu_info.physical_address_bits,
        virtual_address_bits,
    ) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidInitialStackHhdmRange);
    }
    if !memory_map.range_is_within_region_kind(
        boot_stack_physical_start,
        boot_stack_physical_end,
        RegionKind::BootloaderReclaimable,
    ) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InitialStackNotBootloaderReclaimable);
    }
    let frame_allocator =
        FrameAllocator::from_memory_map(&memory_map).map_err(BootInfoError::FrameAllocator)?;

    Ok(BootInfo {
        hhdm_revision: hhdm.revision,
        hhdm_offset: hhdm.offset,
        inherited_cr3_raw,
        inherited_cr3_root,
        executable_address_revision: executable_address.revision,
        kernel_physical_base: executable_address.physical_base,
        kernel_physical_end,
        kernel_virtual_base: executable_address.virtual_base,
        kernel_virtual_end: linked_virtual_end,
        kernel_image_segments,
        boot_stack_physical_start,
        boot_stack_physical_end,
        boot_stack_virtual_start,
        boot_stack_virtual_end,
        memory_map_revision: response.revision,
        memory_map,
        frame_allocator,
        cpu_info,
    })
}

/// Print a report from the copied snapshot; no loader pointers are used here.
pub fn report_boot_information(boot_info: &BootInfo) {
    crate::kprintln!(
        "HHDM: offset {:#018x}, response revision {}",
        boot_info.hhdm_offset,
        boot_info.hhdm_revision
    );
    crate::kprintln!(
        "inherited CR3: raw {:#018x} (page-table contents not inspected)",
        boot_info.inherited_cr3_raw
    );
    match boot_info.inherited_cr3_root {
        Ok(root) => crate::kprintln!(
            "inherited CR3 root: physical {:#018x}, low bits {:?}",
            root.physical_address(),
            root.low_bits()
        ),
        Err(error) => crate::kprintln!("inherited CR3 root could not be decoded: {:?}", error),
    }
    crate::kprintln!(
        "kernel image: physical [{:#018x}, {:#018x}), virtual [{:#018x}, {:#018x}), response revision {}",
        boot_info.kernel_physical_base,
        boot_info.kernel_physical_end,
        boot_info.kernel_virtual_base,
        boot_info.kernel_virtual_end,
        boot_info.executable_address_revision
    );
    let segments = &boot_info.kernel_image_segments;
    crate::kprintln!(
        "kernel mapping text RX: physical [{:#018x}, {:#018x}), virtual [{:#018x}, {:#018x})",
        segments.text.physical_start,
        segments.text.physical_end,
        segments.text.virtual_start,
        segments.text.virtual_end
    );
    crate::kprintln!(
        "kernel mapping rodata R/NX: physical [{:#018x}, {:#018x}), virtual [{:#018x}, {:#018x})",
        segments.rodata.physical_start,
        segments.rodata.physical_end,
        segments.rodata.virtual_start,
        segments.rodata.virtual_end
    );
    crate::kprintln!(
        "kernel mapping data RW/NX: physical [{:#018x}, {:#018x}), virtual [{:#018x}, {:#018x})",
        segments.data.physical_start,
        segments.data.physical_end,
        segments.data.virtual_start,
        segments.data.virtual_end
    );
    crate::kprintln!(
        "boot stack (minimum protocol span): physical [{:#018x}, {:#018x}), virtual [{:#018x}, {:#018x})",
        boot_info.boot_stack_physical_start,
        boot_info.boot_stack_physical_end,
        boot_info.boot_stack_virtual_start,
        boot_info.boot_stack_virtual_end
    );
    crate::kprintln!(
        "memory map: {} validated regions, {} MiB usable RAM, response revision {}",
        boot_info.memory_map.regions().len(),
        boot_info.memory_map.usable_bytes() / (1024 * 1024),
        boot_info.memory_map_revision
    );
    crate::kprintln!(
        "physical frames: {} managed, {} free (4 KiB each)",
        boot_info.frame_allocator.managed_frames(),
        boot_info.frame_allocator.free_frames()
    );
    for region in boot_info.memory_map.regions() {
        crate::kprintln!(
            "  [{:#018x}, {:#018x}) {:?}",
            region.start(),
            region.end(),
            region.kind()
        );
    }
    crate::kprintln!("console: COM1 serial (polling)");

    let cpu = &boot_info.cpu_info;
    let vendor = core::str::from_utf8(&cpu.vendor).unwrap_or("unknown");
    crate::kprintln!(
        "CPU: {} (basic leaf {}, extended leaf {:#x})",
        vendor,
        cpu.max_basic_leaf,
        cpu.max_extended_leaf
    );
    crate::kprintln!(
        "CPU feature support: SSE2={}, NX={}, NXE-enabled={}, PAT={}, 1GiB-pages={}, long-mode={}, LA57-supported={}, PCID-enabled={}",
        cpu.has_sse2,
        cpu.has_nx,
        cpu.nx_enabled,
        cpu.has_pat,
        cpu.has_1g_pages,
        cpu.has_long_mode,
        cpu.has_la57,
        cpu.pcid_enabled
    );
    match cpu.pat_msr_value {
        Some(value) => match PatLayout::from_msr_value(value) {
            Ok(layout) => crate::kprintln!(
                "PAT: IA32_PAT={:#018x}, WB index={:?}, UC index={:?}, WC index={:?}",
                value,
                layout.index_for(CachePolicy::WriteBack),
                layout.index_for(CachePolicy::Uncacheable),
                layout.index_for(CachePolicy::WriteCombining)
            ),
            Err(error) => crate::kprintln!(
                "PAT: IA32_PAT={:#018x} has unsupported layout: {:?}",
                value,
                error
            ),
        },
        None => crate::kprintln!("PAT: unsupported"),
    }
    crate::kprintln!(
        "CPU address widths (bits): physical={:?}, max-linear={:?}, active-linear={} (LA57 active={})",
        cpu.physical_address_bits,
        cpu.max_linear_address_bits,
        cpu.active_linear_address_bits,
        cpu.active_linear_address_bits == 57
    );
}

fn is_valid_pointer<T>(pointer: *const T, virtual_address_bits: u8) -> bool {
    let start = pointer as usize;
    let Some(end) = start.checked_add(size_of::<T>()) else {
        return false;
    };
    !pointer.is_null()
        && start & (align_of::<T>() - 1) == 0
        && is_canonical_virtual_address(start as u64, virtual_address_bits)
        && is_canonical_virtual_address((end - 1) as u64, virtual_address_bits)
}

//! Pure validation for future x86_64 4 KiB mappings.
//!
//! This module validates address shape and permissions only. It does not prove
//! frame ownership or mapping coverage, access physical memory, or modify tables.

use crate::memory::{is_canonical_virtual_address, physical_range_fits_address_width};

/// Base page size supported by the initial Rustix mapping policy.
pub const PAGE_SIZE: u64 = 4096;

/// The physical root address and interpretation of CR3's low bits.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Cr3Root {
    physical_address: u64,
    low_bits: Cr3LowBits,
}

impl Cr3Root {
    pub const fn physical_address(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_address
    }

    pub const fn low_bits(self) -> Cr3LowBits {
        self.low_bits
    }
}

/// CR3's low bits are either a PCID or the paging-structure cache controls.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Cr3LowBits {
    Pcid(u16),
    CacheControls {
        page_level_write_through: bool,
        page_level_cache_disable: bool,
    },
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Cr3Error {
    UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth,
    InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth,
    ReservedBits,
}

/// Decode the currently active CR3 value without dereferencing its root table.
///
/// The caller supplies the reported physical-address width and whether CR4.PCIDE
/// is active, since those determine the valid address and low-bit encodings.
pub fn parse_cr3_root(
    raw: u64,
    physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    pcid_enabled: bool,
) -> Result<Cr3Root, Cr3Error> {
    let physical_address_bits =
        physical_address_bits.ok_or(Cr3Error::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)?;
    if !(12..=MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS).contains(&physical_address_bits) {
        return Err(Cr3Error::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
    }

    let physical_mask = ((1u64 << physical_address_bits) - 1) & !(PAGE_SIZE - 1);
    let low_mask = if pcid_enabled {
        PAGE_SIZE - 1
    } else {
        (1 << 3) | (1 << 4)
    };
    if raw & !(physical_mask | low_mask) != 0 {
        return Err(Cr3Error::ReservedBits);
    }

    let low_bits = if pcid_enabled {
        Cr3LowBits::Pcid((raw & (PAGE_SIZE - 1)) as u16)
    } else {
        Cr3LowBits::CacheControls {
            page_level_write_through: raw & (1 << 3) != 0,
            page_level_cache_disable: raw & (1 << 4) != 0,
        }
    };

    Ok(Cr3Root {
        physical_address: raw & physical_mask,
        low_bits,
    })
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PagePermissions {
    pub writable: bool,
    pub executable: bool,
    pub user_accessible: bool,
}

/// A mapping range whose arithmetic, address widths, alignment, and W^X policy passed validation.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ValidatedMappingRange {
    virtual_start: u64,
    virtual_end: u64,
    physical_start: u64,
    physical_end: u64,
    physical_address_bits: u8,
    permissions: PagePermissions,
}

impl ValidatedMappingRange {
    pub const fn virtual_start(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_start
    }

    pub const fn virtual_end(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_end
    }

    pub const fn physical_start(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_start
    }

    pub const fn physical_end(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_end
    }

    pub const fn permissions(self) -> PagePermissions {
        self.permissions
    }

    pub const fn length(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_end - self.virtual_start
    }

    /// Encode a single 4 KiB leaf PTE within this validated range.
    ///
    /// `pat_index` is the raw three-bit PAT index; the caller must have verified
    /// that the active PAT MSR gives it the intended cache semantics. `nx_enabled`
    /// must reflect EFER.NXE, not merely CPUID support. This only creates entry
    /// bits—it neither owns the frame nor writes a page table.
    pub fn leaf_4k_entry(
        self,
        page_offset: u64,
        pat_index: u8,
        nx_enabled: bool,
    ) -> Result<PageTableEntry, MappingError> {
        if page_offset & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
            return Err(MappingError::UnalignedPageOffset);
        }
        if page_offset >= self.length() {
            return Err(MappingError::PageOffsetOutOfRange);
        }
        if self.physical_address_bits > MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS {
            return Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
        }
        if pat_index > 7 {
            return Err(MappingError::InvalidPatIndex);
        }
        if !self.permissions.executable && !nx_enabled {
            return Err(MappingError::NxNotEnabled);
        }

        let physical_address = self
            .physical_start
            .checked_add(page_offset)
            .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
        let mut bits = physical_address | PTE_PRESENT;
        if self.permissions.writable {
            bits |= PTE_WRITABLE;
        }
        if self.permissions.user_accessible {
            bits |= PTE_USER;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b001 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_WRITE_THROUGH;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b010 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_CACHE_DISABLE;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b100 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_PAT_4K;
        }
        if !self.permissions.executable {
            bits |= PTE_NO_EXECUTE;
        }

        Ok(PageTableEntry(bits))
    }
}

/// Maximum x86_64 physical-address width representable by a 4 KiB PTE.
pub const MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS: u8 = 52;
const PTE_PRESENT: u64 = 1 << 0;
const PTE_WRITABLE: u64 = 1 << 1;
const PTE_USER: u64 = 1 << 2;
const PTE_WRITE_THROUGH: u64 = 1 << 3;
const PTE_CACHE_DISABLE: u64 = 1 << 4;
const PTE_PAT_4K: u64 = 1 << 7;
const PTE_NO_EXECUTE: u64 = 1 << 63;

/// Raw bits for one x86_64 4 KiB leaf page-table entry.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PageTableEntry(u64);

impl PageTableEntry {
    pub const fn bits(self) -> u64 {
        self.0
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum CachePolicy {
    WriteBack,
    Uncacheable,
    WriteCombining,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum PatLayoutError {
    ReservedBits { index: u8 },
    ReservedMemoryType { index: u8, value: u8 },
}

/// Parsed PAT memory types from an `IA32_PAT` MSR value supplied by the caller.
///
/// Parsing does not read the MSR or establish that this is the active value. The
/// MTRR configuration may also affect the effective cache type for an address.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PatLayout {
    memory_types: [u8; 8],
}

impl PatLayout {
    pub fn from_msr_value(value: u64) -> Result<Self, PatLayoutError> {
        let mut memory_types = [0; 8];
        for (index, memory_type) in memory_types.iter_mut().enumerate() {
            let entry = ((value >> (index * 8)) & 0xff) as u8;
            if entry & !0x07 != 0 {
                return Err(PatLayoutError::ReservedBits { index: index as u8 });
            }
            if entry == 0b010 || entry == 0b011 {
                return Err(PatLayoutError::ReservedMemoryType {
                    index: index as u8,
                    value: entry,
                });
            }
            *memory_type = entry;
        }
        Ok(Self { memory_types })
    }

    /// Return the first PAT index with the exact requested memory type.
    pub fn index_for(self, policy: CachePolicy) -> Option<u8> {
        let requested_type = match policy {
            CachePolicy::Uncacheable => 0b000,
            CachePolicy::WriteCombining => 0b001,
            CachePolicy::WriteBack => 0b110,
        };
        self.memory_types
            .iter()
            .position(|memory_type| *memory_type == requested_type)
            .map(|index| index as u8)
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum MappingError {
    EmptyRange,
    UnalignedRange,
    AddressOverflow,
    InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth,
    UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth,
    InvalidVirtualAddressWidth,
    PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds,
    NonCanonicalVirtualRange,
    CrossesCanonicalHole,
    WritableExecutable,
    UnalignedPageOffset,
    PageOffsetOutOfRange,
    InvalidPatIndex,
    NxNotEnabled,
}

/// Validate one contiguous range for a future 4 KiB page-mapping operation.
///
/// The physical-address width must be known; passing `None` is rejected rather
/// than treating an unknown bound as unbounded. Successful validation does not
/// establish that physical frames are owned by a caller or mapped in the HHDM.
pub fn validate_mapping_range(
    virtual_start: u64,
    physical_start: u64,
    length: u64,
    permissions: PagePermissions,
    physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    virtual_address_bits: u8,
) -> Result<ValidatedMappingRange, MappingError> {
    if virtual_address_bits == 0 || virtual_address_bits > 64 {
        return Err(MappingError::InvalidVirtualAddressWidth);
    }
    let physical_address_bits =
        physical_address_bits.ok_or(MappingError::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)?;
    if physical_address_bits == 0 || physical_address_bits > 64 {
        return Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
    }
    if length == 0 {
        return Err(MappingError::EmptyRange);
    }
    if (virtual_start | physical_start | length) & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
        return Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange);
    }
    if permissions.writable && permissions.executable {
        return Err(MappingError::WritableExecutable);
    }

    let virtual_end = virtual_start
        .checked_add(length)
        .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
    let physical_end = physical_start
        .checked_add(length)
        .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
    if !physical_range_fits_address_width(physical_start, physical_end, physical_address_bits) {
        return Err(MappingError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds);
    }

    let last_virtual_address = virtual_end - 1;
    if !is_canonical_virtual_address(virtual_start, virtual_address_bits)
        || !is_canonical_virtual_address(last_virtual_address, virtual_address_bits)
    {
        return Err(MappingError::NonCanonicalVirtualRange);
    }
    if virtual_address_bits < 64
        && ((virtual_start ^ last_virtual_address) & (1u64 << (virtual_address_bits - 1))) != 0
    {
        return Err(MappingError::CrossesCanonicalHole);
    }

    Ok(ValidatedMappingRange {
        virtual_start,
        virtual_end,
        physical_start,
        physical_end,
        physical_address_bits,
        permissions,
    })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::{
        parse_cr3_root, validate_mapping_range, Cr3Error, Cr3LowBits, MappingError,
        PagePermissions, ValidatedMappingRange, PAGE_SIZE,
    };

    const KERNEL_READ_ONLY: PagePermissions = PagePermissions {
        writable: false,
        executable: false,
        user_accessible: false,
    };

    #[test]
    fn decodes_cr3_root_with_pcid_enabled() {
        let root = parse_cr3_root(0x1234_5000 | 0x0abc, Some(48), true).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(root.physical_address(), 0x1234_5000);
        assert_eq!(root.low_bits(), Cr3LowBits::Pcid(0x0abc));
    }

    #[test]
    fn decodes_cr3_cache_controls_without_pcid() {
        let root = parse_cr3_root(0x1234_5000 | (1 << 3) | (1 << 4), Some(48), false).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(root.physical_address(), 0x1234_5000);
        assert_eq!(
            root.low_bits(),
            Cr3LowBits::CacheControls {
                page_level_write_through: true,
                page_level_cache_disable: true,
            }
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_cr3_reserved_bits_and_invalid_address_widths() {
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(0x1000 | 1, Some(48), false),
            Err(Cr3Error::ReservedBits)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(1 << 40, Some(40), true),
            Err(Cr3Error::ReservedBits)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(0x1000, None, false),
            Err(Cr3Error::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(0x1000, Some(53), false),
            Err(Cr3Error::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn accepts_aligned_ranges_at_canonical_boundaries() {
        let lower = validate_mapping_range(
            0x0000_7fff_ffff_e000,
            0x1000,
            0x2000,
            KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            Some(14),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            lower,
            ValidatedMappingRange {
                virtual_start: 0x0000_7fff_ffff_e000,
                virtual_end: 0x0000_8000_0000_0000,
                physical_start: 0x1000,
                physical_end: 0x3000,
                physical_address_bits: 14,
                permissions: KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            }
        );

        let upper = validate_mapping_range(
            0xffff_8000_0000_0000,
            0,
            PAGE_SIZE,
            KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            Some(12),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(upper.virtual_end(), 0xffff_8000_0000_1000);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_empty_and_unaligned_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x2000, 0, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(32), 48),
            Err(MappingError::EmptyRange)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1001, 0x2000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(32), 48),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                0x2000,
                PAGE_SIZE + 1,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(32),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn requires_known_valid_address_widths_and_bounded_physical_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, None, 48),
            Err(MappingError::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(0), 48),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(52), 0),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidVirtualAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                0x1000,
                PAGE_SIZE * 2,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(13),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_noncanonical_ranges_and_ranges_crossing_the_canonical_hole() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x0000_8000_0000_0000,
                0,
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(52),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::NonCanonicalVirtualRange)
        );

        let crosses_hole = (0xffff_8000_0000_1000u64) - 0x0000_7fff_ffff_f000;
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x0000_7fff_ffff_f000,
                0,
                crosses_hole,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                48,
            ),
            Err(MappingError::CrossesCanonicalHole)
        );
    }

    fn pat_value(entries: [u8; 8]) -> u64 {
        entries.iter().enumerate().fold(0, |value, (index, entry)| {
            value | ((*entry as u64) << (index * 8))
        })
    }

    #[test]
    fn resolves_cache_policies_from_the_supplied_pat_layout() {
        let pat = pat_value([6, 4, 7, 0, 6, 1, 7, 0]);
        let layout = super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(pat).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteBack), Some(0));
        assert_eq!(layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::Uncacheable), Some(3));
        assert_eq!(
            layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteCombining),
            Some(5)
        );

        let without_write_combining =
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(pat_value([6, 4, 7, 0, 6, 4, 7, 0])).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            without_write_combining.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteCombining),
            None
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_reserved_pat_entries() {
        assert_eq!(
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(0x08),
            Err(super::PatLayoutError::ReservedBits { index: 0 })
        );
        assert_eq!(
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(0b010 << 24),
            Err(super::PatLayoutError::ReservedMemoryType {
                index: 3,
                value: 0b010,
            })
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn encodes_4k_leaf_permissions_nx_and_pat_index_bits() {
        let writable_user_page = PagePermissions {
            writable: true,
            executable: false,
            user_accessible: true,
        };
        let mapping = validate_mapping_range(
            0x4000,
            0x20_0000,
            PAGE_SIZE * 2,
            writable_user_page,
            Some(52),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();

        let first = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0b101, true).unwrap().bits();
        assert_eq!(
            first,
            0x20_0000 | (1 << 0) | (1 << 1) | (1 << 2) | (1 << 3) | (1 << 7) | (1 << 63)
        );
        let second = mapping
            .leaf_4k_entry(PAGE_SIZE, 0b101, true)
            .unwrap()
            .bits();
        assert_eq!(second & 0x000f_ffff_ffff_f000, 0x20_1000);
        assert_eq!(second & (1 << 63), 1 << 63);

        let all_pat_bits = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0b111, true).unwrap().bits()
            & ((1 << 3) | (1 << 4) | (1 << 7));
        assert_eq!(all_pat_bits, (1 << 3) | (1 << 4) | (1 << 7));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_unencodable_leaf_attributes_and_page_offsets() {
        let mapping =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(52), 48)
                .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 8, true),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPatIndex)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, false),
            Err(MappingError::NxNotEnabled)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(1, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedPageOffset)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(PAGE_SIZE, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::PageOffsetOutOfRange)
        );

        let too_wide =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(53), 48)
                .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            too_wide.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn executable_leaf_does_not_set_nx() {
        let executable = PagePermissions {
            writable: false,
            executable: true,
            user_accessible: false,
        };
        let mapping =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, executable, Some(52), 48).unwrap();
        let entry = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, false).unwrap().bits();
        assert_eq!(entry, 0x20_0000 | 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_arithmetic_overflow_and_writable_executable_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                u64::MAX - (PAGE_SIZE - 1),
                0,
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                64
            ),
            Err(MappingError::AddressOverflow)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                u64::MAX - (PAGE_SIZE - 1),
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                64
            ),
            Err(MappingError::AddressOverflow)
        );

        let writable_executable = PagePermissions {
            writable: true,
            executable: true,
            user_accessible: false,
        };
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x2000, PAGE_SIZE, writable_executable, Some(52), 48),
            Err(MappingError::WritableExecutable)
        );
    }
}

`src/paging.rs` adds pure, host-tested 4 KiB mapping groundwork. Its range validator requires a known physical-address width, checked aligned bounds, canonical virtual endpoints in one canonical half, and rejects writable-executable permissions. A validated range can encode the raw bits of one 4 KiB leaf PTE, including supervisor/user and write permissions, NX when the caller confirms EFER.NXE, and a bounded three-bit PAT index. `PatLayout` parses a supplied `IA32_PAT` value and selects WB, UC, or WC indices. `src/arch/x86_64/cpu.rs` now captures EFER.NXE only when NX is supported and reads `IA32_PAT` only when PAT is supported; these reads are diagnostic and do not modify CPU state. The kernel does not yet verify MTRR interactions or use the resulting cache indices in mappings. None of these helpers proves frame ownership or HHDM coverage, restricts memory types, or touches hardware page tables. No page mapping operation or CR3 transition is implemented.

```

### # Memory-management direction › ## What exists now › L15-16

The boot snapshot now rejects physical map ranges beyond the reported CPUID physical-address width when that CPUID leaf is available. For base revision 3 HHDM-mapped types, it also validates the page-rounded physical span, checked `offset + address` arithmetic, and canonical endpoints in the active linear-address width. If the CPU does not report a physical width, the physical-bound check is skipped and no mapping caller may treat the bound as known. These checks are derived from metadata and Limine's protocol contract; they do not inspect page tables or prove that a non-null loader pointer is readable. Rustix still does not dereference physical memory through the HHDM. Rustix-owned mappings should be non-executable by default; executable kernel text must be explicit. MMIO requires an explicit device range and cache policy, and guard regions must remain unmapped. A page-table transition must preserve the kernel image, active stacks, boot data, descriptor tables, and any loader mappings still needed. Rustix does not reclaim bootloader memory, own or replace the inherited Limine page tables, or provide a heap. Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT setup and fatal exception diagnostics live in `src/arch/x86_64/interrupts.rs`; they do not enable maskable IRQs.

- **Kernel ELF image:** `linker.ld` places `.text`, `.rodata`, `.limine_requests`, `.data`, `.bss`, and `.got` in three `PT_LOAD` segments: RX text, read-only rodata, and RW data/BSS. It exports `__kernel_start` and `__kernel_end` around the complete loadable image, plus page-aligned bounds for each segment. `BootInfo` checks that Limine's reported virtual base matches the linker start, validates ordered contiguous segment bounds covering the rounded image, derives corresponding half-open physical ranges from the executable physical base with checked arithmetic, and validates canonical virtual and reported physical address widths. The release ELF and QEMU runtime report both showed the three aligned ranges on 2026-10-21. The RX, R/NX, and RW/NX labels describe the linker segment intent; these are linker/boot metadata, not proof that the inherited page tables map every page or enforce those permissions, nor proof that loader pointers are readable. The memory-map `ExecutableAndModules` entries remain illustrative, not an authoritative kernel-image extent.
- **Active stack and `BootInfo`:** The assembly `_start` shim captures Limine's entry `RSP` before any Rust prologue. Under the SysV entry convention the synthetic return slot occupies 8 bytes, so `RSP + 8` is the exclusive stack top. The protocol guarantees at least 64 KiB (without a Stack Size request); `BootInfo` records that minimum virtual span, translates it through the HHDM, and requires it to fit within a single `BootloaderReclaimable` physical region. The full loader allocation may be larger, and neither its exact bounds nor a guard page are known. Preserve the inherited mapping; do not infer a guard page.

Any pre-switch failure must discard the candidate root and return its frames without changing the active root. The repository contains a bare-metal `no_std` Rust kernel for `x86_64-unknown-none`. After Limine handoff it validates and copies memory-map metadata into an owned snapshot, captures the executable's physical and virtual bases, complete linker-defined image span, and page-aligned text/rodata/data segment bounds, reports baseline CPUID features, active/maximum address widths, and gated read-only EFER.NXE/IA32_PAT values, seeds a volatile RAM filesystem, and runs Crabshell over the COM1 serial console. Boot-protocol pointers are checked for canonicality using the active 4- or 5-level paging width; physical map bounds and protocol-scoped HHDM range arithmetic are also validated. Crabshell's allocation-free parser is a separate crate; the kernel handles command execution. On 2026-10-16, `just verify` passed in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 both in the working tree and from a clean detached worktree with an empty `target` directory; the clean run downloaded and built the pinned Limine host utility, created the image, and passed the bounded QEMU boot to the shell prompt. The serial log showed memory-map and CPU reporting. Hosted CI has not been observed. A separate interactive QEMU session exercised `help`, `ls`, `meminfo`, directory navigation, and file create/write/append/read/copy/move/remove commands. Milestone 0's local exit criteria are met, and Milestone 1's validation criteria are met for the current Limine path. This is a kernel-mode shell, not a user process. A bounded physical-frame allocator core is initialized from the validated map and reported by `meminfo`; its owned frame token and failed-deallocation semantics are documented and host-tested, but no kernel service allocates or maps frames yet. The Limine base-revision 3 HHDM scope and a conservative initial mapping/alias/cache policy are documented. `BootInfo` records the raw inherited CR3 value, but Rustix does not use the HHDM to access physical memory, inspect page-table contents, or own/replace the inherited tables. The complete linker-defined kernel-image span and the protocol-guaranteed minimum active boot-stack span are captured; the loader's exact stack allocation/guard bounds remain unresolved. There is not yet a heap, a general IRQ subsystem, a scheduler, process isolation, a persistent filesystem, or a Unix compatibility layer. Fatal CPU exceptions have basic GDT/TSS/IDT diagnostics but no recovery. `just fault-smoke` validates a controlled page fault (including CR2) and invalid opcode in QEMU; broader exception coverage and physical-hardware validation remain pending.

```
