# Memory-management direction

## What exists now

`src/memory.rs` contains an allocation-free `MemoryMapBuilder` that copies Limine ranges into validated half-open physical regions. It rejects empty ranges, `base + length` overflow, overlapping ranges, empty maps, and maps exceeding the fixed 256-region capacity. Known Limine types remain distinct, and unknown type values are preserved. Host-side tests cover range validation/classification, canonical-address boundaries, physical-width bounds, and HHDM range arithmetic. `src/boot.rs` stores the resulting map in an owned boot snapshot and reports usable bytes.

`src/frame.rs` provides a fixed-capacity allocator core with host-side tests. It only manages complete 4 KiB frames inside validated `Usable` regions, reserves the first MiB, and coalesces adjacent free ranges. The ownership contract assumes one allocator instance per physical map: within that instance, each eligible frame is owned either by its free-range table or by a non-copyable `Frame` token while allocated. The public constructor does not enforce unique instances, so callers must not initialize multiple allocators for the same physical map; the current boot path retains one allocator in `BootInfo`. `allocate` returns `None` when no free frame remains. `deallocate` consumes the token only on success; on any error, including free-range-table exhaustion, it returns a `DeallocationError` containing the original token and leaves allocator state unchanged. The range tables each have a fixed `MAX_FRAME_RANGES` capacity, so fragmentation can prevent a frame from being returned until nearby ranges coalesce. No kernel service currently allocates frames for mappings or other work.

`Frame::address()` is a physical address value, not a dereferenceable pointer and not a proof that the frame is mapped. CPU diagnostics now read `CR4.LA57` to report the active linear-address width (48 or 57 bits) and query `CPUID.80000008H` for physical and maximum linear-address widths when available. Boot-protocol pointers are checked against the active width, with host tests for 48- and 57-bit canonical boundaries. These facts do not prove that any physical address is mapped.

`src/paging.rs` adds pure, host-tested 4 KiB mapping groundwork and decodes the inherited CR3 root address plus its low-bit interpretation. The CR3 decoder validates the physical-address width and reserved bits and interprets the low 12 bits according to the active `CR4.PCIDE` state; it does not dereference the root. The mapping-range validator requires a known physical-address width, checked aligned bounds, canonical virtual endpoints in one canonical half, and rejects writable-executable permissions. A validated range can encode the raw bits of one 4 KiB leaf PTE, including supervisor/user and write permissions, NX when the caller confirms EFER.NXE, and a bounded three-bit PAT index. `PatLayout` parses a supplied `IA32_PAT` value and selects WB, UC, or WC indices. `src/arch/x86_64/cpu.rs` captures active PCID state, reads EFER.NXE only when NX is supported, and reads `IA32_PAT` only when PAT is supported; these reads are diagnostic and do not modify CPU state. The kernel does not yet verify MTRR interactions or use the resulting cache indices in mappings. None of these helpers proves frame ownership or HHDM coverage, restricts memory types, inspects table entries, or modifies hardware page tables. No page mapping operation or CR3 transition is implemented.

The requested Limine base revision is 3. Its protocol maps only `Usable`, `BootloaderReclaimable`, `ExecutableAndModules`, and `Framebuffer` memory-map regions into the HHDM; reserved, ACPI, bad, and unknown regions are not in that mapping set. HHDM mappings use at least 4 KiB granularity and may cover page-aligned bytes beyond unaligned region boundaries. On x86_64, inherited HHDM mappings are supervisor RWX, normally write-back cached, with framebuffer mappings write-combining when PAT is available. Therefore an HHDM offset is not a blanket physical-memory access grant, and inherited HHDM permissions are not Rustix's future W^X policy. See the [Limine base revision 3 protocol](https://github.com/limine-bootloader/limine-protocol/blob/trunk/PROTOCOL.md#base-revision-3) and its memory-layout/caching sections.

The boot snapshot now rejects physical map ranges beyond the reported CPUID physical-address width when that CPUID leaf is available. For base revision 3 HHDM-mapped types, it also validates the page-rounded physical span, checked `offset + address` arithmetic, and canonical endpoints in the active linear-address width. If the CPU does not report a physical width, the physical-bound check is skipped and no mapping caller may treat the bound as known. These checks are derived from metadata and Limine's protocol contract; they do not inspect page tables or prove that a non-null loader pointer is readable. Rustix still does not dereference physical memory through the HHDM. Rustix-owned mappings should be non-executable by default; executable kernel text must be explicit. MMIO requires an explicit device range and cache policy, and guard regions must remain unmapped. A page-table transition must preserve the kernel image, active stacks, boot data, descriptor tables, and any loader mappings still needed. Rustix does not reclaim bootloader memory, own or replace the inherited Limine page tables, or provide a heap. Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT setup and fatal exception diagnostics live in `src/arch/x86_64/interrupts.rs`; they do not enable maskable IRQs.

## Mapping inventory before a CR3 transition

The current runtime has several mappings that a future page-table transition must preserve. The address sources and remaining gaps are:

- **Kernel ELF image:** `linker.ld` places `.text`, `.rodata`, `.limine_requests`, `.data`, `.bss`, and `.got` in three `PT_LOAD` segments: RX text, read-only rodata, and RW data/BSS. It exports `__kernel_start` and `__kernel_end` around the complete loadable image, plus page-aligned bounds for each segment. `BootInfo` checks that Limine's reported virtual base matches the linker start, validates ordered contiguous segment bounds covering the rounded image, derives corresponding half-open physical ranges from the executable physical base with checked arithmetic, and validates canonical virtual and reported physical address widths. The release ELF and QEMU runtime report both showed the three aligned ranges on 2026-10-21. The RX, R/NX, and RW/NX labels describe the linker segment intent; these are linker/boot metadata, not proof that the inherited page tables map every page or enforce those permissions, nor proof that loader pointers are readable. The memory-map `ExecutableAndModules` entries remain illustrative, not an authoritative kernel-image extent.
- **Active stack and `BootInfo`:** The assembly `_start` shim captures Limine's entry `RSP` before any Rust prologue. Under the SysV entry convention the synthetic return slot occupies 8 bytes, so `RSP + 8` is the exclusive stack top. The protocol guarantees at least 64 KiB (without a Stack Size request); `BootInfo` records that minimum virtual span, translates it through the HHDM, and requires it to fit within a single `BootloaderReclaimable` physical region. The full loader allocation may be larger, and neither its exact bounds nor a guard page are known. Preserve the inherited mapping; do not infer a guard page.
- **Kernel static data:** `RAM_FILESYSTEM`, GDT, IDT, TSS, the ring-0 stack, and double-fault IST stack are static BSS/data in the kernel image. Exception entry also requires the handler code and the TSS/IDT references to remain mapped. Preserve the complete relevant data/BSS pages and stack pages.
- **Limine request data:** request records are in the kernel's writable `.limine_requests` segment. Response objects and the memory-map pointer array are bootloader-reclaimable; `capture_boot_information` copies the values used later, and the shell does not retain those pointers. Keep them mapped until the copy and validation finish; any later reclaim must be explicit rather than reclaiming the whole bootloader-reclaimable class.
- **Inherited page tables:** Limine places its page tables in bootloader-reclaimable memory, but their layout is unspecified. `BootInfo` captures raw CR3 and validates a decoded physical root address plus the low-bit interpretation selected by active `CR4.PCIDE`; this numeric decode does not make the root dereferenceable. Rustix does not inspect root entries or inventory the live mappings. Keep the old root and its backing pages intact while inspecting/building a replacement; do not reclaim them until a verified switch no longer depends on them.
- **Devices:** COM1 uses x86 port I/O and needs no MMIO virtual mapping. Rustix does not currently request or use a framebuffer. Any future framebuffer or device-register mapping needs an explicit physical range and cache policy.

## Initial kernel-owned mapping policy (design only)

A first Rustix-owned x86_64 root should use 4 KiB pages only and require NX support; if NX is unavailable, abort before switching. Preserve ELF permissions exactly: text RX, rodata R/NX, and `.limine_requests`/data/BSS/GOT RW/NX. Reject W+X mappings. Exclude the complete physical kernel-image span from the generic HHDM in the first implementation so no writable direct-map alias can undermine text or rodata permissions. Ordinary RAM HHDM mappings should be RW/NX and WB.

Preserve the inherited PAT/MTRR configuration during the first transition. The current boot report reads the active `IA32_PAT` value when PAT is supported and parses WB/UC/WC entries, but does not yet inspect MTRRs or establish effective cache types for physical ranges. Under Limine base revision 3 on x86_64, use PAT index 0 for WB, index 3 for UC, and index 5 for WC only when the active configuration is verified. Map MMIO only from explicit device ranges and use UC by default; do not infer MMIO from generic reserved memory-map entries. A framebuffer may use WC only after its response and cache policy are explicitly validated. COM1 uses port I/O and requires no MMIO mapping.

Do not use huge pages in the first root; add them only when physical coverage, permissions, and cache attributes are uniform across the entire huge-page extent. Future Rustix-owned downward-growing stacks should have at least one 4 KiB unmapped guard page below them. The Limine bootstrap stack has no known guard, so none may be assumed there.

Never modify Limine's active page tables in place. Build a separate candidate root from owned frames, leave the inherited root and its backing memory intact, and do not switch CR3 until every transition-critical mapping has been preserved and checked. Any pre-switch failure must discard the candidate root and return its frames without changing the active root. This policy is not yet implemented; Rustix does not inspect the inherited root or replace CR3.

## Planned memory layers

1. **Validated physical regions:** implemented as a fixed-capacity owned snapshot; allocator ownership and reclaim policy remain future work.
2. **Frame allocator integration:** the bounded core is initialized from `BootInfo`; add real allocation callers only after the page-mapping policy and ownership rules are implemented.
3. **Kernel virtual memory:** Rustix still runs on inherited Limine page tables. Before replacing them, preserve the executing kernel, bootstrap/Ring-0 stacks, request/response data, and exception stacks; define the HHDM extent and explicit mapping permissions/cache attributes. Keep writable-executable mappings disallowed by default and reserve MMIO and guard regions.
4. **Heap:** add a global allocator only after frame allocation and page mapping are initialized. Define allocation failure behavior for kernel and interrupt contexts.
5. **Process address spaces:** isolate userspace; define copy-in/copy-out helpers and never treat user pointers as kernel references.

## Invariants to preserve

- Physical ranges use checked arithmetic and the half-open convention `[start, end)`.
- Only complete frames in validated `Usable` regions enter the allocator; the first MiB remains reserved. Firmware and loader-reclaimable regions are not silently promoted to free memory.
- Under the single-allocator-per-physical-map contract, a frame is represented by one allocator-free range or one caller-owned, non-copyable `Frame` token. Only a successful `deallocate` transfers the token back to the allocator; errors return the token and leave allocator ranges and accounting unchanged. The current public constructor relies on callers to enforce unique allocator instances.
- `allocate` returns `None` on frame exhaustion. Metadata-capacity exhaustion is an explicit error, never silent loss of a frame.
- A `Frame` address is physical, not directly dereferenceable. No HHDM translation, page-table mutation, or CR3 switch is permitted until address widths, mapping coverage, permissions/cache attributes, and transition-preservation requirements are established.
- Mapping changes validate alignment, canonical addresses, ownership, and permissions before touching tables.
- User-controlled addresses are validated on every syscall boundary; prior validation is not a substitute for access checks during later copies.
- Tests cover unaligned edges, exact-boundary allocations, arithmetic overflow, overlaps, exhaustion, and unchanged state/ownership on failure.

These are design requirements for future milestones, not functionality delivered by the current boot summary.

# Boot protocol and kernel entry

## Current boot contract

Rustix currently expects a Limine-compatible x86_64 handoff. Limine is an external loader; it does not implement Rustix's kernel services. The kernel requests protocol base revision 3, the higher-half direct-map (HHDM) offset, the executable physical/virtual base addresses, and the memory map.

Requests are kept in `.limine_requests_start`, `.limine_requests`, and `.limine_requests_end` by `linker.ld`. The response-bearing request records are mutable and the output `.limine_requests` section is placed in a writable load segment because Limine fills their response pointers before entering Rustix. The ELF entry symbol is `_start`.

### Entry sequence

1. Limine loads the ELF and transfers control to the assembly `_start` shim under the x86_64 Limine protocol. The shim captures the entry `RSP`, aligns the stack for a SysV call, and invokes `rustix_start`.
2. `rustix_start` configures the legacy COM1 UART for polling output and input.
3. The kernel disables maskable interrupts and installs its bootstrap GDT, TSS, and fatal-exception IDT. This does not initialize IRQ delivery.
4. The kernel reads CPU address widths, active `CR4.PCIDE`, and raw CR3. It decodes CR3's physical root address and low-bit meaning against those CPU properties, without dereferencing the root. It reads EFER.NXE only when CPUID reports NX, and reads `IA32_PAT` only when CPUID reports PAT; these MSR reads do not change CPU state. It copies the HHDM, executable-address, and memory-map responses into an owned `BootInfo` snapshot, validates pointers against the active linear width, derives and validates the linker-defined kernel image span and its page-aligned text/rodata/data segment spans from Limine's executable bases, and derives the protocol-guaranteed minimum initial-stack span from the captured entry `RSP`. It validates physical bounds, stack HHDM arithmetic/coverage, and page-rounded HHDM arithmetic for protocol-mapped memory types. It then builds a frame allocator only from validated usable ranges and rejects malformed metadata before starting the shell.
5. The kernel reports raw CR3 and its decoded physical root/low bits when the reported width permits, physical and virtual kernel-image spans, linker-defined text RX, rodata R/NX, and data RW/NX segment spans, minimum protocol-guaranteed stack span, validated memory ranges, frame counts, CPU features/address widths, NXE state, and the active PAT value plus recognized WB/UC/WC indices, then seeds a volatile RAM filesystem and enters Crabshell on the serial console.
6. The shell busy-polls COM1 because no scheduler or UART interrupt handler exists yet. The kernel does not return to the loader.

The linker exports page-aligned virtual bounds for each of the kernel's three load segments. Boot validates their order, contiguity, coverage of the rounded image span, and derived physical ranges, then reports them with the permissions intended by `linker.ld`. Boot also decodes CR3's root physical address and active low-bit interpretation. These are validated linker/boot metadata only: Rustix does not inspect the inherited page tables, so it has not established that the segments are mapped or that their live permissions match the ELF flags.

The memory-map snapshot stores physical regions as checked half-open ranges `[start, end)` and retains Limine's known classifications plus unknown type values. It does not authorize Rustix to access every region, reclaim loader memory, or mutate the current page tables. Rustix checks physical bounds and calculates canonical HHDM ranges for memory types covered by the requested base revision, but does not dereference physical memory through the HHDM or verify the inherited page tables. These metadata checks cannot prove that a Limine-provided non-null pointer is readable; that remains an explicit handoff trust boundary.

## Link and target configuration

- Rust target: `x86_64-unknown-none`
- Linker script: `linker.ld`
- Kernel virtual base: `0xffffffff80000000`
- Required code-generation constraints: kernel code model, static relocation, and disabled red zone
- Rust panic strategy: abort; the panic handler logs and halts

See [`../development/building.md`](../development/building.md) for commands and toolchain setup.

## Failure behavior and limitations

Missing responses, bad pointers/alignment, invalid HHDM values, invalid image or segment bounds, invalid initial-stack spans, excessive counts, null entries, address arithmetic overflow, and invalid or overlapping physical ranges reject the complete boot snapshot. `_start` halts before starting Crabshell rather than continuing with partial memory metadata. Structural checks cannot establish that a non-null loader pointer is mapped and readable; that remains an explicit trust assumption of the Limine handoff. The installed exception handlers print fatal context and halt; no fault recovery or maskable IRQ delivery is provided. COM1 writes use a bounded polling loop so a non-responsive UART does not block forever.

The repository includes a Limine v12.9.3 binary archive pin with SHA-256 verification, a hybrid ISO recipe, and a bounded QEMU serial smoke test that checks Crabshell's prompt. On 2026-10-16, `just verify` passed in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 on both the working tree and a clean detached worktree with an empty `target` directory; the clean run fetched and built the pinned Limine host utility, created the image, and QEMU reported boot metadata before reaching the prompt. Milestone 0's local exit criteria are met. Hosted CI has not been observed. There is still no framebuffer console, reboot/shutdown interface, or broader boot-protocol compatibility test. The available serial and keyboard drivers do not imply a general device model.

## Future boot work

The kernel now snapshots and validates the Limine map and preserves firmware and loader-reclaimable classifications without reclaiming them. No region is considered Rustix-owned or free for allocation yet. Loader-reclaimable memory must remain unavailable until Rustix has copied any needed boot data and explicitly reaches a documented reclaim point; ACPI reclaimable memory needs the ACPI tables to be copied or parsed first. Frame allocation, page-table replacement, and ownership transfer remain future work.

# Rustix agent instructions

These instructions apply to every coding agent working in this repository. Read this file before changing code. For the architecture and detailed subsystem contracts, use the linked documents under `docs/`; this file summarizes the rules agents must preserve.

## Project mission

Rustix is an experimental 64-bit, Unix-like operating system with its own Rust kernel. Limine is the external boot protocol/loader only. Do not describe Limine, a host shell, or a userspace project as the Rustix kernel.

The kernel is `no_std` and targets `x86_64-unknown-none`. Rust is the implementation language for kernel and OS components. The repository currently uses a Python standard-library script only to assemble and smoke-test the boot image; it does not replace kernel or OS functionality.

Unix-like behavior is a long-term design goal, not a claim of POSIX compatibility. Make unsupported behavior explicit rather than approximating it silently.

## Current project status — read before planning

The source contains:

- A Limine-based x86_64 kernel entry, linker layout, serial diagnostics, and boot-memory reporting.
- A separate allocation-free `no_std` `crabshell` parser crate and a kernel-mode COM1 shell frontend.
- A volatile, fixed-capacity RAM filesystem used by the shell.
- A validated, fixed-capacity boot memory-map snapshot, CPUID/address-width reporting, and gated read-only EFER.NXE/IA32_PAT reporting.
- A fixed-capacity physical frame allocator retained in `BootInfo`; Crabshell's `meminfo` command reports counts. No kernel service currently allocates frames for mappings or other services.
- Pure 4 KiB mapping-range validation, leaf-PTE bit encoding, and PAT layout parsing in `src/paging.rs`; these helpers check alignment, address widths, canonical virtual ranges, W^X, NXE input, and PAT indices. Boot separately captures EFER.NXE and `IA32_PAT` read-only when supported. No code establishes frame ownership, mapping coverage, or effective MTRR cache semantics, or modifies page tables.
- Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT setup and fatal exception handlers for vectors 0–31. Double faults use an IST stack and page faults report CR2. Opt-in QEMU tests inject a page fault and invalid opcode and verify diagnostics plus fatal halt; maskable IRQs remain disabled and exceptions are not recoverable.

Important incomplete work:

- Milestone 0's local exit criteria were met on 2026-10-16: `just verify` passed from both the working tree and a clean detached worktree in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04. The detached worktree had an empty `target` directory, so the run exercised fresh Limine download/host-tool compilation, formatting, 19 host tests, target check, optimized build, image creation, and the 20-second QEMU prompt smoke test. Hosted CI has not been observed.
- A separate interactive `just run` session exercised directory navigation and create/write/append/read/copy/move/list/remove operations; `cp` replaced an existing destination, `rmdir` rejected a nonempty directory and succeeded after its child was removed, and unsupported `memtest` was rejected as expected. Boot snapshot tests and QEMU boot-metadata reporting were exercised. Structural checks still cannot prove that a Limine-provided non-null pointer is mapped or readable; that remains a loader trust boundary.
- Milestone 0 is closed for local acceptance; hosted CI remains pending. Milestone 1's validation criteria are met for the current Limine path, subject to the documented pointer-readability trust boundary. Milestone 2 is **in progress**, not complete. The frame allocator specifies token ownership and preserves the token on failed deallocation; unique allocator-instance use is still caller-enforced. CPU address widths, physical map bounds, protocol-scoped HHDM range arithmetic, Limine executable image bases, page-aligned linker-defined kernel segment bounds, and gated read-only EFER.NXE/IA32_PAT reporting are checked; the segment bounds were reported in QEMU but do not prove inherited page-table coverage or permissions. The transition-critical mapping inventory is documented. `just fault-smoke` has validated controlled page-fault/CR2 and invalid-opcode diagnostics plus fatal halting in QEMU. Rustix still inherits Limine's page tables; CR3's root address is decoded but table entries and complete live mapping extents are not inspected, MTRR interactions are not validated, and there is no kernel-owned page-table manager, heap, or live frame-allocation caller.
- There is no scheduler, userspace, syscall ABI, persistent filesystem, user/kernel isolation, or general IRQ subsystem.

Never advance status or check off a roadmap item merely because source code or an interface exists. Update `docs/roadmap/README.md` only to reflect implementation and validation actually achieved.

## Source-of-truth documents

Consult the relevant documentation before modifying a subsystem:

- [`docs/index.md`](docs/index.md) — documentation entry point and current status.
- [`docs/roadmap/README.md`](docs/roadmap/README.md) — milestone sequencing and exit criteria.
- [`docs/architecture/overview.md`](docs/architecture/overview.md) — current architecture and intended layers.
- [`docs/architecture/boot.md`](docs/architecture/boot.md) — Limine requests, boot snapshot, and trust boundary.
- [`docs/architecture/memory.md`](docs/architecture/memory.md) — memory ownership, frame allocator, and paging direction.
- [`docs/architecture/crabshell.md`](docs/architecture/crabshell.md) — parser/kernel boundary and shell/VFS contract.
- [`docs/architecture/drivers.md`](docs/architecture/drivers.md) — current driver scope and limitations.
- [`docs/security.md`](docs/security.md) — unsafe boundaries and security policy.
- [`docs/development/building.md`](docs/development/building.md) and [`docs/development/testing.md`](docs/development/testing.md) — supported commands and validation expectations.

When code and documentation disagree, inspect the implementation, resolve the discrepancy deliberately, and update the relevant documentation in the same change.

## Repository map

- `src/main.rs` — freestanding entry, boot snapshot acquisition, panic path, and shell handoff.
- `src/boot.rs` — mutable Limine requests, structural response/range validation, owned `BootInfo`, and boot diagnostics.
- `src/memory.rs` — allocation-free memory-map, address-width, canonical-address, Limine HHDM-range, and linker-defined kernel segment validation.
- `src/frame.rs` — bounded physical-frame allocator core; it only uses validated `Usable` ranges, reserves the first MiB, and has fixed range metadata.
- `src/paging.rs` — pure checked range and 4 KiB leaf-PTE bit construction for future mappings; no table access or physical-memory dereference.
- `src/shell.rs` — serial line editor and kernel-side Crabshell executor.
- `src/vfs.rs` — fixed-capacity volatile RAM filesystem and host-side tests.
- `crates/crabshell/` — separate `no_std` parser crate. It has no terminal, filesystem, or kernel dependencies.
- `src/arch/x86_64/serial.rs` — polling COM1 driver for shell input/output.
- `src/arch/x86_64/interrupts.rs` — bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT, assembly stubs, and fatal exception diagnostics.
- `src/arch/x86_64/cpu.rs` — CPUID identification and selected feature reporting.
- `src/arch/x86_64/keyboard.rs` — raw, non-blocking PS/2-compatible scancode polling; not an input subsystem.
- `src/arch/x86_64/io.rs` — narrow port-I/O wrappers.
- `linker.ld`, `.cargo/config.toml`, `rust-toolchain.toml` — target, linker, and toolchain configuration.
- `limine.conf`, `scripts/boot.py` — boot configuration and pinned image/emulator workflow.
- `justfile` — supported development commands.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — CI validation workflow.

## Engineering rules

### Keep changes incremental and coherent

1. Inspect the relevant source, tests, docs, and call sites before editing.
2. State assumptions, invariants, and failure behavior for new kernel interfaces.
3. Prefer small vertical slices that compile and can be tested over broad scaffolding or placeholder APIs.
4. Update tests and documentation alongside behavior changes.
5. Do not change unrelated code, rename files gratuitously, add speculative features, or rewrite project history.
6. Do not commit or create branches unless the user explicitly asks.

### Rust and dependency policy

- Preserve the kernel's `no_std`/freestanding constraints. Do not introduce `std` into kernel code.
- Prefer `core` and existing in-tree abstractions. Do not add third-party Rust dependencies without a concrete need and compatibility/security review.
- Keep the Crabshell parser independent of kernel I/O and VFS implementation. Kernel execution belongs in `src/shell.rs` or an appropriate kernel service.
- Do not add allocation-dependent APIs before a real heap exists. Fixed-capacity structures must document their limits and exhaustion behavior.
- Respect the workspace structure and the host-testable `rustix-kernel` library versus freestanding `rustix` binary boundary.

### Unsafe and hardware code

Unsafe code is expected only at explicit hardware, boot-protocol, assembly, or raw-memory boundaries. Keep each unsafe block narrow and document the invariant that makes it sound. Do not add broad `unsafe impl`, mutable global references, unchecked pointer arithmetic, or unreviewed inline/global assembly.

Before changing boot or memory code, account for these facts:

- Limine fills response pointers before `_start`; the request records must remain writable in the ELF image.
- Rustix validates nullness, alignment, counts, arithmetic, and physical ranges, but cannot prove that a non-null bootloader pointer is readable.
- Physical ranges are half-open `[start, end)` and must use checked arithmetic.
- Only regions classified `Usable` may enter the frame allocator. ACPI, bootloader-reclaimable, framebuffer, reserved, unknown, and other types are not free frames.
- A `Frame` is a physical address, not a dereferenceable pointer. Do not access it through the HHDM or change CR3 until an explicit, reviewed mapping policy and ownership proof exist.
- The kernel currently inherits Limine's page tables. Do not casually replace or mutate them: preserve the executing kernel, boot stack, `BootInfo`, request/response data, exception stacks, and mappings needed to continue execution.
- The exception setup is fatal-only. It does not enable maskable interrupts, configure a PIC/APIC, or provide recovery. Do not call `sti` or claim interrupt support without an IRQ design and tests.
- The shell and VFS run in ring 0 and are not isolated or secure.

For hardware changes, document assumptions about CPU mode, selectors, stacks, page permissions, interrupt state, and failure handling. Avoid new architecture mechanisms that cannot be statically reviewed or validated.

### Shell and filesystem contract

- Crabshell is presently a kernel-mode serial shell, not a userspace program or a POSIX shell.
- Its parser is a separate `no_std`, allocation-free crate; retain that boundary.
- The RAM filesystem is volatile and bounded. Preserve explicit errors and test capacity, path, and mutation edge cases.
- Do not imply persistence, permissions, links, process launching, or shell features that do not exist.
- Update the parser tests and `docs/architecture/crabshell.md` when adding or changing commands.

## Validation workflow

Run the narrowest relevant checks first, then broaden them. From the repository root, the supported commands are:

```sh
just fmt
just test
just check
just build
just image
just smoke
just verify
```

`just test` runs host-side library tests (memory-map validation, frame allocator, VFS, and Crabshell parser). `just check` checks `x86_64-unknown-none`; `just build` links the optimized ELF. `just verify` includes formatting, host tests, target check, pinned Limine image creation, and QEMU prompt smoke test. Image/boot checks need Python, `xorriso`, QEMU, the Rust target, and network access for the first pinned Limine download; Linux/WSL also needs `cc` to build Limine's host utility.

For changes to `src/main.rs`, `src/boot.rs`, linker/request configuration, `src/arch/x86_64/`, `src/frame.rs`, or paging/heap code, the intended full validation is `just verify`. If the environment does not permit a check, report the exact blocker and leave the corresponding roadmap criterion pending.

Do not claim a check passed unless it was run and its successful result was observed. Editor diagnostics are useful but are not a substitute for `cargo test`, a target build, or QEMU. A target check is not a final link; a linked ELF is not proof of boot; a prompt smoke test is not proof of interactive shell behavior or physical-hardware correctness.

## Completion criteria for agent changes

Before reporting work complete:

- Ensure source, tests, docs, and call sites agree.
- Review unsafe and hardware assumptions, including error paths and capacity limits.
- Run appropriate formatting/tests/build/boot checks, or clearly state why they could not run.
- Report files changed, behavior delivered, validation actually observed, and remaining risks.
- Keep milestone status accurate. A subsystem is not complete just because it has code.

```


**Tool Call: docs\\roadmap\\README.md**
Status: Completed

Diff: rustix/docs/roadmap/README.md
```
# Rustix roadmap

This roadmap describes an incremental path from the current bare-metal kernel to a useful Unix-like operating system. It is a dependency plan, not a schedule or a claim of compatibility. Features become current only after the implementation, tests, and exit criteria below agree.

## How to read status

- A checked box means the source/design work exists; it does **not** imply the code compiled, linked, booted, or passed an integration test.
- A milestone is not complete until its stated exit criteria are met and the evidence is recorded.
- Hardware work needs target build and emulator evidence. Host tests cannot validate privileged instructions, bootloader pointers, page tables, device I/O, or exception-entry assembly.
- Future work is intentionally not represented by placeholder APIs or dummy implementations.

## Current status

- **Milestone 0: local exit criteria met from a clean detached worktree; hosted CI pending.** On 2026-10-16, `just verify` passed in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 from both the working tree and a clean detached worktree with an empty `target` directory. The clean run downloaded and compiled the pinned Limine host utility, formatted and tested the workspace, checked and built the target, created the image, and passed the 20-second QEMU prompt smoke test. Hosted CI has not been observed.
- **Milestone 1: validation criteria met for the current Limine path.** The bounded boot snapshot, memory-range classification, and CPUID reporting were covered by host tests and QEMU reporting; the Limine pointer-readability trust boundary remains explicit.
- **Milestone 2: in progress, not complete.** A bounded frame allocator is retained in `BootInfo`, and bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT fatal exception handlers exist. Controlled page-fault and invalid-opcode diagnostics plus fatal halting passed QEMU fault-injection tests. The kernel still inherits Limine's page tables; there is no owned page-table manager, global heap, or live frame-allocation caller.
- **Milestones 3 and later: planned.** There is no general IRQ system, scheduler, userspace, syscall ABI, persistent filesystem, GUI, or networking stack.

## Dependency path

```text
Reliable boot
    -> validated boot state
    -> owned mappings + frame allocation + heap + fault diagnostics
    -> timer/IRQ model + scheduler
    -> isolated processes + syscall ABI
    -> VFS/storage + userland utilities
    -> graphical terminal and other optional services
```

Some device-driver work can proceed in parallel after the core ownership, locking, and testing rules exist. It must not bypass them with direct, unreviewed hardware access.

---

## Milestone 0 — repeatable Rust kernel boot (local exit criteria met)

### Implementation present

- [x] `no_std` Rust kernel binary for `x86_64-unknown-none` with its own ELF linker layout and `_start`.
- [x] Limine boot requests and documented external-loader boundary.
- [x] COM1 serial output/input, startup reporting, and panic/fatal halt path.
- [x] Narrow x86 port-I/O helpers and raw non-blocking PS/2-compatible scancode polling.
- [x] `just` tasks for formatting, host tests, target checks, ELF/image builds, and QEMU smoke/run workflows.
- [x] Pinned Limine v12.9.3 binary archive with SHA-256 verification, BIOS/UEFI hybrid image recipe, and CI workflow.
- [x] Separate allocation-free Crabshell parser, kernel serial frontend, and bounded volatile RAM filesystem.

### Validation evidence and remaining proof

- [x] Current source passed formatting, host tests, target check, and optimized build through `just verify`.
- [x] The pinned image built and the bounded QEMU smoke test reached the Crabshell prompt in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 on 2026-10-16.
- [x] A separate interactive QEMU session exercised `help`, `ls`, `meminfo`, directory navigation, and file create/write/append/read/copy/move/remove operations; `cp` replaced an existing destination, `rmdir` refused a nonempty directory, and unsupported `memtest` was rejected as expected.
- [x] Repeat verification from a clean detached worktree with an empty `target` directory and uncached build/tool state; fresh Limine download/host-tool compilation, image creation, and QEMU prompt smoke test all passed on 2026-10-16.
- [ ] Observe the hosted CI workflow; it has not yet been run or verified.

The QEMU serial log showed the startup banner, HHDM offset, validated memory-map summary, frame counts, CPUID report, and initial prompt. The interactive command check is a focused manual test, not exhaustive shell coverage or a persistence test. `cat` returned `hello Rustix from QEMU`, `cp` replaced an existing destination with source data, `rmdir` refused a nonempty directory, and `pwd` returned to `/home/rustix` after cleanup. If a future smoke test fails, preserve `target/rustix-qemu-serial.log` as failure evidence.

**Exit criteria:** met locally. A clean detached worktree with uncached build/tool state built the image, reached `_start`, reported the expected startup state, and reached the Crabshell prompt in the automated QEMU test. Hosted CI remains unobserved, but is tracked separately from the local exit criteria.

---

## Milestone 1 — validated boot state and machine diagnostics (current Limine path validated)

### Source work present

- [x] Copy Limine HHDM and memory-map information into an owned `BootInfo` before shell startup.
- [x] Bound map entries; check pointer nullness/alignment, count conversion, arithmetic, nonzero ranges, and overlap.
- [x] Convert ranges to half-open `[start, end)` physical intervals.
- [x] Preserve usable, reserved, ACPI, bad-memory, bootloader-reclaimable, executable/module, framebuffer, and unknown region classifications.
- [x] Reject the complete snapshot rather than continue with partial or invalid map metadata.
- [x] Report baseline CPUID vendor/features and document the current COM1-only console.
- [x] Document that classifying a range does not transfer ownership or authorize access/reclamation.

### Remaining proof and polish

- [x] Host tests cover malformed and boundary memory-map values, including empty input, capacity, zero length, overflow, overlap, adjacency, and unknown types.
- [ ] Add serial/report formatting tests if output formatting is separated into a host-testable pure helper.
- [x] Build and boot the image with the current request-section/linker layout; the QEMU serial log showed the boot metadata and CPUID report.
- [ ] Revisit loader pointer validation before introducing another boot source: numeric checks cannot prove non-null response data is mapped and readable.

**Exit criteria:** met for the current Limine path. Host tests and QEMU metadata/reporting were observed in the 2026-10-16 WSL2 `just verify` run, including the clean detached-worktree verification. The loader readability trust boundary remains documented and must be reconsidered before adding another boot source.

---

## Milestone 2 — memory ownership, paging, heap, and exceptions (in progress)

This milestone must establish a safe basis for future allocation and kernel services. Do not allocate a frame for a mapping or dereference it through the HHDM until the mapping/ownership proof is explicit.

### Source work present

- [x] Fixed-capacity frame allocator over validated `Usable` ranges, with page alignment, first-MiB reservation, deallocation, coalescing, bounded metadata, and host tests (`src/frame.rs`).
- [x] Initialize allocator metadata from the boot snapshot and expose counts through Crabshell `meminfo`.
- [x] Specify frame-token ownership and failure semantics: `Frame` is non-copyable, failed deallocation returns the token without changing allocator state, and the current API documents (but does not enforce) one allocator instance per physical map.
- [x] Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT and fatal handlers for exception vectors 0–31.
- [x] Give double faults a dedicated IST stack; report saved register/error context and CR2/error bits for page faults.
- [x] Keep maskable IRQ delivery disabled; current exception handling is fatal-only.

### Required memory-management work

- [x] Read `CR4.LA57` and `CPUID.80000008H` when available to report active/max address widths; validate Limine pointers against active canonical width and host-test 48-/57-bit boundaries.
- [x] Document base revision 3 HHDM region coverage, page-boundary behavior, x86 permissions/cache attributes, and the rule that Rustix does not yet use the HHDM for physical access.
- [x] Reject physical map ranges outside the reported CPUID width when available; validate page-rounded HHDM address arithmetic/canonicality for base revision 3 mapped types. This is protocol-derived validation, not a page-table walk or proof of pointer readability.
- [x] Record current transition-critical objects, their mapping sources/permissions, and unresolved bounds in `docs/architecture/memory.md`.
- [x] Capture Limine's executable physical/virtual bases and resolve the complete linker-defined half-open kernel-image span in `BootInfo`; validate the linker/loader base agreement, canonical virtual span, arithmetic, and reported physical width.
- [x] Capture and validate the linker-defined page-aligned text, rodata, and data segment bounds in `BootInfo`; host-test their ordering, contiguity, rounded image coverage, and derived physical ranges. `just verify` reported all three in QEMU on 2026-10-21. This metadata does not prove inherited page-table coverage or live permissions.
- [x] Capture and validate the protocol-guaranteed minimum 64 KiB active boot-stack span from entry `RSP`; validate its HHDM translation and `BootloaderReclaimable` backing. The actual loader allocation may be larger.
- [x] Record the Limine limitation that exact initial-stack allocation bounds and guard status are not provided; preserve only the known minimum span and do not infer a guard page.
- [x] Decode the inherited CR3 root address and interpret its low bits using the reported physical-address width and active `CR4.PCIDE` state; host-test reserved-bit and width rejection. This does not inspect page-table entries or prove live mappings.
- [x] Document the initial W^X/alias, cache, MMIO, huge-page, guard-page, and inherited-root preservation policy in `docs/architecture/memory.md`; this is design only, not an implementation.
- [ ] Design a kernel-owned page-table transition that preserves every mapping needed to execute during and after the switch: kernel text/data/BSS, current stack, `BootInfo`, Limine requests/responses still needed, GDT/TSS/IDT, exception stacks, serial state, and any direct-map access used by the transition.
- [x] Add pure host-tested 4 KiB mapping-range validation and leaf-PTE bit encoding. Validation requires alignment, checked bounds, known physical width, canonical virtual ranges, and W^X; encoding bounds the physical width and PAT index and requires NXE for non-executable entries. These helpers do not prove frame ownership, mapping coverage, or PAT cache semantics, and do not touch tables.
- [ ] Implement page-table allocation and mapping operations with verified cache semantics, frame ownership, and supported page-size behavior; preserve explicit writable-executable rejection.
- [ ] Ensure mapping failure (including no free frame or range-table exhaustion) rolls back partial page-table allocations or leaves the old map active.
- [x] Add host tests for raw PTE-bit encoding and parsing a supplied PAT layout to find WB/UC/WC indexes.
- [x] Read EFER.NXE only when NX is supported and read `IA32_PAT` only when PAT is supported; report these read-only values and recognized PAT indexes at boot.
- [x] Host-test that rejected deallocations preserve free-range state and return the frame token for unaligned, unmanaged, already-free, and range-table-full cases.
- [ ] Establish effective PAT/MTRR cache types for target physical ranges before using cache indexes; add reviewable emulator tests for mapping, unmapping, permission behavior, and frame reclamation before replacing the inherited CR3.
- [ ] Only after a mapping policy works, add a kernel heap with documented alignment, allocation/free semantics, deterministic exhaustion behavior, and an explicit rule for interrupt contexts.

### Required exception/descriptor validation

- [x] Source contains GDT/TSS/IDT setup and normalized stubs for architectural exception vectors 0–31; double fault uses IST1.
- [x] Target-assemble/link and inspect the stubs and descriptor setup; ELF disassembly confirms call-stack alignment and the error-code normalization paths, while compile-time size assertions pin the IDT, TSS, and exception-context layouts.
- [x] Add QEMU fault-injection tests for a controlled page fault and invalid opcode. `just fault-smoke` verifies vector diagnostics, the exact CR2 address, no shell prompt or return marker, and that QEMU remains halted until its timeout; it then restores the default release kernel and ISO.
- [x] Boot the descriptor setup and both tested exception paths in QEMU. These checks cover only the injected page fault and invalid opcode, not all vectors or physical hardware.

**Exit criteria:** the kernel owns and validates its page-table state, frame allocation/mapping invariants are tested, OOM/failure leaves memory state consistent, page faults are diagnosable, and the kernel remains bootable under emulator tests. Not met: page-table ownership/transition and heap work remain outstanding.

---

## Milestone 3 — IRQs, time, and kernel scheduling

Do not enable interrupts as a side effect of installing exception gates. IRQ delivery needs its own controller, acknowledgement, masking, ownership, and synchronization design.

### Planned work

- [ ] Select and document the initial interrupt controller policy for QEMU and target hardware (for example, legacy PIC compatibility versus local I/O APIC; do not enable both accidentally).
- [ ] Add IRQ vector allocation, controller masking/unmasking, end-of-interrupt acknowledgement, spurious-interrupt handling, and a policy for unexpected IRQs.
- [ ] Add a calibrated/verified timer source and monotonic timekeeping; test interval arithmetic and drift assumptions.
- [ ] Establish interrupt-safe lock primitives and rules for code callable from normal, exception, and IRQ contexts. No allocator or blocking console operation may run in an unsafe interrupt context.
- [ ] Add a minimal kernel-thread context and lifecycle model, stack ownership/guard policy, context-switch tests, and emulator scheduling tests.
- [ ] Add preemption only after lock, stack, timer, and interrupt-return invariants have deterministic tests.
- [ ] Keep application-processor startup/SMP as a separate sub-milestone after per-CPU state, TLB invalidation, and lock semantics exist.

**Exit criteria:** tested IRQ acknowledgement and timer behavior, deterministic thread lifecycle/context-switch tests, and bounded QEMU tests under repeated timer interrupts. No `sti` before the design and tests are ready.

---

## Milestone 4 — isolated processes and syscall ABI

Userspace begins only after page tables, exception handling, scheduling, and controlled memory allocation are trustworthy. The syscall ABI is a product interface and must not emerge accidentally from internal functions.

### Planned work

- [ ] Decide and document the x86_64 user-entry/syscall mechanism, register convention, selector/MSR setup, per-CPU state requirements, and safe return path.
- [ ] Add user/kernel page permissions and process-owned address spaces, including teardown and TLB invalidation rules.
- [ ] Implement copy-in/copy-out helpers that validate lengths, arithmetic, canonical ranges, permissions, and faults; never form a kernel reference directly from a user pointer.
- [ ] Specify version discovery, stable syscall numbers, argument layouts, error encoding, partial-I/O behavior, interruption/restart, and compatibility policy before exposing calls.
- [ ] Add initial process lifecycle and descriptor-table semantics: create/load, exit, wait, close, duplication/inheritance, and process cleanup.
- [ ] Load a deliberately small documented executable format with bounds/segment/permission checks; reject malformed binaries safely.
- [ ] Build adversarial tests for bad pointers, overflow, invalid mappings, process teardown, descriptor misuse, and failed exec.

### First user-visible target

A small controlled `init` process should start from a reproducible image, write through a descriptor, exit with a status, and be waited on by a kernel test harness. Do not claim userspace or process isolation from ring-0 tasks that merely use process-shaped structs.

**Exit criteria:** an isolated init process runs in user mode and uses a versioned, documented syscall subset; invalid user pointers and process teardown are tested without corrupting the kernel.

---

## Milestone 5 — VFS, storage, and device ownership

Keep the current RAM filesystem as a bootstrap/test implementation. Persistence and device drivers require explicit block ownership, error handling, and recovery behavior.

### Planned work

- [ ] Define VFS node, mount, path, metadata, and file-descriptor contracts independently of a particular disk format.
- [ ] Preserve volatile RAMFS as a test backend and add tests for mount/path errors, capacity, partial I/O, and object lifetime.
- [ ] Add a narrowly scoped block-device interface with request validation, timeout/error reporting, and a QEMU-supported initial device driver (such as virtio-blk after PCI discovery is ready).
- [ ] Choose and document the first on-disk filesystem format. Specify partition discovery, byte order, block size, metadata limits, corruption handling, and read-only/recovery behavior before writable support.
- [ ] Add persistent write/read/remount/reboot integration tests; never claim persistence from an in-memory cache alone.
- [ ] Add a driver ownership and DMA policy before any device can write into arbitrary kernel memory.
- [ ] Define file descriptors and basic process I/O semantics before exposing them to applications.

**Exit criteria:** a reproducible image mounts a documented on-disk filesystem, user-visible tools can create/read files across reboot, and corruption/I/O errors fail safely.

---

## Milestone 6 — initial userspace and command-line utilities

Crabshell is currently an in-kernel parser/frontend. The long-term terminal application should reuse its parser model without linking kernel internals or bypassing syscalls.

### Planned work

- [ ] Start a user-mode `init` process and establish executable search, environment, current directory, descriptor inheritance, and process startup rules.
- [ ] Port or adapt Crabshell into a userspace terminal application that uses only the documented syscall/VFS interfaces.
- [ ] Add a small utility set with explicit scope: file listing, text output, create/remove/copy/move, and system information.
- [ ] Add command-line quoting, escaping, history, redirection, and pipelines only with parser tests and process/descriptor/pipe semantics to support them.
- [ ] Add integration tests that launch tools from the filesystem image and exercise them through syscalls; keep unsupported features explicit.

**Exit criteria:** shell and basic tools run as isolated user processes from a reproducible image; no shell command directly calls kernel-private VFS internals.

---

## Milestone 7 — graphical console and GUI (long-term)

A GUI is intentionally downstream of stable memory management, interrupts/input, process isolation, drivers, and userspace. Keep Crabshell's parser independent so a graphical terminal frontend can reuse it.

### Planned work

- [ ] Add validated framebuffer discovery and memory ownership; implement a minimal text/graphics console without compromising serial panic diagnostics.
- [ ] Establish an input subsystem that separates raw device events from key mapping and terminal policy. Plan PS/2 and USB HID support separately; do not treat the current raw scancode poller as a keyboard driver stack.
- [ ] Define a graphics buffer ownership/presentation API, clipping rules, pixel format, and bounds/overflow checks.
- [ ] Implement a minimal compositor/windowing policy only after process isolation and input routing are ready.
- [ ] Run Crabshell as a terminal application in a GUI window using the same parser and userspace services as the serial/TTY path.
- [ ] Preserve serial as a headless recovery/debug console and add repeatable QEMU display/input tests.

**Exit criteria:** a documented GUI session starts from userspace, renders a terminal, accepts tested input, and does not require direct kernel-private VFS access.

---

## Milestone 8 — networking (long-term, after drivers and process isolation)

Networking is not implied by the current device drivers. The initial target should be a tightly scoped virtual-device path with clear ownership and bounded buffers.

### Planned work

- [ ] Complete PCI/device discovery and define DMA buffer ownership, address translation, cache synchronization, and reset/error behavior.
- [ ] Implement one virtual NIC driver with bounded queues and timeouts; test against QEMU user networking.
- [ ] Choose and document a minimal link/network protocol scope before implementation. Keep packet parsing length-checked and fuzzable on the host.
- [ ] Add interface configuration, routing, socket/descriptor ownership, and per-process access policy.
- [ ] Add adversarial packet tests and integration tests for link loss, malformed packets, queue exhaustion, and device reset.
- [ ] Add higher-level transports/services only when the required protocol semantics, entropy source, and security model are designed.

**Exit criteria:** documented user processes can communicate through a tested virtual NIC/network stack, with malformed input and resource exhaustion handled safely. Do not claim internet, TLS, or POSIX sockets merely from a packet driver.

---

## Cross-cutting acceptance gates

Every milestone should include the relevant subset of these gates:

1. **Design:** documented ownership, invariants, resource limits, error semantics, and unsupported cases.
2. **Tests:** host tests for pure policy and malformed/boundary input; integration tests for hardware-facing behavior.
3. **Safety:** review unsafe blocks, pointer provenance/access, integer overflow, privilege boundaries, concurrency, and cleanup/rollback paths.
4. **Build:** format, host tests, target check, and final ELF link as appropriate.
5. **Boot/integration:** QEMU tests for changes to boot, paging, descriptors, interrupts, drivers, processes, or persistence.
6. **Documentation/status:** update architecture and roadmap docs; never check off a criterion without evidence.
7. **Compatibility:** claim Unix/POSIX behavior only for the exact documented subset with conformance-style tests.

## Working rule

Prefer a small end-to-end vertical slice over broad, unbootable scaffolding. A source file, interface, or architecture document is not proof that the corresponding subsystem is operational. When a check cannot run, record the exact environment blocker and leave that acceptance gate pending.

# Rustix documentation

Rustix is a 64-bit, Rust-first, Unix-like operating-system project. This index separates current implementation facts from planned interfaces: roadmap items are proposals until the corresponding code and tests exist.

## Start here

- [Project and implementation overview](architecture/overview.md)
- [Boot protocol and current entry path](architecture/boot.md)
- [Memory-management direction](architecture/memory.md)
- [x86_64 drivers and hardware boundary](architecture/drivers.md)
- [Crabshell and the volatile RAM filesystem](architecture/crabshell.md)
- [Unix-like process and syscall direction](architecture/syscall-abi.md)
- [Build and toolchain guide](development/building.md)
- [Testing and verification](development/testing.md)
- [Security and unsafe-code policy](security.md)
- [Milestones and acceptance criteria](roadmap/README.md)

## Current status

The repository contains a bare-metal `no_std` Rust kernel for `x86_64-unknown-none`. After Limine handoff it validates and copies memory-map metadata into an owned snapshot, captures the executable's physical and virtual bases, complete linker-defined image span, and page-aligned text/rodata/data segment bounds, reports baseline CPUID features, active/maximum address widths, and gated read-only EFER.NXE/IA32_PAT values, seeds a volatile RAM filesystem, and runs Crabshell over the COM1 serial console. Boot-protocol pointers are checked for canonicality using the active 4- or 5-level paging width; physical map bounds and protocol-scoped HHDM range arithmetic are also validated. Crabshell's allocation-free parser is a separate crate; the kernel handles command execution. On 2026-10-16, `just verify` passed in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 both in the working tree and from a clean detached worktree with an empty `target` directory; the clean run downloaded and built the pinned Limine host utility, created the image, and passed the bounded QEMU boot to the shell prompt. The serial log showed memory-map and CPU reporting. Hosted CI has not been observed. A separate interactive QEMU session exercised `help`, `ls`, `meminfo`, directory navigation, and file create/write/append/read/copy/move/remove commands. Milestone 0's local exit criteria are met, and Milestone 1's validation criteria are met for the current Limine path. This is a kernel-mode shell, not a user process. A bounded physical-frame allocator core is initialized from the validated map and reported by `meminfo`; its owned frame token and failed-deallocation semantics are documented and host-tested, but no kernel service allocates or maps frames yet. The Limine base-revision 3 HHDM scope and a conservative initial mapping/alias/cache policy are documented. `BootInfo` records raw CR3 and a validated decode of its root physical address and low-bit interpretation, but Rustix does not use the HHDM to access physical memory, inspect page-table contents, or own/replace the inherited tables. The complete linker-defined kernel-image span and the protocol-guaranteed minimum active boot-stack span are captured; Limine does not provide the exact initial stack allocation or guard status, so Rustix preserves only the known minimum span and does not infer a guard page. There is not yet a heap, a general IRQ subsystem, a scheduler, process isolation, a persistent filesystem, or a Unix compatibility layer. Fatal CPU exceptions have basic GDT/TSS/IDT diagnostics but no recovery. `just fault-smoke` validates a controlled page fault (including CR2) and invalid opcode in QEMU; broader exception coverage and physical-hardware validation remain pending.

## Documentation conventions

- **Implemented** means present in the repository and checked by current code or tests.
- **Planned** means an architectural direction, not a stable ABI or delivered capability.
- Any future ABI proposal must state versioning, error behavior, memory ownership, and compatibility expectations before it is treated as stable.

//! CPU feature and gated, read-only MSR reporting for x86_64 boot diagnostics.

use core::arch::asm;
use core::arch::x86_64::{__cpuid, __cpuid_count};

const IA32_EFER: u32 = 0xc000_0080;
const IA32_PAT: u32 = 0x0000_0277;
const EFER_NXE: u64 = 1 << 11;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct CpuInfo {
    pub vendor: [u8; 12],
    pub max_basic_leaf: u32,
    pub max_extended_leaf: u32,
    pub has_sse2: bool,
    pub has_nx: bool,
    pub nx_enabled: bool,
    pub has_pat: bool,
    pub pat_msr_value: Option<u64>,
    pub has_1g_pages: bool,
    pub has_long_mode: bool,
    pub has_la57: bool,
    pub physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    pub max_linear_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    pub active_linear_address_bits: u8,
    pub pcid_enabled: bool,
}

/// Read CPUID identity, selected feature bits, and supported read-only MSR state.
pub fn identify() -> CpuInfo {
    // CPUID is part of the x86_64 architectural baseline.
    let basic = __cpuid(0);
    let mut vendor = [0; 12];
    vendor[0..4].copy_from_slice(&basic.ebx.to_le_bytes());
    vendor[4..8].copy_from_slice(&basic.edx.to_le_bytes());
    vendor[8..12].copy_from_slice(&basic.ecx.to_le_bytes());

    let basic_features = if basic.eax >= 1 {
        Some(__cpuid(1))
    } else {
        None
    };
    let extended = __cpuid(0x8000_0000);
    let extended_features = if extended.eax >= 0x8000_0001 {
        Some(__cpuid(0x8000_0001))
    } else {
        None
    };
    let structured_features = if basic.eax >= 7 {
        Some(__cpuid_count(7, 0))
    } else {
        None
    };
    let address_widths = if extended.eax >= 0x8000_0008 {
        Some(__cpuid(0x8000_0008).eax)
    } else {
        None
    };
    let cr4 = read_cr4_raw();
    let has_nx = extended_features.is_some_and(|features| features.edx & (1 << 20) != 0);
    let nx_enabled = if has_nx {
        // SAFETY: EFER is available in x86_64 long mode; CPUID confirms NX support,
        // and this boot-time code executes at CPL 0. The read does not change state.
        unsafe { read_msr(IA32_EFER) & EFER_NXE != 0 }
    } else {
        false
    };
    let has_pat = basic_features.is_some_and(|features| features.edx & (1 << 16) != 0);
    let pat_msr_value = if has_pat {
        // SAFETY: CPUID reports PAT support and boot-time kernel code runs at CPL 0.
        Some(unsafe { read_msr(IA32_PAT) })
    } else {
        None
    };

    CpuInfo {
        vendor,
        max_basic_leaf: basic.eax,
        max_extended_leaf: extended.eax,
        has_sse2: basic_features.is_some_and(|features| features.edx & (1 << 26) != 0),
        has_nx,
        nx_enabled,
        has_pat,
        pat_msr_value,
        has_1g_pages: extended_features.is_some_and(|features| features.edx & (1 << 26) != 0),
        has_long_mode: extended_features.is_some_and(|features| features.edx & (1 << 29) != 0),
        has_la57: structured_features.is_some_and(|features| features.ecx & (1 << 16) != 0),
        physical_address_bits: address_widths.map(|eax| (eax & 0xff) as u8),
        max_linear_address_bits: address_widths.map(|eax| ((eax >> 8) & 0xff) as u8),
        active_linear_address_bits: if cr4 & (1 << 12) != 0 { 57 } else { 48 },
        pcid_enabled: cr4 & (1 << 17) != 0,
    }
}

/// Read the current raw CR3 value without changing page-table state.
pub fn read_cr3_raw() -> u64 {
    let cr3: u64;
    // SAFETY: Rustix runs at CPL 0; reading CR3 is privileged but non-mutating.
    unsafe { asm!("mov {}, cr3", out(reg) cr3, options(nomem, nostack, preserves_flags)) };
    cr3
}

/// Read the active linear-address width from CR4.LA57 without changing paging.
/// Read a model-specific register at CPL 0.
///
/// # Safety
/// The caller must ensure the MSR exists on this CPU and that the current
/// privilege level permits the read.
unsafe fn read_msr(index: u32) -> u64 {
    let low: u32;
    let high: u32;
    // SAFETY: The caller guarantees that this MSR is supported and accessible.
    unsafe {
        asm!(
            "rdmsr",
            in("ecx") index,
            out("eax") low,
            out("edx") high,
            options(nomem, nostack)
        );
    }
    (u64::from(high) << 32) | u64::from(low)
}


fn read_cr4_raw() -> u64 {
    let cr4: u64;
    // SAFETY: Rustix runs at CPL 0; reading CR4 is privileged but non-mutating.
    unsafe { asm!("mov {}, cr4", out(reg) cr4, options(nomem, nostack, preserves_flags)) };
    cr4
}

```

    pub pcid_enabled: bool,
}

pub fn identify() -> CpuInfo {

    vendor[4..8].copy_from_slice(&basic.edx.to_le_bytes());
    vendor[8..12].copy_from_slice(&basic.ecx.to_le_bytes());

        Some(__cpuid(0x8000_0001))
    } else {
        None

    } else {
        None
    };
    let address_widths = if extended.eax >= 0x8000_0008 {

        None

    let nx_enabled = if has_nx {

        // and this boot-time code executes at CPL 0. The read does not change state.
        unsafe { read_msr(IA32_EFER) & EFER_NXE != 0 }
    } else {
        false
    };
    let has_pat = basic_features.is_some_and(|features| features.edx & (1 << 16) != 0);
    let pat_msr_value = if has_pat {
        // SAFETY: CPUID reports PAT support and boot-time kernel code runs at CPL 0.

    } else {

        has_la57: structured_features.is_some_and(|features| features.ecx & (1 << 16) != 0),
        physical_address_bits: address_widths.map(|eax| (eax & 0xff) as u8),
        max_linear_address_bits: address_widths.map(|eax| ((eax >> 8) & 0xff) as u8),

/// Read the current raw CR3 value without changing page-table state.
pub fn read_cr3_raw() -> u64 {

## Milestone 2 progress

Added checked decoding of the inherited CR3 root in `src/paging.rs`. It validates the reported physical-address width and reserved bits, and interprets CR3’s low bits according to active `CR4.PCIDE`. `BootInfo` now records and reports the decode result; it **does not** dereference the root or inspect page-table entries.

Added host tests for PCID and cache-control formats, plus reserved-bit and width rejection. Updated the boot, memory, roadmap, and status docs to describe the decode’s limits and clarify that Limine does not provide the initial stack’s exact allocation bounds or guard status.

**Validation:** Formatting, all 42 host tests, target check, and release build passed. `just verify` stopped at image creation because `xorriso` is missing; QEMU smoke testing did not run. Diagnostics were clean. `git diff --check` reported only existing LF/CRLF conversion warnings.

The kernel still inherits Limine’s page tables. The next substantial M2 work is safely inspecting their live mappings and establishing effective PAT/MTRR cache semantics before implementing owned page-table operations. Install `xorriso` in the test environment and rerun `just verify` to complete the boot validation for this change.

```rustix/docs/index.md
    18	## Current status
    19	
    20	The repository contains a bare-metal `no_std` Rust kernel for `x86_64-unknown-none`. After Limine handoff it validates and copies memory-map metadata into an owned snapshot, captures the executable's physical and virtual bases, complete linker-defined image span, and page-aligned text/rodata/data segment bounds, reports baseline CPUID features, active/maximum address widths, and gated read-only EFER.NXE/IA32_PAT values, seeds a volatile RAM filesystem, and runs Crabshell over the COM1 serial console. Boot-protocol pointers are checked for canonicality using the active 4- or 5-level paging width; physical map bounds and protocol-scoped HHDM range arithmetic are also validated. Crabshell's allocation-free parser is a separate crate; the kernel handles command execution. On 2026-10-16, `just verify` passed in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 both in the working tree and from a clean detached worktree with an empty `target` directory; the clean run downloaded and built the pinned Limine host utility, created the image, and passed the bounded QEMU boot to the shell prompt. The serial log showed memory-map and CPU reporting. Hosted CI has not been observed. A separate interactive QEMU session exercised `help`, `ls`, `meminfo`, directory navigation, and file create/write/append/read/copy/move/remove commands. Milestone 0's local exit criteria are met, and Milestone 1's validation criteria are met for the current Limine path. This is a kernel-mode shell, not a user process. A bounded physical-frame allocator core is initialized from the validated map and reported by `meminfo`; its owned frame token and failed-deallocation semantics are documented and host-tested, but no kernel service allocates or maps frames yet. The Limine base-revision 3 HHDM scope and a conservative initial mapping/alias/cache policy are documented. `BootInfo` records raw CR3 and a validated decode of its root physical address and low-bit interpretation, but Rustix does not use the HHDM to access physical memory, inspect page-table contents, or own/replace the inherited tables. The complete linker-defined kernel-image span and the protocol-guaranteed minimum active boot-stack span are captured; Limine does not provide the exact initial stack allocation or guard status, so Rustix preserves only the known minimum span and does not infer a guard page. There is not yet a heap, a general IRQ subsystem, a scheduler, process isolation, a persistent filesystem, or a Unix compatibility layer. Fatal CPU exceptions have basic GDT/TSS/IDT diagnostics but no recovery. `just fault-smoke` validates a controlled page fault (including CR2) and invalid opcode in QEMU; broader exception coverage and physical-hardware validation remain pending.
    21

```rustix/docs/architecture/boot.md
    11	1. Limine loads the ELF and transfers control to the assembly `_start` shim under the x86_64 Limine protocol. The shim captures the entry `RSP`, aligns the stack for a SysV call, and invokes `rustix_start`.
    12	2. `rustix_start` configures the legacy COM1 UART for polling output and input.
    13	3. The kernel disables maskable interrupts and installs its bootstrap GDT, TSS, and fatal-exception IDT. This does not initialize IRQ delivery.
    14	4. The kernel reads CPU address widths, active `CR4.PCIDE`, and raw CR3. It decodes CR3's physical root address and low-bit meaning against those CPU properties, without dereferencing the root. It reads EFER.NXE only when CPUID reports NX, and reads `IA32_PAT` only when CPUID reports PAT; these MSR reads do not change CPU state. It copies the HHDM, executable-address, and memory-map responses into an owned `BootInfo` snapshot, validates pointers against the active linear width, derives and validates the linker-defined kernel image span and its page-aligned text/rodata/data segment spans from Limine's executable bases, and derives the protocol-guaranteed minimum initial-stack span from the captured entry `RSP`. It validates physical bounds, stack HHDM arithmetic/coverage, and page-rounded HHDM arithmetic for protocol-mapped memory types. It then builds a frame allocator only from validated usable ranges and rejects malformed metadata before starting the shell.
    15	5. The kernel reports raw CR3 and its decoded physical root/low bits when the reported width permits, physical and virtual kernel-image spans, linker-defined text RX, rodata R/NX, and data RW/NX segment spans, minimum protocol-guaranteed stack span, validated memory ranges, frame counts, CPU features/address widths, NXE state, and the active PAT value plus recognized WB/UC/WC indices, then seeds a volatile RAM filesystem and enters Crabshell on the serial console.
    16	6. The shell busy-polls COM1 because no scheduler or UART interrupt handler exists yet. The kernel does not return to the loader.
    17	
    18	The linker exports page-aligned virtual bounds for each of the kernel's three load segments. Boot validates their order, contiguity, coverage of the rounded image span, and derived physical ranges, then reports them with the permissions intended by `linker.ld`. Boot also decodes CR3's root physical address and active low-bit interpretation. These are validated linker/boot metadata only: Rustix does not inspect the inherited page tables, so it has not established that the segments are mapped or that their live permissions match the ELF flags.
    19	
    20	The memory-map snapshot stores physical regions as checked half-open ranges `[start, end)` and retains Limine's known classifications plus unknown type values. It does not authorize Rustix to access every region, reclaim loader memory, or mutate the current page tables. Rustix checks physical bounds and calculates canonical HHDM ranges for memory types covered by the requested base revision, but does not dereference physical memory through the HHDM or verify the inherited page tables. These metadata checks cannot prove that a Limine-provided non-null pointer is readable; that remains an explicit handoff trust boundary.

# Rustix roadmap

This roadmap describes an incremental path from the current bare-metal kernel to a useful Unix-like operating system. It is a dependency plan, not a schedule or a claim of compatibility. Features become current only after the implementation, tests, and exit criteria below agree.

## How to read status

- A checked box means the source/design work exists; it does **not** imply the code compiled, linked, booted, or passed an integration test.
- A milestone is not complete until its stated exit criteria are met and the evidence is recorded.
- Hardware work needs target build and emulator evidence. Host tests cannot validate privileged instructions, bootloader pointers, page tables, device I/O, or exception-entry assembly.
- Future work is intentionally not represented by placeholder APIs or dummy implementations.

## Current status

- **Milestone 0: local exit criteria met from a clean detached worktree; hosted CI pending.** On 2026-10-16, `just verify` passed in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 from both the working tree and a clean detached worktree with an empty `target` directory. The clean run downloaded and compiled the pinned Limine host utility, formatted and tested the workspace, checked and built the target, created the image, and passed the 20-second QEMU prompt smoke test. Hosted CI has not been observed.
- **Milestone 1: validation criteria met for the current Limine path.** The bounded boot snapshot, memory-range classification, and CPUID reporting were covered by host tests and QEMU reporting; the Limine pointer-readability trust boundary remains explicit.
- **Milestone 2: in progress, not complete.** A bounded frame allocator is retained in `BootInfo`, and bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT fatal exception handlers exist. Controlled page-fault and invalid-opcode diagnostics plus fatal halting passed QEMU fault-injection tests. The kernel still inherits Limine's page tables; there is no owned page-table manager, global heap, or live frame-allocation caller.
- **Milestones 3 and later: planned.** There is no general IRQ system, scheduler, userspace, syscall ABI, persistent filesystem, GUI, or networking stack.

## Dependency path

```text
Reliable boot
    -> validated boot state
    -> owned mappings + frame allocation + heap + fault diagnostics
    -> timer/IRQ model + scheduler
    -> isolated processes + syscall ABI
    -> VFS/storage + userland utilities
    -> graphical terminal and other optional services
```

Some device-driver work can proceed in parallel after the core ownership, locking, and testing rules exist. It must not bypass them with direct, unreviewed hardware access.

## Milestone 0 — repeatable Rust kernel boot (local exit criteria met)

### Implementation present

- [x] `no_std` Rust kernel binary for `x86_64-unknown-none` with its own ELF linker layout and `_start`.
- [x] Limine boot requests and documented external-loader boundary.
- [x] COM1 serial output/input, startup reporting, and panic/fatal halt path.
- [x] Narrow x86 port-I/O helpers and raw non-blocking PS/2-compatible scancode polling.
- [x] `just` tasks for formatting, host tests, target checks, ELF/image builds, and QEMU smoke/run workflows.
- [x] Pinned Limine v12.9.3 binary archive with SHA-256 verification, BIOS/UEFI hybrid image recipe, and CI workflow.
- [x] Separate allocation-free Crabshell parser, kernel serial frontend, and bounded volatile RAM filesystem.

### Validation evidence and remaining proof

- [x] Current source passed formatting, host tests, target check, and optimized build through `just verify`.
- [x] The pinned image built and the bounded QEMU smoke test reached the Crabshell prompt in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 on 2026-10-16.
- [x] A separate interactive QEMU session exercised `help`, `ls`, `meminfo`, directory navigation, and file create/write/append/read/copy/move/remove operations; `cp` replaced an existing destination, `rmdir` refused a nonempty directory, and unsupported `memtest` was rejected as expected.
- [x] Repeat verification from a clean detached worktree with an empty `target` directory and uncached build/tool state; fresh Limine download/host-tool compilation, image creation, and QEMU prompt smoke test all passed on 2026-10-16.
- [ ] Observe the hosted CI workflow; it has not yet been run or verified.

The QEMU serial log showed the startup banner, HHDM offset, validated memory-map summary, frame counts, CPUID report, and initial prompt. The interactive command check is a focused manual test, not exhaustive shell coverage or a persistence test. `cat` returned `hello Rustix from QEMU`, `cp` replaced an existing destination with source data, `rmdir` refused a nonempty directory, and `pwd` returned to `/home/rustix` after cleanup. If a future smoke test fails, preserve `target/rustix-qemu-serial.log` as failure evidence.

**Exit criteria:** met locally. A clean detached worktree with uncached build/tool state built the image, reached `_start`, reported the expected startup state, and reached the Crabshell prompt in the automated QEMU test. Hosted CI remains unobserved, but is tracked separately from the local exit criteria.
