## Milestone 1 — validated boot state and machine diagnostics (current Limine path validated)

### Source work present

- [x] Copy Limine HHDM and memory-map information into an owned `BootInfo` before shell startup.
- [x] Bound map entries; check pointer nullness/alignment, count conversion, arithmetic, nonzero ranges, and overlap.
- [x] Convert ranges to half-open `[start, end)` physical intervals.
- [x] Preserve usable, reserved, ACPI, bad-memory, bootloader-reclaimable, executable/module, framebuffer, and unknown region classifications.
- [x] Reject the complete snapshot rather than continue with partial or invalid map metadata.
- [x] Report baseline CPUID vendor/features and document the current COM1-only console.
- [x] Document that classifying a range does not transfer ownership or authorize access/reclamation.

### Remaining proof and polish

- [x] Host tests cover malformed and boundary memory-map values, including empty input, capacity, zero length, overflow, overlap, adjacency, and unknown types.
- [ ] Add serial/report formatting tests if output formatting is separated into a host-testable pure helper.
- [x] Build and boot the image with the current request-section/linker layout; the QEMU serial log showed the boot metadata and CPUID report.
- [ ] Revisit loader pointer validation before introducing another boot source: numeric checks cannot prove non-null response data is mapped and readable.

**Exit criteria:** met for the current Limine path. Host tests and QEMU metadata/reporting were observed in the 2026-10-16 WSL2 `just verify` run, including the clean detached-worktree verification. The loader readability trust boundary remains documented and must be reconsidered before adding another boot source.

## Milestone 2 — memory ownership, paging, heap, and exceptions (in progress)

This milestone must establish a safe basis for future allocation and kernel services. Do not allocate a frame for a mapping or dereference it through the HHDM until the mapping/ownership proof is explicit.

### Source work present

- [x] Fixed-capacity frame allocator over validated `Usable` ranges, with page alignment, first-MiB reservation, deallocation, coalescing, bounded metadata, and host tests (`src/frame.rs`).
- [x] Initialize allocator metadata from the boot snapshot and expose counts through Crabshell `meminfo`.
- [x] Specify frame-token ownership and failure semantics: `Frame` is non-copyable, failed deallocation returns the token without changing allocator state, and the current API documents (but does not enforce) one allocator instance per physical map.
- [x] Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT and fatal handlers for exception vectors 0–31.
- [x] Give double faults a dedicated IST stack; report saved register/error context and CR2/error bits for page faults.
- [x] Keep maskable IRQ delivery disabled; current exception handling is fatal-only.

### Required memory-management work

- [x] Read `CR4.LA57` and `CPUID.80000008H` when available to report active/max address widths; validate Limine pointers against active canonical width and host-test 48-/57-bit boundaries.
- [x] Document base revision 3 HHDM region coverage, page-boundary behavior, x86 permissions/cache attributes, and the rule that Rustix does not yet use the HHDM for physical access.
- [x] Reject physical map ranges outside the reported CPUID width when available; validate page-rounded HHDM address arithmetic/canonicality for base revision 3 mapped types. This is protocol-derived validation, not a page-table walk or proof of pointer readability.
- [x] Record current transition-critical objects, their mapping sources/permissions, and unresolved bounds in `docs/architecture/memory.md`.
- [x] Capture Limine's executable physical/virtual bases and resolve the complete linker-defined half-open kernel-image span in `BootInfo`; validate the linker/loader base agreement, canonical virtual span, arithmetic, and reported physical width.
- [x] Capture and validate the linker-defined page-aligned text, rodata, and data segment bounds in `BootInfo`; host-test their ordering, contiguity, rounded image coverage, and derived physical ranges. `just verify` reported all three in QEMU on 2026-10-21. This metadata does not prove inherited page-table coverage or live permissions.
- [x] Capture and validate the protocol-guaranteed minimum 64 KiB active boot-stack span from entry `RSP`; validate its HHDM translation and `BootloaderReclaimable` backing. The actual loader allocation may be larger.
- [x] Record the Limine limitation that exact initial-stack allocation bounds and guard status are not provided; preserve only the known minimum span and do not infer a guard page.
- [x] Decode the inherited CR3 root address and interpret its low bits using the reported physical-address width and active `CR4.PCIDE` state; host-test reserved-bit and width rejection. `just verify` passed on 2026-10-21, including target build, ISO creation, and QEMU prompt smoke. This does not inspect page-table entries or prove live mappings.
- [x] Document the initial W^X/alias, cache, MMIO, huge-page, guard-page, and inherited-root preservation policy in `docs/architecture/memory.md`; this is design only, not an implementation.
- [ ] Design a kernel-owned page-table transition that preserves every mapping needed to execute during and after the switch: kernel text/data/BSS, current stack, `BootInfo`, Limine requests/responses still needed, GDT/TSS/IDT, exception stacks, serial state, and any direct-map access used by the transition.
- [x] Add pure host-tested 4 KiB mapping-range validation and leaf-PTE bit encoding. Validation requires alignment, checked bounds, known physical width, canonical virtual ranges, and W^X; encoding bounds the physical width and PAT index and requires NXE for non-executable entries. These helpers do not prove frame ownership, mapping coverage, or PAT cache semantics, and do not touch tables.
- [ ] Implement page-table allocation and mapping operations with verified cache semantics, frame ownership, and supported page-size behavior; preserve explicit writable-executable rejection.
- [ ] Ensure mapping failure (including no free frame or range-table exhaustion) rolls back partial page-table allocations or leaves the old map active.
- [x] Add host tests for raw PTE-bit encoding and parsing a supplied PAT layout to find WB/UC/WC indexes.
- [x] Read EFER.NXE only when NX is supported and read `IA32_PAT` only when PAT is supported; report these read-only values and recognized PAT indexes at boot.
- [x] Host-test that rejected deallocations preserve free-range state and return the frame token for unaligned, unmanaged, already-free, and range-table-full cases.
- [ ] Establish effective PAT/MTRR cache types for target physical ranges before using cache indexes; add reviewable emulator tests for mapping, unmapping, permission behavior, and frame reclamation before replacing the inherited CR3.
- [ ] Only after a mapping policy works, add a kernel heap with documented alignment, allocation/free semantics, deterministic exhaustion behavior, and an explicit rule for interrupt contexts.

### Required exception/descriptor validation

- [x] Source contains GDT/TSS/IDT setup and normalized stubs for architectural exception vectors 0–31; double fault uses IST1.
- [x] Target-assemble/link and inspect the stubs and descriptor setup; ELF disassembly confirms call-stack alignment and the error-code normalization paths, while compile-time size assertions pin the IDT, TSS, and exception-context layouts.
- [x] Add QEMU fault-injection tests for a controlled page fault and invalid opcode. `just fault-smoke` verifies vector diagnostics, the exact CR2 address, no shell prompt or return marker, and that QEMU remains halted until its timeout; it then restores the default release kernel and ISO.
- [x] Boot the descriptor setup and both tested exception paths in QEMU. These checks cover only the injected page fault and invalid opcode, not all vectors or physical hardware.

**Exit criteria:** the kernel owns and validates its page-table state, frame allocation/mapping invariants are tested, OOM/failure leaves memory state consistent, page faults are diagnosable, and the kernel remains bootable under emulator tests. Not met: page-table ownership/transition and heap work remain outstanding.

---

## Milestone 3 — IRQs, time, and kernel scheduling

Do not enable interrupts as a side effect of installing exception gates. IRQ delivery needs its own controller, acknowledgement, masking, ownership, and synchronization design.

### Planned work

- [ ] Select and document the initial interrupt controller policy for QEMU and target hardware (for example, legacy PIC compatibility versus local I/O APIC; do not enable both accidentally).
- [ ] Add IRQ vector allocation, controller masking/unmasking, end-of-interrupt acknowledgement, spurious-interrupt handling, and a policy for unexpected IRQs.
- [ ] Add a calibrated/verified timer source and monotonic timekeeping; test interval arithmetic and drift assumptions.
- [ ] Establish interrupt-safe lock primitives and rules for code callable from normal, exception, and IRQ contexts. No allocator or blocking console operation may run in an unsafe interrupt context.
- [ ] Add a minimal kernel-thread context and lifecycle model, stack ownership/guard policy, context-switch tests, and emulator scheduling tests.
- [ ] Add preemption only after lock, stack, timer, and interrupt-return invariants have deterministic tests.
- [ ] Keep application-processor startup/SMP as a separate sub-milestone after per-CPU state, TLB invalidation, and lock semantics exist.

**Exit criteria:** tested IRQ acknowledgement and timer behavior, deterministic thread lifecycle/context-switch tests, and bounded QEMU tests under repeated timer interrupts. No `sti` before the design and tests are ready.

---

## Milestone 4 — isolated processes and syscall ABI

Userspace begins only after page tables, exception handling, scheduling, and controlled memory allocation are trustworthy. The syscall ABI is a product interface and must not emerge accidentally from internal functions.

### Planned work

- [ ] Decide and document the x86_64 user-entry/syscall mechanism, register convention, selector/MSR setup, per-CPU state requirements, and safe return path.
- [ ] Add user/kernel page permissions and process-owned address spaces, including teardown and TLB invalidation rules.
- [ ] Implement copy-in/copy-out helpers that validate lengths, arithmetic, canonical ranges, permissions, and faults; never form a kernel reference directly from a user pointer.
- [ ] Specify version discovery, stable syscall numbers, argument layouts, error encoding, partial-I/O behavior, interruption/restart, and compatibility policy before exposing calls.
- [ ] Add initial process lifecycle and descriptor-table semantics: create/load, exit, wait, close, duplication/inheritance, and process cleanup.
- [ ] Load a deliberately small documented executable format with bounds/segment/permission checks; reject malformed binaries safely.
- [ ] Build adversarial tests for bad pointers, overflow, invalid mappings, process teardown, descriptor misuse, and failed exec.

### First user-visible target

A small controlled `init` process should start from a reproducible image, write through a descriptor, exit with a status, and be waited on by a kernel test harness. Do not claim userspace or process isolation from ring-0 tasks that merely use process-shaped structs.

**Exit criteria:** an isolated init process runs in user mode and uses a versioned, documented syscall subset; invalid user pointers and process teardown are tested without corrupting the kernel.

---

## Milestone 5 — VFS, storage, and device ownership

Keep the current RAM filesystem as a bootstrap/test implementation. Persistence and device drivers require explicit block ownership, error handling, and recovery behavior.

### Planned work

- [ ] Define VFS node, mount, path, metadata, and file-descriptor contracts independently of a particular disk format.
- [ ] Preserve volatile RAMFS as a test backend and add tests for mount/path errors, capacity, partial I/O, and object lifetime.
- [ ] Add a narrowly scoped block-device interface with request validation, timeout/error reporting, and a QEMU-supported initial device driver (such as virtio-blk after PCI discovery is ready).
- [ ] Choose and document the first on-disk filesystem format. Specify partition discovery, byte order, block size, metadata limits, corruption handling, and read-only/recovery behavior before writable support.
- [ ] Add persistent write/read/remount/reboot integration tests; never claim persistence from an in-memory cache alone.
- [ ] Add a driver ownership and DMA policy before any device can write into arbitrary kernel memory.
- [ ] Define file descriptors and basic process I/O semantics before exposing them to applications.

**Exit criteria:** a reproducible image mounts a documented on-disk filesystem, user-visible tools can create/read files across reboot, and corruption/I/O errors fail safely.

---

## Milestone 6 — initial userspace and command-line utilities

Crabshell is currently an in-kernel parser/frontend. The long-term terminal application should reuse its parser model without linking kernel internals or bypassing syscalls.

### Planned work

- [ ] Start a user-mode `init` process and establish executable search, environment, current directory, descriptor inheritance, and process startup rules.
- [ ] Port or adapt Crabshell into a userspace terminal application that uses only the documented syscall/VFS interfaces.
- [ ] Add a small utility set with explicit scope: file listing, text output, create/remove/copy/move, and system information.
- [ ] Add command-line quoting, escaping, history, redirection, and pipelines only with parser tests and process/descriptor/pipe semantics to support them.
- [ ] Add integration tests that launch tools from the filesystem image and exercise them through syscalls; keep unsupported features explicit.

**Exit criteria:** shell and basic tools run as isolated user processes from a reproducible image; no shell command directly calls kernel-private VFS internals.

---

## Milestone 7 — graphical console and GUI (long-term)

A GUI is intentionally downstream of stable memory management, interrupts/input, process isolation, drivers, and userspace. Keep Crabshell's parser independent so a graphical terminal frontend can reuse it.

### Planned work

- [ ] Add validated framebuffer discovery and memory ownership; implement a minimal text/graphics console without compromising serial panic diagnostics.
- [ ] Establish an input subsystem that separates raw device events from key mapping and terminal policy. Plan PS/2 and USB HID support separately; do not treat the current raw scancode poller as a keyboard driver stack.
- [ ] Define a graphics buffer ownership/presentation API, clipping rules, pixel format, and bounds/overflow checks.
- [ ] Implement a minimal compositor/windowing policy only after process isolation and input routing are ready.
- [ ] Run Crabshell as a terminal application in a GUI window using the same parser and userspace services as the serial/TTY path.
- [ ] Preserve serial as a headless recovery/debug console and add repeatable QEMU display/input tests.

**Exit criteria:** a documented GUI session starts from userspace, renders a terminal, accepts tested input, and does not require direct kernel-private VFS access.

---

## Milestone 8 — networking (long-term, after drivers and process isolation)

Networking is not implied by the current device drivers. The initial target should be a tightly scoped virtual-device path with clear ownership and bounded buffers.

### Planned work

- [ ] Complete PCI/device discovery and define DMA buffer ownership, address translation, cache synchronization, and reset/error behavior.
- [ ] Implement one virtual NIC driver with bounded queues and timeouts; test against QEMU user networking.
- [ ] Choose and document a minimal link/network protocol scope before implementation. Keep packet parsing length-checked and fuzzable on the host.
- [ ] Add interface configuration, routing, socket/descriptor ownership, and per-process access policy.
- [ ] Add adversarial packet tests and integration tests for link loss, malformed packets, queue exhaustion, and device reset.
- [ ] Add higher-level transports/services only when the required protocol semantics, entropy source, and security model are designed.

**Exit criteria:** documented user processes can communicate through a tested virtual NIC/network stack, with malformed input and resource exhaustion handled safely. Do not claim internet, TLS, or POSIX sockets merely from a packet driver.

---

## Cross-cutting acceptance gates

Every milestone should include the relevant subset of these gates:

1. **Design:** documented ownership, invariants, resource limits, error semantics, and unsupported cases.
2. **Tests:** host tests for pure policy and malformed/boundary input; integration tests for hardware-facing behavior.
3. **Safety:** review unsafe blocks, pointer provenance/access, integer overflow, privilege boundaries, concurrency, and cleanup/rollback paths.
4. **Build:** format, host tests, target check, and final ELF link as appropriate.
5. **Boot/integration:** QEMU tests for changes to boot, paging, descriptors, interrupts, drivers, processes, or persistence.
6. **Documentation/status:** update architecture and roadmap docs; never check off a criterion without evidence.
7. **Compatibility:** claim Unix/POSIX behavior only for the exact documented subset with conformance-style tests.

## Working rule

Prefer a small end-to-end vertical slice over broad, unbootable scaffolding. A source file, interface, or architecture document is not proof that the corresponding subsystem is operational. When a check cannot run, record the exact environment blocker and leave that acceptance gate pending.

# Rustix documentation

Rustix is a 64-bit, Rust-first, Unix-like operating-system project. This index separates current implementation facts from planned interfaces: roadmap items are proposals until the corresponding code and tests exist.

## Start here

- [Project and implementation overview](architecture/overview.md)
- [Boot protocol and current entry path](architecture/boot.md)
- [Memory-management direction](architecture/memory.md)
- [x86_64 drivers and hardware boundary](architecture/drivers.md)
- [Crabshell and the volatile RAM filesystem](architecture/crabshell.md)
- [Unix-like process and syscall direction](architecture/syscall-abi.md)
- [Build and toolchain guide](development/building.md)
- [Testing and verification](development/testing.md)
- [Security and unsafe-code policy](security.md)
- [Milestones and acceptance criteria](roadmap/README.md)

## Current status

The repository contains a bare-metal `no_std` Rust kernel for `x86_64-unknown-none`. After Limine handoff it validates and copies memory-map metadata into an owned snapshot, captures the executable's physical and virtual bases, complete linker-defined image span, and page-aligned text/rodata/data segment bounds, reports baseline CPUID features, active/maximum address widths, and gated read-only EFER.NXE/IA32_PAT values, seeds a volatile RAM filesystem, and runs Crabshell over the COM1 serial console. Boot-protocol pointers are checked for canonicality using the active 4- or 5-level paging width; physical map bounds and protocol-scoped HHDM range arithmetic are also validated. Crabshell's allocation-free parser is a separate crate; the kernel handles command execution. On 2026-10-16, `just verify` passed in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 both in the working tree and from a clean detached worktree with an empty `target` directory; the clean run downloaded and built the pinned Limine host utility, created the image, and passed the bounded QEMU boot to the shell prompt. The serial log showed memory-map and CPU reporting. Hosted CI has not been observed. A separate interactive QEMU session exercised `help`, `ls`, `meminfo`, directory navigation, and file create/write/append/read/copy/move/remove commands. Milestone 0's local exit criteria are met, and Milestone 1's validation criteria are met for the current Limine path. This is a kernel-mode shell, not a user process. A bounded physical-frame allocator core is initialized from the validated map and reported by `meminfo`; its owned frame token and failed-deallocation semantics are documented and host-tested, but no kernel service allocates or maps frames yet. The Limine base-revision 3 HHDM scope and a conservative initial mapping/alias/cache policy are documented. `BootInfo` records raw CR3 and a validated decode of its root physical address and low-bit interpretation, but Rustix does not use the HHDM to access physical memory, inspect page-table contents, or own/replace the inherited tables. The complete linker-defined kernel-image span and the protocol-guaranteed minimum active boot-stack span are captured; Limine does not provide the exact initial stack allocation or guard status, so Rustix preserves only the known minimum span and does not infer a guard page. There is not yet a heap, a general IRQ subsystem, a scheduler, process isolation, a persistent filesystem, or a Unix compatibility layer. Fatal CPU exceptions have basic GDT/TSS/IDT diagnostics but no recovery. `just fault-smoke` validates a controlled page fault (including CR2) and invalid opcode in QEMU; broader exception coverage and physical-hardware validation remain pending. On 2026-10-21, `just verify` passed after the CR3 decode addition, including host tests, target check, release build, ISO creation, and QEMU prompt smoke; this does not inspect page-table entries.

## Documentation conventions

- **Implemented** means present in the repository and checked by current code or tests.
- **Planned** means an architectural direction, not a stable ABI or delivered capability.
- Any future ABI proposal must state versioning, error behavior, memory ownership, and compatibility expectations before it is treated as stable.

//! Pure validation for future x86_64 4 KiB mappings.
//!
//! This module validates address shape and permissions only. It does not prove
//! frame ownership or mapping coverage, access physical memory, or modify tables.

use crate::memory::{is_canonical_virtual_address, physical_range_fits_address_width};

/// Base page size supported by the initial Rustix mapping policy.
pub const PAGE_SIZE: u64 = 4096;

/// The physical root address and interpretation of CR3's low bits.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Cr3Root {
    physical_address: u64,
    low_bits: Cr3LowBits,
}

impl Cr3Root {
    pub const fn physical_address(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_address
    }

    pub const fn low_bits(self) -> Cr3LowBits {
        self.low_bits
    }
}

/// CR3's low bits are either a PCID or the paging-structure cache controls.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Cr3LowBits {
    Pcid(u16),
    CacheControls {
        page_level_write_through: bool,
        page_level_cache_disable: bool,
    },
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Cr3Error {
    UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth,
    InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth,
    ReservedBits,
}

/// Decode the currently active CR3 value without dereferencing its root table.
///
/// The caller supplies the reported physical-address width and whether CR4.PCIDE
/// is active, since those determine the valid address and low-bit encodings.
pub fn parse_cr3_root(
    raw: u64,
    physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    pcid_enabled: bool,
) -> Result<Cr3Root, Cr3Error> {
    let physical_address_bits =
        physical_address_bits.ok_or(Cr3Error::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)?;
    if !(12..=MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS).contains(&physical_address_bits) {
        return Err(Cr3Error::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
    }

    let physical_mask = ((1u64 << physical_address_bits) - 1) & !(PAGE_SIZE - 1);
    let low_mask = if pcid_enabled {
        PAGE_SIZE - 1
    } else {
        (1 << 3) | (1 << 4)
    };
    if raw & !(physical_mask | low_mask) != 0 {
        return Err(Cr3Error::ReservedBits);
    }

    let low_bits = if pcid_enabled {
        Cr3LowBits::Pcid((raw & (PAGE_SIZE - 1)) as u16)
    } else {
        Cr3LowBits::CacheControls {
            page_level_write_through: raw & (1 << 3) != 0,
            page_level_cache_disable: raw & (1 << 4) != 0,
        }
    };

    Ok(Cr3Root {
        physical_address: raw & physical_mask,
        low_bits,
    })
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PagePermissions {
    pub writable: bool,
    pub executable: bool,
    pub user_accessible: bool,
}

/// A mapping range whose arithmetic, address widths, alignment, and W^X policy passed validation.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ValidatedMappingRange {
    virtual_start: u64,
    virtual_end: u64,
    physical_start: u64,
    physical_end: u64,
    physical_address_bits: u8,
    permissions: PagePermissions,
}

impl ValidatedMappingRange {
    pub const fn virtual_start(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_start
    }

    pub const fn virtual_end(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_end
    }

    pub const fn physical_start(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_start
    }

    pub const fn physical_end(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_end
    }

    pub const fn permissions(self) -> PagePermissions {
        self.permissions
    }

    pub const fn length(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_end - self.virtual_start
    }

    /// Encode a single 4 KiB leaf PTE within this validated range.
    ///
    /// `pat_index` is the raw three-bit PAT index; the caller must have verified
    /// that the active PAT MSR gives it the intended cache semantics. `nx_enabled`
    /// must reflect EFER.NXE, not merely CPUID support. This only creates entry
    /// bits—it neither owns the frame nor writes a page table.
    pub fn leaf_4k_entry(
        self,
        page_offset: u64,
        pat_index: u8,
        nx_enabled: bool,
    ) -> Result<PageTableEntry, MappingError> {
        if page_offset & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
            return Err(MappingError::UnalignedPageOffset);
        }
        if page_offset >= self.length() {
            return Err(MappingError::PageOffsetOutOfRange);
        }
        if self.physical_address_bits > MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS {
            return Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
        }
        if pat_index > 7 {
            return Err(MappingError::InvalidPatIndex);
        }
        if !self.permissions.executable && !nx_enabled {
            return Err(MappingError::NxNotEnabled);
        }

        let physical_address = self
            .physical_start
            .checked_add(page_offset)
            .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
        let mut bits = physical_address | PTE_PRESENT;
        if self.permissions.writable {
            bits |= PTE_WRITABLE;
        }
        if self.permissions.user_accessible {
            bits |= PTE_USER;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b001 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_WRITE_THROUGH;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b010 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_CACHE_DISABLE;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b100 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_PAT_4K;
        }
        if !self.permissions.executable {
            bits |= PTE_NO_EXECUTE;
        }

        Ok(PageTableEntry(bits))
    }
}

/// Maximum x86_64 physical-address width representable by a 4 KiB PTE.
pub const MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS: u8 = 52;
const PTE_PRESENT: u64 = 1 << 0;
const PTE_WRITABLE: u64 = 1 << 1;
const PTE_USER: u64 = 1 << 2;
const PTE_WRITE_THROUGH: u64 = 1 << 3;
const PTE_CACHE_DISABLE: u64 = 1 << 4;
const PTE_PAT_4K: u64 = 1 << 7;
const PTE_NO_EXECUTE: u64 = 1 << 63;

/// Raw bits for one x86_64 4 KiB leaf page-table entry.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PageTableEntry(u64);

impl PageTableEntry {
    pub const fn bits(self) -> u64 {
        self.0
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum CachePolicy {
    WriteBack,
    Uncacheable,
    WriteCombining,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum PatLayoutError {
    ReservedBits { index: u8 },
    ReservedMemoryType { index: u8, value: u8 },
}

/// Parsed PAT memory types from an `IA32_PAT` MSR value supplied by the caller.
///
/// Parsing does not read the MSR or establish that this is the active value. The
/// MTRR configuration may also affect the effective cache type for an address.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PatLayout {
    memory_types: [u8; 8],
}

impl PatLayout {
    pub fn from_msr_value(value: u64) -> Result<Self, PatLayoutError> {
        let mut memory_types = [0; 8];
        for (index, memory_type) in memory_types.iter_mut().enumerate() {
            let entry = ((value >> (index * 8)) & 0xff) as u8;
            if entry & !0x07 != 0 {
                return Err(PatLayoutError::ReservedBits { index: index as u8 });
            }
            if entry == 0b010 || entry == 0b011 {
                return Err(PatLayoutError::ReservedMemoryType {
                    index: index as u8,
                    value: entry,
                });
            }
            *memory_type = entry;
        }
        Ok(Self { memory_types })
    }

    /// Return the first PAT index with the exact requested memory type.
    pub fn index_for(self, policy: CachePolicy) -> Option<u8> {
        let requested_type = match policy {
            CachePolicy::Uncacheable => 0b000,
            CachePolicy::WriteCombining => 0b001,
            CachePolicy::WriteBack => 0b110,
        };
        self.memory_types
            .iter()
            .position(|memory_type| *memory_type == requested_type)
            .map(|index| index as u8)
    }
}

pub const FIXED_MTRR_TYPE_COUNT: usize = 88;
const MAX_MTRR_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS: u8 = 52;
const MTRR_VALID: u64 = 1 << 11;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
#[repr(u8)]
pub enum MtrrMemoryType {
    Uncacheable = 0,
    WriteCombining = 1,
    WriteThrough = 4,
    WriteProtected = 5,
    WriteBack = 6,
}

impl MtrrMemoryType {
    pub fn from_raw(value: u8) -> Result<Self, MtrrError> {
        match value {
            0 => Ok(Self::Uncacheable),
            1 => Ok(Self::WriteCombining),
            4 => Ok(Self::WriteThrough),
            5 => Ok(Self::WriteProtected),
            6 => Ok(Self::WriteBack),
            value => Err(MtrrError::ReservedMemoryType { value }),
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum MtrrError {
    InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth,
    ReservedBaseBits,
    ReservedMaskBits,
    ReservedMemoryType { value: u8 },
    FixedRangesUnavailable,
    PhysicalAddressOutOfRange,
    PhysicalAddressWidthMismatch,
    UndefinedOverlap,
}

/// Decoded fixed MTRR memory types in ascending physical-address order.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct FixedMtrrRanges {
    memory_types: [MtrrMemoryType; FIXED_MTRR_TYPE_COUNT],
}

impl FixedMtrrRanges {
    /// Decode FIX64K_00000, FIX16K_80000/A0000, and FIX4K_C0000..F8000.
    pub fn from_msr_values(registers: [u64; 11]) -> Result<Self, MtrrError> {
        let mut memory_types = [MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable; FIXED_MTRR_TYPE_COUNT];
        for (register_index, value) in registers.iter().enumerate() {
            for slot in 0..8 {
                let memory_type = ((value >> (slot * 8)) & 0xff) as u8;
                memory_types[register_index * 8 + slot] = MtrrMemoryType::from_raw(memory_type)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(Self { memory_types })
    }

    fn type_for_address(&self, address: u64) -> Option<MtrrMemoryType> {
        let index = if address < 0x80_000 {
            (address / 0x1_0000) as usize
        } else if address < 0xa0_000 {
            8 + ((address - 0x80_000) / 0x4000) as usize
        } else if address < 0xc0_000 {
            16 + ((address - 0xa0_000) / 0x4000) as usize
        } else if address < 0x10_0000 {
            24 + ((address - 0xc0_000) / 0x1000) as usize
        } else {
            return None;
        };
        Some(self.memory_types[index])
    }
}

/// One valid variable-range MTRR. Address masks need not be contiguous; bits
/// omitted by the mask simply do not participate in matching.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct VariableMtrr {
    base_match: u64,
    address_mask: u64,
    physical_address_bits: u8,
    memory_type: MtrrMemoryType,
}

impl VariableMtrr {
    /// Decode one IA32_MTRR_PHYSBASE/PHYSMASK pair. Invalid pairs are ignored.
    pub fn from_msr_values(
        base: u64,
        mask: u64,
        physical_address_bits: u8,
    ) -> Result<Option<Self>, MtrrError> {
        if !(12..=MAX_MTRR_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS).contains(&physical_address_bits) {
            return Err(MtrrError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
        }
        let physical_mask = ((1u64 << physical_address_bits) - 1) & !(PAGE_SIZE - 1);
        if base & !(physical_mask | 0xff) != 0 {
            return Err(MtrrError::ReservedBaseBits);
        }
        if mask & !(physical_mask | MTRR_VALID) != 0 {
            return Err(MtrrError::ReservedMaskBits);
        }
        let memory_type = MtrrMemoryType::from_raw((base & 0xff) as u8)?;
        if mask & MTRR_VALID == 0 {
            return Ok(None);
        }

        let address_mask = mask & physical_mask;
        Ok(Some(Self {
            base_match: base & address_mask,
            address_mask,
            physical_address_bits,
            memory_type,
        }))
    }

    fn matches(self, address: u64) -> bool {
        address & self.address_mask == self.base_match
    }
}

/// MTRR policy decoded from supplied register values; it does not access MSRs.
pub struct MtrrLayout<'a> {
    physical_address_bits: u8,
    enabled: bool,
    fixed_enabled: bool,
    default_type: MtrrMemoryType,
    fixed_ranges: Option<&'a FixedMtrrRanges>,
    variable_ranges: &'a [VariableMtrr],
}

impl<'a> MtrrLayout<'a> {
    pub fn new(
        physical_address_bits: u8,
        enabled: bool,
        fixed_enabled: bool,
        default_type: MtrrMemoryType,
        fixed_ranges: Option<&'a FixedMtrrRanges>,
        variable_ranges: &'a [VariableMtrr],
    ) -> Result<Self, MtrrError> {
        if !(12..=MAX_MTRR_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS).contains(&physical_address_bits) {
            return Err(MtrrError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
        }
        if enabled && fixed_enabled && fixed_ranges.is_none() {
            return Err(MtrrError::FixedRangesUnavailable);
        }
        if variable_ranges
            .iter()
            .any(|range| range.physical_address_bits != physical_address_bits)
        {
            return Err(MtrrError::PhysicalAddressWidthMismatch);
        }

        Ok(Self {
            physical_address_bits,
            enabled,
            fixed_enabled,
            default_type,
            fixed_ranges,
            variable_ranges,
        })
    }

    /// Resolve the MTRR type for one physical address, rejecting undefined overlaps.
    pub fn memory_type_for(&self, address: u64) -> Result<MtrrMemoryType, MtrrError> {
        if address >= (1u64 << self.physical_address_bits) {
            return Err(MtrrError::PhysicalAddressOutOfRange);
        }
        if !self.enabled {
            return Ok(MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable);
        }
        if self.fixed_enabled {
            if let Some(memory_type) = self
                .fixed_ranges
                .and_then(|ranges| ranges.type_for_address(address))
            {
                return Ok(memory_type);
            }
        }

        let mut matched_types = 0u8;
        for range in self.variable_ranges {
            if range.matches(address) {
                matched_types |= 1 << range.memory_type as u8;
            }
        }
        if matched_types == 0 {
            return Ok(self.default_type);
        }
        if matched_types & (1 << MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable as u8) != 0 {
            return Ok(MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable);
        }
        if matched_types.count_ones() == 1 {
            return MtrrMemoryType::from_raw(matched_types.trailing_zeros() as u8);
        }
        let write_back_and_through = (1 << MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack as u8)
            | (1 << MtrrMemoryType::WriteThrough as u8);
        if matched_types == write_back_and_through {
            return Ok(MtrrMemoryType::WriteThrough);
        }
        Err(MtrrError::UndefinedOverlap)
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum MappingError {
    EmptyRange,
    UnalignedRange,
    AddressOverflow,
    InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth,
    UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth,
    InvalidVirtualAddressWidth,
    PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds,
    NonCanonicalVirtualRange,
    CrossesCanonicalHole,
    WritableExecutable,
    UnalignedPageOffset,
    PageOffsetOutOfRange,
    InvalidPatIndex,
    NxNotEnabled,
}

/// Validate one contiguous range for a future 4 KiB page-mapping operation.
///
/// The physical-address width must be known; passing `None` is rejected rather
/// than treating an unknown bound as unbounded. Successful validation does not
/// establish that physical frames are owned by a caller or mapped in the HHDM.
pub fn validate_mapping_range(
    virtual_start: u64,
    physical_start: u64,
    length: u64,
    permissions: PagePermissions,
    physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    virtual_address_bits: u8,
) -> Result<ValidatedMappingRange, MappingError> {
    if virtual_address_bits == 0 || virtual_address_bits > 64 {
        return Err(MappingError::InvalidVirtualAddressWidth);
    }
    let physical_address_bits =
        physical_address_bits.ok_or(MappingError::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)?;
    if physical_address_bits == 0 || physical_address_bits > 64 {
        return Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
    }
    if length == 0 {
        return Err(MappingError::EmptyRange);
    }
    if (virtual_start | physical_start | length) & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
        return Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange);
    }
    if permissions.writable && permissions.executable {
        return Err(MappingError::WritableExecutable);
    }

    let virtual_end = virtual_start
        .checked_add(length)
        .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
    let physical_end = physical_start
        .checked_add(length)
        .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
    if !physical_range_fits_address_width(physical_start, physical_end, physical_address_bits) {
        return Err(MappingError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds);
    }

    let last_virtual_address = virtual_end - 1;
    if !is_canonical_virtual_address(virtual_start, virtual_address_bits)
        || !is_canonical_virtual_address(last_virtual_address, virtual_address_bits)
    {
        return Err(MappingError::NonCanonicalVirtualRange);
    }
    if virtual_address_bits < 64
        && ((virtual_start ^ last_virtual_address) & (1u64 << (virtual_address_bits - 1))) != 0
    {
        return Err(MappingError::CrossesCanonicalHole);
    }

    Ok(ValidatedMappingRange {
        virtual_start,
        virtual_end,
        physical_start,
        physical_end,
        physical_address_bits,
        permissions,
    })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::{
        parse_cr3_root, validate_mapping_range, Cr3Error, Cr3LowBits, MappingError,
        PagePermissions, ValidatedMappingRange, PAGE_SIZE,
    };

    const KERNEL_READ_ONLY: PagePermissions = PagePermissions {
        writable: false,
        executable: false,
        user_accessible: false,
    };

    #[test]
    fn decodes_cr3_root_with_pcid_enabled() {
        let root = parse_cr3_root(0x1234_5000 | 0x0abc, Some(48), true).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(root.physical_address(), 0x1234_5000);
        assert_eq!(root.low_bits(), Cr3LowBits::Pcid(0x0abc));
    }

    #[test]
    fn decodes_cr3_cache_controls_without_pcid() {
        let root = parse_cr3_root(0x1234_5000 | (1 << 3) | (1 << 4), Some(48), false).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(root.physical_address(), 0x1234_5000);
        assert_eq!(
            root.low_bits(),
            Cr3LowBits::CacheControls {
                page_level_write_through: true,
                page_level_cache_disable: true,
            }
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_cr3_reserved_bits_and_invalid_address_widths() {
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(0x1000 | 1, Some(48), false),
            Err(Cr3Error::ReservedBits)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(1 << 40, Some(40), true),
            Err(Cr3Error::ReservedBits)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(0x1000, None, false),
            Err(Cr3Error::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(0x1000, Some(53), false),
            Err(Cr3Error::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn accepts_aligned_ranges_at_canonical_boundaries() {
        let lower = validate_mapping_range(
            0x0000_7fff_ffff_e000,
            0x1000,
            0x2000,
            KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            Some(14),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            lower,
            ValidatedMappingRange {
                virtual_start: 0x0000_7fff_ffff_e000,
                virtual_end: 0x0000_8000_0000_0000,
                physical_start: 0x1000,
                physical_end: 0x3000,
                physical_address_bits: 14,
                permissions: KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            }
        );

        let upper = validate_mapping_range(
            0xffff_8000_0000_0000,
            0,
            PAGE_SIZE,
            KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            Some(12),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(upper.virtual_end(), 0xffff_8000_0000_1000);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_empty_and_unaligned_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x2000, 0, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(32), 48),
            Err(MappingError::EmptyRange)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1001, 0x2000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(32), 48),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                0x2000,
                PAGE_SIZE + 1,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(32),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn requires_known_valid_address_widths_and_bounded_physical_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, None, 48),
            Err(MappingError::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(0), 48),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(52), 0),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidVirtualAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                0x1000,
                PAGE_SIZE * 2,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(13),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_noncanonical_ranges_and_ranges_crossing_the_canonical_hole() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x0000_8000_0000_0000,
                0,
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(52),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::NonCanonicalVirtualRange)
        );

        let crosses_hole = (0xffff_8000_0000_1000u64) - 0x0000_7fff_ffff_f000;
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x0000_7fff_ffff_f000,
                0,
                crosses_hole,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                48,
            ),
            Err(MappingError::CrossesCanonicalHole)
        );
    }

    fn pat_value(entries: [u8; 8]) -> u64 {
        entries.iter().enumerate().fold(0, |value, (index, entry)| {
            value | ((*entry as u64) << (index * 8))
        })
    }

    #[test]
    fn resolves_cache_policies_from_the_supplied_pat_layout() {
        let pat = pat_value([6, 4, 7, 0, 6, 1, 7, 0]);
        let layout = super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(pat).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteBack), Some(0));
        assert_eq!(layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::Uncacheable), Some(3));
        assert_eq!(
            layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteCombining),
            Some(5)
        );

        let without_write_combining =
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(pat_value([6, 4, 7, 0, 6, 4, 7, 0])).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            without_write_combining.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteCombining),
            None
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_reserved_pat_entries() {
        assert_eq!(
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(0x08),
            Err(super::PatLayoutError::ReservedBits { index: 0 })
        );
        assert_eq!(
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(0b010 << 24),
            Err(super::PatLayoutError::ReservedMemoryType {
                index: 3,
                value: 0b010,
            })
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn encodes_4k_leaf_permissions_nx_and_pat_index_bits() {
        let writable_user_page = PagePermissions {
            writable: true,
            executable: false,
            user_accessible: true,
        };
        let mapping = validate_mapping_range(
            0x4000,
            0x20_0000,
            PAGE_SIZE * 2,
            writable_user_page,
            Some(52),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();

        let first = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0b101, true).unwrap().bits();
        assert_eq!(
            first,
            0x20_0000 | (1 << 0) | (1 << 1) | (1 << 2) | (1 << 3) | (1 << 7) | (1 << 63)
        );
        let second = mapping
            .leaf_4k_entry(PAGE_SIZE, 0b101, true)
            .unwrap()
            .bits();
        assert_eq!(second & 0x000f_ffff_ffff_f000, 0x20_1000);
        assert_eq!(second & (1 << 63), 1 << 63);

        let all_pat_bits = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0b111, true).unwrap().bits()
            & ((1 << 3) | (1 << 4) | (1 << 7));
        assert_eq!(all_pat_bits, (1 << 3) | (1 << 4) | (1 << 7));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_unencodable_leaf_attributes_and_page_offsets() {
        let mapping =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(52), 48)
                .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 8, true),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPatIndex)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, false),
            Err(MappingError::NxNotEnabled)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(1, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedPageOffset)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(PAGE_SIZE, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::PageOffsetOutOfRange)
        );

        let too_wide =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(53), 48)
                .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            too_wide.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn executable_leaf_does_not_set_nx() {
        let executable = PagePermissions {
            writable: false,
            executable: true,
            user_accessible: false,
        };
        let mapping =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, executable, Some(52), 48).unwrap();
        let entry = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, false).unwrap().bits();
        assert_eq!(entry, 0x20_0000 | 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_arithmetic_overflow_and_writable_executable_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                u64::MAX - (PAGE_SIZE - 1),
                0,
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                64
            ),
            Err(MappingError::AddressOverflow)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                u64::MAX - (PAGE_SIZE - 1),
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                64
            ),
            Err(MappingError::AddressOverflow)
        );

        let writable_executable = PagePermissions {
            writable: true,
            executable: true,
            user_accessible: false,
        };
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x2000, PAGE_SIZE, writable_executable, Some(52), 48),
            Err(MappingError::WritableExecutable)
        );
    }
}
