//! Pure validation for future x86_64 4 KiB mappings.
//!
//! This module validates address shape and permissions only. It does not prove
//! frame ownership or mapping coverage, access physical memory, or modify tables.

use crate::memory::{is_canonical_virtual_address, physical_range_fits_address_width};

/// Base page size supported by the initial Rustix mapping policy.
pub const PAGE_SIZE: u64 = 4096;

/// The physical root address and interpretation of CR3's low bits.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Cr3Root {
    physical_address: u64,
    low_bits: Cr3LowBits,
}

impl Cr3Root {
    pub const fn physical_address(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_address
    }

    pub const fn low_bits(self) -> Cr3LowBits {
        self.low_bits
    }
}

/// CR3's low bits are either a PCID or the paging-structure cache controls.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Cr3LowBits {
    Pcid(u16),
    CacheControls {
        page_level_write_through: bool,
        page_level_cache_disable: bool,
    },
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Cr3Error {
    UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth,
    InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth,
    ReservedBits,
}

/// Decode the currently active CR3 value without dereferencing its root table.
///
/// The caller supplies the reported physical-address width and whether CR4.PCIDE
/// is active, since those determine the valid address and low-bit encodings.
pub fn parse_cr3_root(
    raw: u64,
    physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    pcid_enabled: bool,
) -> Result<Cr3Root, Cr3Error> {
    let physical_address_bits =
        physical_address_bits.ok_or(Cr3Error::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)?;
    if !(12..=MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS).contains(&physical_address_bits) {
        return Err(Cr3Error::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
    }

    let physical_mask = ((1u64 << physical_address_bits) - 1) & !(PAGE_SIZE - 1);
    let low_mask = if pcid_enabled {
        PAGE_SIZE - 1
    } else {
        (1 << 3) | (1 << 4)
    };
    if raw & !(physical_mask | low_mask) != 0 {
        return Err(Cr3Error::ReservedBits);
    }

    let low_bits = if pcid_enabled {
        Cr3LowBits::Pcid((raw & (PAGE_SIZE - 1)) as u16)
    } else {
        Cr3LowBits::CacheControls {
            page_level_write_through: raw & (1 << 3) != 0,
            page_level_cache_disable: raw & (1 << 4) != 0,
        }
    };

    Ok(Cr3Root {
        physical_address: raw & physical_mask,
        low_bits,
    })
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PagePermissions {
    pub writable: bool,
    pub executable: bool,
    pub user_accessible: bool,
}

/// A mapping range whose arithmetic, address widths, alignment, and W^X policy passed validation.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ValidatedMappingRange {
    virtual_start: u64,
    virtual_end: u64,
    physical_start: u64,
    physical_end: u64,
    physical_address_bits: u8,
    permissions: PagePermissions,
}

impl ValidatedMappingRange {
    pub const fn virtual_start(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_start
    }

    pub const fn virtual_end(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_end
    }

    pub const fn physical_start(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_start
    }

    pub const fn physical_end(self) -> u64 {
        self.physical_end
    }

    pub const fn permissions(self) -> PagePermissions {
        self.permissions
    }

    pub const fn length(self) -> u64 {
        self.virtual_end - self.virtual_start
    }

    /// Encode a single 4 KiB leaf PTE within this validated range.
    ///
    /// `pat_index` is the raw three-bit PAT index; the caller must have verified
    /// that the active PAT MSR gives it the intended cache semantics. `nx_enabled`
    /// must reflect EFER.NXE, not merely CPUID support. This only creates entry
    /// bits—it neither owns the frame nor writes a page table.
    pub fn leaf_4k_entry(
        self,
        page_offset: u64,
        pat_index: u8,
        nx_enabled: bool,
    ) -> Result<PageTableEntry, MappingError> {
        if page_offset & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
            return Err(MappingError::UnalignedPageOffset);
        }
        if page_offset >= self.length() {
            return Err(MappingError::PageOffsetOutOfRange);
        }
        if self.physical_address_bits > MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS {
            return Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
        }
        if pat_index > 7 {
            return Err(MappingError::InvalidPatIndex);
        }
        if !self.permissions.executable && !nx_enabled {
            return Err(MappingError::NxNotEnabled);
        }

        let physical_address = self
            .physical_start
            .checked_add(page_offset)
            .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
        let mut bits = physical_address | PTE_PRESENT;
        if self.permissions.writable {
            bits |= PTE_WRITABLE;
        }
        if self.permissions.user_accessible {
            bits |= PTE_USER;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b001 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_WRITE_THROUGH;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b010 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_CACHE_DISABLE;
        }
        if pat_index & 0b100 != 0 {
            bits |= PTE_PAT_4K;
        }
        if !self.permissions.executable {
            bits |= PTE_NO_EXECUTE;
        }

        Ok(PageTableEntry(bits))
    }
}

/// Maximum x86_64 physical-address width representable by a 4 KiB PTE.
pub const MAX_PTE_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS: u8 = 52;
const PTE_PRESENT: u64 = 1 << 0;
const PTE_WRITABLE: u64 = 1 << 1;
const PTE_USER: u64 = 1 << 2;
const PTE_WRITE_THROUGH: u64 = 1 << 3;
const PTE_CACHE_DISABLE: u64 = 1 << 4;
const PTE_PAT_4K: u64 = 1 << 7;
const PTE_NO_EXECUTE: u64 = 1 << 63;

/// Raw bits for one x86_64 4 KiB leaf page-table entry.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PageTableEntry(u64);

impl PageTableEntry {
    pub const fn bits(self) -> u64 {
        self.0
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum CachePolicy {
    WriteBack,
    Uncacheable,
    WriteCombining,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum PatLayoutError {
    ReservedBits { index: u8 },
    ReservedMemoryType { index: u8, value: u8 },
}

/// Parsed PAT memory types from an `IA32_PAT` MSR value supplied by the caller.
///
/// Parsing does not read the MSR or establish that this is the active value. The
/// MTRR configuration may also affect the effective cache type for an address.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PatLayout {
    memory_types: [u8; 8],
}

impl PatLayout {
    pub fn from_msr_value(value: u64) -> Result<Self, PatLayoutError> {
        let mut memory_types = [0; 8];
        for (index, memory_type) in memory_types.iter_mut().enumerate() {
            let entry = ((value >> (index * 8)) & 0xff) as u8;
            if entry & !0x07 != 0 {
                return Err(PatLayoutError::ReservedBits { index: index as u8 });
            }
            if entry == 0b010 || entry == 0b011 {
                return Err(PatLayoutError::ReservedMemoryType {
                    index: index as u8,
                    value: entry,
                });
            }
            *memory_type = entry;
        }
        Ok(Self { memory_types })
    }

    /// Return the first PAT index with the exact requested memory type.
    pub fn index_for(self, policy: CachePolicy) -> Option<u8> {
        let requested_type = match policy {
            CachePolicy::Uncacheable => 0b000,
            CachePolicy::WriteCombining => 0b001,
            CachePolicy::WriteBack => 0b110,
        };
        self.memory_types
            .iter()
            .position(|memory_type| *memory_type == requested_type)
            .map(|index| index as u8)
    }
}

pub const FIXED_MTRR_TYPE_COUNT: usize = 88;
const MAX_MTRR_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS: u8 = 52;
const MTRR_VALID: u64 = 1 << 11;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
#[repr(u8)]
pub enum MtrrMemoryType {
    Uncacheable = 0,
    WriteCombining = 1,
    WriteThrough = 4,
    WriteProtected = 5,
    WriteBack = 6,
}

impl MtrrMemoryType {
    pub fn from_raw(value: u8) -> Result<Self, MtrrError> {
        match value {
            0 => Ok(Self::Uncacheable),
            1 => Ok(Self::WriteCombining),
            4 => Ok(Self::WriteThrough),
            5 => Ok(Self::WriteProtected),
            6 => Ok(Self::WriteBack),
            value => Err(MtrrError::ReservedMemoryType { value }),
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum MtrrError {
    InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth,
    ReservedBaseBits,
    ReservedMaskBits,
    ReservedMemoryType { value: u8 },
    FixedRangesUnavailable,
    PhysicalAddressOutOfRange,
    PhysicalAddressWidthMismatch,
    UndefinedOverlap,
}

/// Decoded fixed MTRR memory types in ascending physical-address order.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct FixedMtrrRanges {
    memory_types: [MtrrMemoryType; FIXED_MTRR_TYPE_COUNT],
}

impl FixedMtrrRanges {
    /// Decode FIX64K_00000, FIX16K_80000/A0000, and FIX4K_C0000..F8000.
    pub fn from_msr_values(registers: [u64; 11]) -> Result<Self, MtrrError> {
        let mut memory_types = [MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable; FIXED_MTRR_TYPE_COUNT];
        for (register_index, value) in registers.iter().enumerate() {
            for slot in 0..8 {
                let memory_type = ((value >> (slot * 8)) & 0xff) as u8;
                memory_types[register_index * 8 + slot] = MtrrMemoryType::from_raw(memory_type)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(Self { memory_types })
    }

    fn type_for_address(&self, address: u64) -> Option<MtrrMemoryType> {
        let index = if address < 0x80_000 {
            (address / 0x1_0000) as usize
        } else if address < 0xa0_000 {
            8 + ((address - 0x80_000) / 0x4000) as usize
        } else if address < 0xc0_000 {
            16 + ((address - 0xa0_000) / 0x4000) as usize
        } else if address < 0x10_0000 {
            24 + ((address - 0xc0_000) / 0x1000) as usize
        } else {
            return None;
        };
        Some(self.memory_types[index])
    }
}

/// One valid variable-range MTRR. Address masks need not be contiguous; bits
/// omitted by the mask simply do not participate in matching.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct VariableMtrr {
    base_match: u64,
    address_mask: u64,
    physical_address_bits: u8,
    memory_type: MtrrMemoryType,
}

impl VariableMtrr {
    /// Decode one IA32_MTRR_PHYSBASE/PHYSMASK pair. A clear valid bit returns `Ok(None).`
    pub fn from_msr_values(
        base: u64,
        mask: u64,
        physical_address_bits: u8,
    ) -> Result<Option<Self>, MtrrError> {
        if !(12..=MAX_MTRR_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS).contains(&physical_address_bits) {
            return Err(MtrrError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
        }
        let physical_mask = ((1u64 << physical_address_bits) - 1) & !(PAGE_SIZE - 1);
        if base & !(physical_mask | 0xff) != 0 {
            return Err(MtrrError::ReservedBaseBits);
        }
        if mask & !(physical_mask | MTRR_VALID) != 0 {
            return Err(MtrrError::ReservedMaskBits);
        }
        let memory_type = MtrrMemoryType::from_raw((base & 0xff) as u8)?;
        if mask & MTRR_VALID == 0 {
            return Ok(None);
        }

        let address_mask = mask & physical_mask;
        Ok(Some(Self {
            base_match: base & address_mask,
            address_mask,
            physical_address_bits,
            memory_type,
        }))
    }

    fn matches(self, address: u64) -> bool {
        address & self.address_mask == self.base_match
    }
}

/// MTRR policy decoded from supplied register values; it does not access MSRs.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct MtrrLayout<'a> {
    physical_address_bits: u8,
    enabled: bool,
    fixed_enabled: bool,
    default_type: MtrrMemoryType,
    fixed_ranges: Option<&'a FixedMtrrRanges>,
    variable_ranges: &'a [VariableMtrr],
}

impl<'a> MtrrLayout<'a> {
    pub fn new(
        physical_address_bits: u8,
        enabled: bool,
        fixed_enabled: bool,
        default_type: MtrrMemoryType,
        fixed_ranges: Option<&'a FixedMtrrRanges>,
        variable_ranges: &'a [VariableMtrr],
    ) -> Result<Self, MtrrError> {
        if !(12..=MAX_MTRR_PHYSICAL_ADDRESS_BITS).contains(&physical_address_bits) {
            return Err(MtrrError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
        }
        if enabled && fixed_enabled && fixed_ranges.is_none() {
            return Err(MtrrError::FixedRangesUnavailable);
        }
        if variable_ranges
            .iter()
            .any(|range| range.physical_address_bits != physical_address_bits)
        {
            return Err(MtrrError::PhysicalAddressWidthMismatch);
        }

        Ok(Self {
            physical_address_bits,
            enabled,
            fixed_enabled,
            default_type,
            fixed_ranges,
            variable_ranges,
        })
    }

    /// Resolve the MTRR type for one physical address, rejecting undefined overlaps.
    pub fn memory_type_for(&self, address: u64) -> Result<MtrrMemoryType, MtrrError> {
        if address >= (1u64 << self.physical_address_bits) {
            return Err(MtrrError::PhysicalAddressOutOfRange);
        }
        if !self.enabled {
            return Ok(MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable);
        }
        if self.fixed_enabled {
            if let Some(memory_type) = self
                .fixed_ranges
                .and_then(|ranges| ranges.type_for_address(address))
            {
                return Ok(memory_type);
            }
        }

        let mut matched_types = 0u8;
        for range in self.variable_ranges {
            if range.matches(address) {
                matched_types |= 1 << range.memory_type as u8;
            }
        }
        if matched_types == 0 {
            return Ok(self.default_type);
        }
        if matched_types & (1 << MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable as u8) != 0 {
            return Ok(MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable);
        }
        if matched_types.count_ones() == 1 {
            return MtrrMemoryType::from_raw(matched_types.trailing_zeros() as u8);
        }
        let write_back_and_through =
            (1 << MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack as u8) | (1 << MtrrMemoryType::WriteThrough as u8);
        if matched_types == write_back_and_through {
            return Ok(MtrrMemoryType::WriteThrough);
        }
        Err(MtrrError::UndefinedOverlap)
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum MappingError {
    EmptyRange,
    UnalignedRange,
    AddressOverflow,
    InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth,
    UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth,
    InvalidVirtualAddressWidth,
    PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds,
    NonCanonicalVirtualRange,
    CrossesCanonicalHole,
    WritableExecutable,
    UnalignedPageOffset,
    PageOffsetOutOfRange,
    InvalidPatIndex,
    NxNotEnabled,
}

/// Validate one contiguous range for a future 4 KiB page-mapping operation.
///
/// The physical-address width must be known; passing `None` is rejected rather
/// than treating an unknown bound as unbounded. Successful validation does not
/// establish that physical frames are owned by a caller or mapped in the HHDM.
pub fn validate_mapping_range(
    virtual_start: u64,
    physical_start: u64,
    length: u64,
    permissions: PagePermissions,
    physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    virtual_address_bits: u8,
) -> Result<ValidatedMappingRange, MappingError> {
    if virtual_address_bits == 0 || virtual_address_bits > 64 {
        return Err(MappingError::InvalidVirtualAddressWidth);
    }
    let physical_address_bits =
        physical_address_bits.ok_or(MappingError::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)?;
    if physical_address_bits == 0 || physical_address_bits > 64 {
        return Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth);
    }
    if length == 0 {
        return Err(MappingError::EmptyRange);
    }
    if (virtual_start | physical_start | length) & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
        return Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange);
    }
    if permissions.writable && permissions.executable {
        return Err(MappingError::WritableExecutable);
    }

    let virtual_end = virtual_start
        .checked_add(length)
        .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
    let physical_end = physical_start
        .checked_add(length)
        .ok_or(MappingError::AddressOverflow)?;
    if !physical_range_fits_address_width(physical_start, physical_end, physical_address_bits) {
        return Err(MappingError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds);
    }

    let last_virtual_address = virtual_end - 1;
    if !is_canonical_virtual_address(virtual_start, virtual_address_bits)
        || !is_canonical_virtual_address(last_virtual_address, virtual_address_bits)
    {
        return Err(MappingError::NonCanonicalVirtualRange);
    }
    if virtual_address_bits < 64
        && ((virtual_start ^ last_virtual_address) & (1u64 << (virtual_address_bits - 1))) != 0
    {
        return Err(MappingError::CrossesCanonicalHole);
    }

    Ok(ValidatedMappingRange {
        virtual_start,
        virtual_end,
        physical_start,
        physical_end,
        physical_address_bits,
        permissions,
    })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::{
        parse_cr3_root, validate_mapping_range, Cr3Error, Cr3LowBits, FixedMtrrRanges,
        MappingError, MtrrError, MtrrLayout, MtrrMemoryType, PagePermissions,
        ValidatedMappingRange, VariableMtrr, PAGE_SIZE,
    };

    const KERNEL_READ_ONLY: PagePermissions = PagePermissions {
        writable: false,
        executable: false,
        user_accessible: false,
    };

    fn variable_mtrr(base: u64, size: u64, memory_type: MtrrMemoryType) -> VariableMtrr {
        let physical_address_bits = 36;
        let physical_mask = ((1u64 << physical_address_bits) - 1) & !(PAGE_SIZE - 1);
        let mask = physical_mask & !(size - 1) | (1 << 11);
        VariableMtrr::from_msr_values(base | memory_type as u8 as u64, mask, physical_address_bits)
            .unwrap()
            .unwrap()
    }

    #[test]
    fn decodes_cr3_root_with_pcid_enabled() {
        let root = parse_cr3_root(0x1234_5000 | 0x0abc, Some(48), true).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(root.physical_address(), 0x1234_5000);
        assert_eq!(root.low_bits(), Cr3LowBits::Pcid(0x0abc));
    }

    #[test]
    fn decodes_cr3_cache_controls_without_pcid() {
        let root = parse_cr3_root(0x1234_5000 | (1 << 3) | (1 << 4), Some(48), false).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(root.physical_address(), 0x1234_5000);
        assert_eq!(
            root.low_bits(),
            Cr3LowBits::CacheControls {
                page_level_write_through: true,
                page_level_cache_disable: true,
            }
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_cr3_reserved_bits_and_invalid_address_widths() {
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(0x1000 | 1, Some(48), false),
            Err(Cr3Error::ReservedBits)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(1 << 40, Some(40), true),
            Err(Cr3Error::ReservedBits)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(0x1000, None, false),
            Err(Cr3Error::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            parse_cr3_root(0x1000, Some(53), false),
            Err(Cr3Error::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn resolves_variable_mtrr_ranges_and_default_type() {
        let ranges = [variable_mtrr(
            0x20_0000,
            0x20_0000,
            MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack,
        )];
        let layout =
            MtrrLayout::new(36, true, false, MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable, None, &ranges).unwrap();

        assert_eq!(
            layout.memory_type_for(0x20_0000),
            Ok(MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            layout.memory_type_for(0x3f_ffff),
            Ok(MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            layout.memory_type_for(0x40_0000),
            Ok(MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            layout.memory_type_for(1u64 << 36),
            Err(MtrrError::PhysicalAddressOutOfRange)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn fixed_mtrrs_override_variable_and_default_ranges_below_one_mib() {
        let mut registers = [0; 11];
        registers[2] = MtrrMemoryType::WriteThrough as u64;
        let fixed = FixedMtrrRanges::from_msr_values(registers).unwrap();
        let variable = [variable_mtrr(0, 0x10_0000, MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack)];
        let layout = MtrrLayout::new(
            36,
            true,
            true,
            MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack,
            Some(&fixed),
            &variable,
        )
        .unwrap();

        assert_eq!(
            layout.memory_type_for(0xa_0000),
            Ok(MtrrMemoryType::WriteThrough)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            layout.memory_type_for(0xa_4000),
            Ok(MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            layout.memory_type_for(0x10_0000),
            Ok(MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn resolves_defined_variable_mtrr_overlaps_and_rejects_undefined_ones() {
        let write_back_through = [
            variable_mtrr(0x20_0000, 0x20_0000, MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack),
            variable_mtrr(0x20_0000, 0x20_0000, MtrrMemoryType::WriteThrough),
        ];
        let layout = MtrrLayout::new(
            36,
            true,
            false,
            MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable,
            None,
            &write_back_through,
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            layout.memory_type_for(0x20_0000),
            Ok(MtrrMemoryType::WriteThrough)
        );

        let uncacheable_override = [
            write_back_through[0],
            variable_mtrr(0x20_0000, 0x20_0000, MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable),
        ];
        let layout = MtrrLayout::new(
            36,
            true,
            false,
            MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack,
            None,
            &uncacheable_override,
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            layout.memory_type_for(0x20_0000),
            Ok(MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable)
        );

        let undefined = [
            variable_mtrr(0x20_0000, 0x20_0000, MtrrMemoryType::WriteCombining),
            variable_mtrr(0x20_0000, 0x20_0000, MtrrMemoryType::WriteProtected),
        ];
        let layout =
            MtrrLayout::new(36, true, false, MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack, None, &undefined).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            layout.memory_type_for(0x20_0000),
            Err(MtrrError::UndefinedOverlap)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn disabled_mtrrs_force_uncacheable_and_bad_registers_are_rejected() {
        let layout =
            MtrrLayout::new(36, false, true, MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack, None, &[]).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            layout.memory_type_for(0x20_0000),
            Ok(MtrrMemoryType::Uncacheable)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            MtrrLayout::new(36, true, true, MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack, None, &[]),
            Err(MtrrError::FixedRangesUnavailable)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            VariableMtrr::from_msr_values(0x1000 | 0x200, 0, 36),
            Err(MtrrError::ReservedBaseBits)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            VariableMtrr::from_msr_values(0x1000 | 6, 1 << 10, 36),
            Err(MtrrError::ReservedMaskBits)
        );
        assert_eq!(VariableMtrr::from_msr_values(0x1000 | 6, 0, 36), Ok(None));
        let width_mismatch = [variable_mtrr(0x20_0000, 0x20_0000, MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack)];
        assert_eq!(
            MtrrLayout::new(
                35,
                true,
                false,
                MtrrMemoryType::WriteBack,
                None,
                &width_mismatch,
            ),
            Err(MtrrError::PhysicalAddressWidthMismatch)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            VariableMtrr::from_msr_values(0x1000 | 2, 1 << 11, 36),
            Err(MtrrError::ReservedMemoryType { value: 2 })
        );
        let mut invalid_fixed = [0; 11];
        invalid_fixed[0] = 2;
        assert_eq!(
            FixedMtrrRanges::from_msr_values(invalid_fixed),
            Err(MtrrError::ReservedMemoryType { value: 2 })
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn accepts_aligned_ranges_at_canonical_boundaries() {
        let lower = validate_mapping_range(
            0x0000_7fff_ffff_e000,
            0x1000,
            0x2000,
            KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            Some(14),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            lower,
            ValidatedMappingRange {
                virtual_start: 0x0000_7fff_ffff_e000,
                virtual_end: 0x0000_8000_0000_0000,
                physical_start: 0x1000,
                physical_end: 0x3000,
                physical_address_bits: 14,
                permissions: KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            }
        );

        let upper = validate_mapping_range(
            0xffff_8000_0000_0000,
            0,
            PAGE_SIZE,
            KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
            Some(12),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(upper.virtual_end(), 0xffff_8000_0000_1000);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_empty_and_unaligned_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x2000, 0, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(32), 48),
            Err(MappingError::EmptyRange)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1001, 0x2000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(32), 48),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                0x2000,
                PAGE_SIZE + 1,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(32),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedRange)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn requires_known_valid_address_widths_and_bounded_physical_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, None, 48),
            Err(MappingError::UnknownPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(0), 48),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x1000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(52), 0),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidVirtualAddressWidth)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                0x1000,
                PAGE_SIZE * 2,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(13),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_noncanonical_ranges_and_ranges_crossing_the_canonical_hole() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x0000_8000_0000_0000,
                0,
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(52),
                48
            ),
            Err(MappingError::NonCanonicalVirtualRange)
        );

        let crosses_hole = (0xffff_8000_0000_1000u64) - 0x0000_7fff_ffff_f000;
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x0000_7fff_ffff_f000,
                0,
                crosses_hole,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                48,
            ),
            Err(MappingError::CrossesCanonicalHole)
        );
    }

    fn pat_value(entries: [u8; 8]) -> u64 {
        entries.iter().enumerate().fold(0, |value, (index, entry)| {
            value | ((*entry as u64) << (index * 8))
        })
    }

    #[test]
    fn resolves_cache_policies_from_the_supplied_pat_layout() {
        let pat = pat_value([6, 4, 7, 0, 6, 1, 7, 0]);
        let layout = super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(pat).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteBack), Some(0));
        assert_eq!(layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::Uncacheable), Some(3));
        assert_eq!(
            layout.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteCombining),
            Some(5)
        );

        let without_write_combining =
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(pat_value([6, 4, 7, 0, 6, 4, 7, 0])).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            without_write_combining.index_for(super::CachePolicy::WriteCombining),
            None
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_reserved_pat_entries() {
        assert_eq!(
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(0x08),
            Err(super::PatLayoutError::ReservedBits { index: 0 })
        );
        assert_eq!(
            super::PatLayout::from_msr_value(0b010 << 24),
            Err(super::PatLayoutError::ReservedMemoryType {
                index: 3,
                value: 0b010,
            })
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn encodes_4k_leaf_permissions_nx_and_pat_index_bits() {
        let writable_user_page = PagePermissions {
            writable: true,
            executable: false,
            user_accessible: true,
        };
        let mapping = validate_mapping_range(
            0x4000,
            0x20_0000,
            PAGE_SIZE * 2,
            writable_user_page,
            Some(52),
            48,
        )
        .unwrap();

        let first = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0b101, true).unwrap().bits();
        assert_eq!(
            first,
            0x20_0000 | (1 << 0) | (1 << 1) | (1 << 2) | (1 << 3) | (1 << 7) | (1 << 63)
        );
        let second = mapping
            .leaf_4k_entry(PAGE_SIZE, 0b101, true)
            .unwrap()
            .bits();
        assert_eq!(second & 0x000f_ffff_ffff_f000, 0x20_1000);
        assert_eq!(second & (1 << 63), 1 << 63);

        let all_pat_bits = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0b111, true).unwrap().bits()
            & ((1 << 3) | (1 << 4) | (1 << 7));
        assert_eq!(all_pat_bits, (1 << 3) | (1 << 4) | (1 << 7));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_unencodable_leaf_attributes_and_page_offsets() {
        let mapping =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(52), 48)
                .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 8, true),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPatIndex)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, false),
            Err(MappingError::NxNotEnabled)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(1, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::UnalignedPageOffset)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            mapping.leaf_4k_entry(PAGE_SIZE, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::PageOffsetOutOfRange)
        );

        let too_wide =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, KERNEL_READ_ONLY, Some(53), 48)
                .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            too_wide.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, true),
            Err(MappingError::InvalidPhysicalAddressWidth)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn executable_leaf_does_not_set_nx() {
        let executable = PagePermissions {
            writable: false,
            executable: true,
            user_accessible: false,
        };
        let mapping =
            validate_mapping_range(0x4000, 0x20_0000, PAGE_SIZE, executable, Some(52), 48).unwrap();
        let entry = mapping.leaf_4k_entry(0, 0, false).unwrap().bits();
        assert_eq!(entry, 0x20_0000 | 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_arithmetic_overflow_and_writable_executable_ranges() {
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                u64::MAX - (PAGE_SIZE - 1),
                0,
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                64
            ),
            Err(MappingError::AddressOverflow)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(
                0x1000,
                u64::MAX - (PAGE_SIZE - 1),
                PAGE_SIZE,
                KERNEL_READ_ONLY,
                Some(64),
                64
            ),
            Err(MappingError::AddressOverflow)
        );

        let writable_executable = PagePermissions {
            writable: true,
            executable: true,
            user_accessible: false,
        };
        assert_eq!(
            validate_mapping_range(0x1000, 0x2000, PAGE_SIZE, writable_executable, Some(52), 48),
            Err(MappingError::WritableExecutable)
        );
    }
}

# Memory-management direction

## What exists now

`src/memory.rs` contains an allocation-free `MemoryMapBuilder` that copies Limine ranges into validated half-open physical regions. It rejects empty ranges, `base + length` overflow, overlapping ranges, empty maps, and maps exceeding the fixed 256-region capacity. Known Limine types remain distinct, and unknown type values are preserved. Host-side tests cover range validation/classification, canonical-address boundaries, physical-width bounds, and HHDM range arithmetic. `src/boot.rs` stores the resulting map in an owned boot snapshot and reports usable bytes.

`src/frame.rs` provides a fixed-capacity allocator core with host-side tests. It only manages complete 4 KiB frames inside validated `Usable` regions, reserves the first MiB, and coalesces adjacent free ranges. The ownership contract assumes one allocator instance per physical map: within that instance, each eligible frame is owned either by its free-range table or by a non-copyable `Frame` token while allocated. The public constructor does not enforce unique instances, so callers must not initialize multiple allocators for the same physical map; the current boot path retains one allocator in `BootInfo`. `allocate` returns `None` when no free frame remains. `deallocate` consumes the token only on success; on any error, including free-range-table exhaustion, it returns a `DeallocationError` containing the original token and leaves allocator state unchanged. The range tables each have a fixed `MAX_FRAME_RANGES` capacity, so fragmentation can prevent a frame from being returned until nearby ranges coalesce. No kernel service currently allocates frames for mappings or other work.

`Frame::address()` is a physical address value, not a dereferenceable pointer and not a proof that the frame is mapped. CPU diagnostics now read `CR4.LA57` to report the active linear-address width (48 or 57 bits) and query `CPUID.80000008H` for physical and maximum linear-address widths when available. Boot-protocol pointers are checked against the active width, with host tests for 48- and 57-bit canonical boundaries. These facts do not prove that any physical address is mapped.

`src/paging.rs` adds pure, host-tested 4 KiB mapping groundwork and decodes the inherited CR3 root address plus its low-bit interpretation. The CR3 decoder validates the physical-address width and reserved bits and interprets the low 12 bits according to the active `CR4.PCIDE` state; it does not dereference the root. The mapping-range validator requires a known physical-address width, checked aligned bounds, canonical virtual endpoints in one canonical half, and rejects writable-executable permissions. A validated range can encode the raw bits of one 4 KiB leaf PTE, including supervisor/user and write permissions, NX when the caller confirms EFER.NXE, and a bounded three-bit PAT index. `PatLayout` parses a supplied `IA32_PAT` value and selects WB, UC, or WC indices. `MtrrMemoryType`, `VariableMtrr`, `FixedMtrrRanges`, and `MtrrLayout` parse caller-supplied register values and resolve the MTRR type at one physical address, including fixed-range priority and specified variable-overlap rules; undefined overlaps fail closed. This is a pure model only: `src/arch/x86_64/cpu.rs` does not capture MTRR state, and no code combines PAT with MTRR to establish an effective cache type for a mapping. The CPU reads EFER.NXE only when NX is supported and reads `IA32_PAT` only when PAT is supported; these reads are diagnostic and do not modify CPU state. None of these helpers proves frame ownership or HHDM coverage, inspects table entries, or modifies hardware page tables. No page mapping operation or CR3 transition is implemented.

The requested Limine base revision is 3. Its protocol maps only `Usable`, `BootloaderReclaimable`, `ExecutableAndModules`, and `Framebuffer` memory-map regions into the HHDM; reserved, ACPI, bad, and unknown regions are not in that mapping set. HHDM mappings use at least 4 KiB granularity and may cover page-aligned bytes beyond unaligned region boundaries. On x86_64, inherited HHDM mappings are supervisor RWX, normally write-back cached, with framebuffer mappings write-combining when PAT is available. Therefore an HHDM offset is not a blanket physical-memory access grant, and inherited HHDM permissions are not Rustix's future W^X policy. See the [Limine base revision 3 protocol](https://github.com/limine-bootloader/limine-protocol/blob/trunk/PROTOCOL.md#base-revision-3) and its memory-layout/caching sections.

The boot snapshot now rejects physical map ranges beyond the reported CPUID physical-address width when that CPUID leaf is available. For base revision 3 HHDM-mapped types, it also validates the page-rounded physical span, checked `offset + address` arithmetic, and canonical endpoints in the active linear-address width. If the CPU does not report a physical width, the physical-bound check is skipped and no mapping caller may treat the bound as known. These checks are derived from metadata and Limine's protocol contract; they do not inspect page tables or prove that a non-null loader pointer is readable. Rustix still does not dereference physical memory through the HHDM. Rustix-owned mappings should be non-executable by default; executable kernel text must be explicit. MMIO requires an explicit device range and cache policy, and guard regions must remain unmapped. A page-table transition must preserve the kernel image, active stacks, boot data, descriptor tables, and any loader mappings still needed. Rustix does not reclaim bootloader memory, own or replace the inherited Limine page tables, or provide a heap. Bootstrap GDT/TSS/IDT setup and fatal exception diagnostics live in `src/arch/x86_64/interrupts.rs`; they do not enable maskable IRQs.

## Mapping inventory before a CR3 transition

The current runtime has several mappings that a future page-table transition must preserve. The address sources and remaining gaps are:

- **Kernel ELF image:** `linker.ld` places `.text`, `.rodata`, `.limine_requests`, `.data`, `.bss`, and `.got` in three `PT_LOAD` segments: RX text, read-only rodata, and RW data/BSS. It exports `__kernel_start` and `__kernel_end` around the complete loadable image, plus page-aligned bounds for each segment. `BootInfo` checks that Limine's reported virtual base matches the linker start, validates ordered contiguous segment bounds covering the rounded image, derives corresponding half-open physical ranges from the executable physical base with checked arithmetic, and validates canonical virtual and reported physical address widths. The release ELF and QEMU runtime report both showed the three aligned ranges on 2026-10-21. The RX, R/NX, and RW/NX labels describe the linker segment intent; these are linker/boot metadata, not proof that the inherited page tables map every page or enforce those permissions, nor proof that loader pointers are readable. The memory-map `ExecutableAndModules` entries remain illustrative, not an authoritative kernel-image extent.
- **Active stack and `BootInfo`:** The assembly `_start` shim captures Limine's entry `RSP` before any Rust prologue. Under the SysV entry convention the synthetic return slot occupies 8 bytes, so `RSP + 8` is the exclusive stack top. The protocol guarantees at least 64 KiB (without a Stack Size request); `BootInfo` records that minimum virtual span, translates it through the HHDM, and requires it to fit within a single `BootloaderReclaimable` physical region. The full loader allocation may be larger, and neither its exact bounds nor a guard page are known. Preserve the inherited mapping; do not infer a guard page.
- **Kernel static data:** `RAM_FILESYSTEM`, GDT, IDT, TSS, the ring-0 stack, and double-fault IST stack are static BSS/data in the kernel image. Exception entry also requires the handler code and the TSS/IDT references to remain mapped. Preserve the complete relevant data/BSS pages and stack pages.
- **Limine request data:** request records are in the kernel's writable `.limine_requests` segment. Response objects and the memory-map pointer array are bootloader-reclaimable; `capture_boot_information` copies the values used later, and the shell does not retain those pointers. Keep them mapped until the copy and validation finish; any later reclaim must be explicit rather than reclaiming the whole bootloader-reclaimable class.
- **Inherited page tables:** Limine places its page tables in bootloader-reclaimable memory, but their layout is unspecified. `BootInfo` captures raw CR3 and validates a decoded physical root address plus the low-bit interpretation selected by active `CR4.PCIDE`; this numeric decode does not make the root dereferenceable. Rustix does not inspect root entries or inventory the live mappings. Keep the old root and its backing pages intact while inspecting/building a replacement; do not reclaim them until a verified switch no longer depends on them.
- **Devices:** COM1 uses x86 port I/O and needs no MMIO virtual mapping. Rustix does not currently request or use a framebuffer. Any future framebuffer or device-register mapping needs an explicit physical range and cache policy.

## Initial kernel-owned mapping policy (design only)

A first Rustix-owned x86_64 root should use 4 KiB pages only and require NX support; if NX is unavailable, abort before switching. Preserve ELF permissions exactly: text RX, rodata R/NX, and `.limine_requests`/data/BSS/GOT RW/NX. Reject W+X mappings. Exclude the complete physical kernel-image span from the generic HHDM in the first implementation so no writable direct-map alias can undermine text or rodata permissions. Ordinary RAM HHDM mappings should be RW/NX and WB.

Preserve the inherited PAT/MTRR configuration during the first transition. The current boot report reads the active `IA32_PAT` value when PAT is supported and parses WB/UC/WC entries, but does not yet inspect MTRRs or establish effective cache types for physical ranges. Under Limine base revision 3 on x86_64, use PAT index 0 for WB, index 3 for UC, and index 5 for WC only when the active configuration is verified. Map MMIO only from explicit device ranges and use UC by default; do not infer MMIO from generic reserved memory-map entries. A framebuffer may use WC only after its response and cache policy are explicitly validated. COM1 uses port I/O and requires no MMIO mapping.

Do not use huge pages in the first root; add them only when physical coverage, permissions, and cache attributes are uniform across the entire huge-page extent. Future Rustix-owned downward-growing stacks should have at least one 4 KiB unmapped guard page below them. The Limine bootstrap stack has no known guard, so none may be assumed there.

Never modify Limine's active page tables in place. Build a separate candidate root from owned frames, leave the inherited root and its backing memory intact, and do not switch CR3 until every transition-critical mapping has been preserved and checked. Any pre-switch failure must discard the candidate root and return its frames without changing the active root. This policy is not yet implemented; Rustix does not inspect the inherited root or replace CR3.

## Planned memory layers

1. **Validated physical regions:** implemented as a fixed-capacity owned snapshot; allocator ownership and reclaim policy remain future work.
2. **Frame allocator integration:** the bounded core is initialized from `BootInfo`; add real allocation callers only after the page-mapping policy and ownership rules are implemented.
3. **Kernel virtual memory:** Rustix still runs on inherited Limine page tables. Before replacing them, preserve the executing kernel, bootstrap/Ring-0 stacks, request/response data, and exception stacks; define the HHDM extent and explicit mapping permissions/cache attributes. Keep writable-executable mappings disallowed by default and reserve MMIO and guard regions.
4. **Heap:** add a global allocator only after frame allocation and page mapping are initialized. Define allocation failure behavior for kernel and interrupt contexts.
5. **Process address spaces:** isolate userspace; define copy-in/copy-out helpers and never treat user pointers as kernel references.

## Invariants to preserve

- Physical ranges use checked arithmetic and the half-open convention `[start, end)`.
- Only complete frames in validated `Usable` regions enter the allocator; the first MiB remains reserved. Firmware and loader-reclaimable regions are not silently promoted to free memory.
- Under the single-allocator-per-physical-map contract, a frame is represented by one allocator-free range or one caller-owned, non-copyable `Frame` token. Only a successful `deallocate` transfers the token back to the allocator; errors return the token and leave allocator ranges and accounting unchanged. The current public constructor relies on callers to enforce unique allocator instances.
- `allocate` returns `None` on frame exhaustion. Metadata-capacity exhaustion is an explicit error, never silent loss of a frame.
- A `Frame` address is physical, not directly dereferenceable. No HHDM translation, page-table mutation, or CR3 switch is permitted until address widths, mapping coverage, permissions/cache attributes, and transition-preservation requirements are established.
- Mapping changes validate alignment, canonical addresses, ownership, and permissions before touching tables.
- User-controlled addresses are validated on every syscall boundary; prior validation is not a substitute for access checks during later copies.
- Tests cover unaligned edges, exact-boundary allocations, arithmetic overflow, overlaps, exhaustion, and unchanged state/ownership on failure.

These are design requirements for future milestones, not functionality delivered by the current boot summary.
